## Chapter 1: To Untangle Me From You

### Notes

#### Work Notes

I've been so excited and inspired to write this for the ATLA Winter Solstice gift exchange! lesmiserablol asked for something that included friends to lovers, getting together, mutual pining, roommates, miscommunication, happy endings, and fluff, and I went—why not all of those at once! (And how not spit out a multi-chapter fic instead of a oneshot while I'm at it lol)

&#45;-This got out of control length-wise (what a shock) but it’s all written and just needs editing --I’ll post updates on Fridays  
&#45;-There will be smut in this and I’ll bump up the rating when it’s added, but it should be easy to skip if that isn’t your thing (I’ll post the lines you can jump to when we come to it) --I realized partway through writing that I fudged all the age gaps between the Gaang relative to when they were in school together, so…don’t think too hard about it, because clearly I didn’t lol

### Text

***Three days and twenty-one hours until the end***

“You know,” Aang says, scratching at his beanie with his screwdriver as he glances around, “This might be easier if we had more room.”

“It’s fine,” Sokka says as if he isn’t trying to shove his nightstand aside to give his shoulders space alongside his bedframe and feeling it run up against his pile of different satchels, laptop bags, backpacks, totes, and gym bags that he really did mean to buy some kind of basket or something for and never quite got around to.

“Maybe we should pack some boxes first,” Aang muses as if one just *packs boxes*. As if the very idea of it doesn’t have Sokka’s shoulders hunching up to his ears, his body bracing for a blow.

“We’re fine, we already started taking this apart,” Sokka says, fully aware that he is both digging in his heels and that Aang isn’t exactly wrong. But the idea of yet another half-finished moving task floating in his vision, an impossible-to-ignore reminder…no. Just no.

Though will a *finished* moving task really be any easier to ignore?

Fuck.

Aang is shrugging, though, and brandishing his screwdriver with all his usual enthusiasm. “What’s the order, again? Slats then undo the connectors? Or do we need the slats so there isn’t too much pressure on the joints?”

All completely reasonable questions, and the act of thinking about how to disassemble his bedframe shouldn’t have Sokka squeezing his eyes shut and gripping his own screwdriver hard enough to make his hand ache.

“Because when I try to pull this side out, it just seems to shove that other part in.”

“That’s how it stays together,” Sokka says, trying for levity and hearing it come out clipped instead. Hopefully, Aang will chalk it up to him trying to wedge himself into this space…

Aang doesn’t respond except to continue fiddling around with his screwdriver, though, and Sokka’s shoulders are just starting to loosen in the silence when Aang suddenly asks, “Do we even *need* to take it apart? Don’t you usually just move your bed all in one piece?”

“It needs to fit in the storage unit,” Sokka says, tight. The storage unit Sokka didn’t measure out, the one he didn’t plan around, the one he didn’t think about because he didn’t want to and only realized last night was going to be too small and that there were no others left to be had.

“Right,” Aang says, nodding, “So it has to come apart.” A moment more of untwisting screws, then, diffident, “And you know how to do that? Because,” he continues when Sokka doesn’t reply because he isn’t confident he can trust the tone of whatever is about to come out, “I bet we could find the instructions online.”

“It’s fine,” Sokka grits out, trying to wiggle his Allen wrench into the barely-in-existence space between the slats again and hearing a voice in the back of his head that sounds suspiciously like Zuko’s whispering that this is why you keep the instructions.

“This is still the one you got from Ikea, right? Back in college.”

“Somehow,” Sokka grunts, trying to shove away the memory.

“I bet they log all their instructions, even the old ones, and—”

“It’s *fine.*”

“Because me and Katara have found—”

“I don’t need fucking in*structions*,” Sokka grunts, trying to get some weight behind this *stupid* Allen wrench but of *course* the space is too tight for his hex and *finally,* finally feels the overly tight, overly hidden bolt come loose, his flash of triumph quickly guttering against his awareness of Aang’s sudden silence.

“Sokka,” Aang starts, slow, but Sokka is already taking a deep breath and shoving it down, rallying a smile and a self-deprecating eye roll as easily as if he were fourteen again.

“It’s a challenge, right?” Sokka says, giving the bedframe in question a rueful look before brandishing his wrench. “But we got this. We don’t need Ikea to tell us what to do.”

Aang eyes him a moment longer before shrugging. “I thought you liked when Ikea told you what to do,” he teases as he slides to the other end of the bedframe with his own wrench. “All those maps.”

***Nine years, seven months, and thirty days until the end***

*“*Heathen*,” Sokka accuses as he drags their unwieldy crat of flat packed furniture to* *the side to give a harried-looking woman room to pass between the towering shelves,* *“You put your feet on the* coffee table? *Where your* food *goes?”*

*“Wait,* food—"

*“What’s next, standing on the counters?”*

*Zuko coughs a little and goes suspiciously silent, and suspiciously engrossed in* *pressing himself flat to avoid getting clipped by a cart full of teetering boxes that really* *isn’t anywhere near him, which…*

*“…What,” Sokka frowns, “To clean the tops of the cabinets?”*

*“Well how else do you get up there?” Zuko huffs, exasperated. “We wipe down the* *counters anyway!”*

*“Okay, wow. So adding a step stool to the list, noted.”*

*“Great,” Zuko says, rolling his eyes, “Go find one and you can use* that *as your* *ottoman.”*

*“Boo,” Sokka jeers, ignoring the startled look it gets him from the couple passing by.* *“If you’d just come with me and* seen *it—”*

*“Well, I didn’t.”*

*“Well, you would have it you’d followed the map instead of trying to find the desks on* *your own!”*

*“A map,” Zuko scoffs. “A furniture store doesn’t need a* map.*”*

*“They* give it to you.” *Sokka loves it.* *“And if you’d seen it, you’d know how much we* *need it! Padded top and the fabric was so soft and I really* *think* *it’ll bring the room* *together.”*

*“Sokka,” Zuko says, shaking his head and giving the box in question a shove with his* *foot. “It’s nearly the size of my* bed*.”*

*“Oh, you mean your* crib*.”*

*“It’s not a crib!” Zuko protests, head coming up so fast the hair is actually briefly out* *of both his eyes. “It’s a—” he looks at the tag. “Hemnes daybed! For people! Adult* *people!”*

*“You need a* nightbed*,” Sokka scoffs. “Zuko, it’s got sides*. *All it need is a guardrail so* *you don’t fall off in the night.”*

*“Maybe I like to be cozy.”*

*“Maybe you punched Marcus in the face on reflex last year when they tried to cuddle* *with you.”*

*They both pause as Aang darts over with a fake plant in a geometric pot to hold up for* *their judgment. A moment of silence as they look, then Zuko shrugs, nodding.*

*“Add it to the cart,” he says. “Bring some life to the place.”*

*“You know that’s fake, right?” Sokka checks as Aang darts away again.*

*Zuko gives him a sideways look. “You think we can keep a real one alive? And* *maybe,” Zuko adds, rounding back on Sokka, “I need* storage*.”*

*Sokka snorts. “Maybe you can loft your bedframe and shove boxes underneath like the* *rest of us.”*

*“Maybe I’m trying to be classy.”*

*“Yeah, classy in your crib. You can host your own episode of MTV Cribs,” Sokka says,* *delighted. “Shove a minifridge in there, show off your collection of hair clips you* *don’t admit you have.”*

*Zuko glares. Which would be more effective if he had a hair clip to keep his bangs out* *of his face.*

*“Come on,” Sokka says,. “You really think you’re going to get laid in that thing?”*

*Zuko glares harder, his unscarred cheek pinking, lips going flat and white that way* *they do when he’s fighting against a smile.*

*“You’re telling me,” Sokka says, dropping his voice and leaning in, giving his* *eyebrows a little waggle just to see Zuko roll his eyes. “That you’re going to be out on* *campus, maybe see some fine guy or girl at a party, go through all the work to chat* *them up.” Zuko snorts, as if Sokka hasn’t seen him reeling them in before. “Being all* *suave or getting a date, and you’re going to bring them back to your place and invite* *them up. Put on the candles—”*

*“How detailed is this little fantasy of yours?” Zuko asks, dry.*

*“—have the playlist ready, dim the lights,* really *make it all romantic. And then you’re* *going to try to put the moves on them in a* crib*?” Sokka laughs, shaking his head.* *“They’re going to think you’ve got some* weird *stuff going on, hotman.”*

*“And what are you doing?” Zuko huffs, scraping Sokka with a look. “The cheapest,* *barest raw wood bedframe you can get splinters off of every time you walk into an* *unfinished edge? Good luck not sending all* your *dates home bleeding.”*

*“As if I wouldn’t* sand *it.”*

*“Oh, yeah, just what the start of our sophomore year needs, you in front of our new* *apartment building on move in day* sanding a bedframe *talking to everyone who walks* *by about* durable finishes*.”*

*Sokka snaps his mouth shut on his ready monolog about the best wood stains and* *lacquers for indoor, everyday furniture just as Aang gently but firmly shoves a* *watering can between them, Sokka blinking at the copper shine of it as Zuko makes a* *considering noise.*

*“I want it, let’s do it,” Zuko says, decisive, all the encouragement Aang needs—and* *where is he getting this stuff from so fast, anyway? Did he fill up his own secondary* *cart somewhere?*

*Sokka snorts. “What, for our fake flowers?”*

*Zuko gives him a sideways look, lips quirking. “We gotta sell it, right?”*

*And they’ll need all the help they can get. “A bedframe is just a vehicle for the* *mattress, anyway,” Sokka sniffs. “*That’s *where you want to be spending your money.”*

*“Your mattress where you put the moves on people.”*

*“You know it, hot stuff.”*

*“Where you put the moves on yourself more like.”*

*“We have a good time,” Sokka grins, enjoying the way it makes Zuko’s blush again* *even though he was the one who brought it up. “Besides, space is at a premium in* *those bedrooms. This crib is huge and its basically just a twin bed! Why do you even* *need—”*

*“A headboard?” Zuko finishes, dry, as Aang comes back with a stack of shower* *curtain options.*

*“No brown,” Sokka says immediately. “Brown in a bathroom, bad look.”*

*“The constellations are nice but there’s going to be mildew all over that in a heartbeat* *and we won’t be able to tell,” Zuko frowns, head cocked. “I like the other one* *though.”*

*“It’s nice,” Sokka agrees, taking in the simple pattern of red lobsters against a white* *background. “It’s a* reference.”

*Zuko shrugs. “It’s red.”*

*“I’m just saying,” Sokka says, turning back to Zuko as Aang darts away again, “You* *need to think strategically about the space. It’s not piece by piece, it’s about the* *overall* experience*.”*

*“The overall experience of your bedroom.”*

*“Exactly!”*

*“Maybe I want my experience to say, doesn’t put all my shit on the floor.”*

*“One, it’s on a chair,” Sokka says, ticking off a finger. “And two—your experience* *right now is going to say, ‘tried to recreate the bedroom I’ve had since I was a* *toddler, but they were out of racecar beds.”*

*Aang dangles a* luxurious *looking bathmat between them. “Oh* hell *yeah,” Zuko says* *over top of Sokka’s, “Now that’s what I’m talking about!”*

*Aang looks pleased, hesitating instead of darting away again and turning his head* *sideways to take in the picture printed on the side of the box. “…Is that a giant crib?”*

*“Ha!”*

*“Fuck,” Zuko mutters.*

*“You guys have some news?” Aang says, not even trying to fight a smile. “Are you* *adopting a giant baby?”*

*“Zuko is actually going to spend his year looking for a—”*

*“Fine, fine! I’ll switch beds—"*

*“Ottoman space in the car!” Sokka cheers, pumping his fist.*

*“*—If.” *Zuko pauses for emphasis. “I get to pick the ottoman.”*

*“Deal,” Sokka says almost before Zuko is even done saying the words*.

*“And you’re sanding and staining my bed, too,” Zuko adds, eyes narrowing.*

*“Easy peasy,” Sokka says, with all the confidence of having painted Katara’s dresser* *exactly once as a gift for her twelfth birthday, but really, how hard can it be? There* *are plenty of videos out there. “This is going to look great,” Sokka assures him, “That* *bed is going to be the star of the show.”*

*“I thought it was about the holistic experience,” Zuko says, dry, right as Aang comes* *back with what is* clearly *a stuffed animal masquerading as a pillow. Sokka stares at it* *for a long moment, trying to envision having it on his couch, in the middle of his living* *room, where all his friends and potential more-than-friends will—*

*“Yes,” Zuko says, decisive, stalking off back toward the showroom before Sokka or* *Aang can say anything about it.*

Sokka jolts back to himself at the low crack of about-to-split wood, blinking as he realizes that he’s been tightening this screw instead of loosening it, over and over nearly past the point the wood can handle and fuck, splitting a support post of his bedframe after ten—nearly, nearly, not quite— fucking years would be just what he needs right now.

Part of Sokka wants to just hurl his wrench away and scream, but that’s not what well-adjusted, calm people do in the middle of a move, so instead he forces himself to inhale, then again, to take a steadying breath and then actually focus on what he’s doing instead of on how much he doesn’t want to be doing it.

If he can just make himself concentrate on how the wood joins together instead of on his memories of sanding it alongside Zuko, on the challenge of taking his bedframe apart into a nice storable shape instead of any other thought beating against the edges of his attention—if he can just do that, can just see it as a puzzle and absolutely nothing else, then eventually it’s easy enough to reverse how to take it apart. To break it down into pegs and bolts and slats and screws and support pieces instead of memories until Sokka is sitting in a neat pile of wooden planks on one side of him and his mattress leaning against the wall on the other and he realizes that he should have taken apart his bed last, not first, and suddenly it’s all he can do not to cry.

Fuck. *Fuck*. What a stupid, dumb, rookie mistake. After six moves maybe he’d fucking know how to go about it. But then, Sokka is aware that he normally schedules the packing down to the hour and maps out the strategy for loading and unloading the truck down to the wire bedroom trashcan Zuko always forgets to account for. And this time…well, this time Sokka procrastinated like he was trying to win a trophy, and Zuko, who normally keeps Sokka from putting things off, was acting like he was aiming for his own medal, too, and now here they are: no schedule, no plan, and four days to pack and move out an entire apartment because the one thing Zuko *did* manage to do was schedule the U-Haul for pickup at seven in the fucking morning on April 1st like the cruelest April Fool’s joke known to man.

“Sokka,” Aang says, Sokka jerking his focus away from his mattress and onto Aang who is searching Sokka’s face like he might have said Sokka’s name more than once. “Are you okay?”

“Yeah,” Sokka says, his voice coming out unexpectedly raspy. He clears his throat, reaching for something else to say even as his attention drifts back to his bedframe, his eyes seeking all the pockmarks and dents and scratches from nearly ten years of daily use and six moves and that time with the handcuffs where it turned out Alicia really *wasn’t* expecting him to agree to the idea and she fumbled the keys down the floor vent and panicked and hid in the bathroom and wouldn’t come out because she was so embarrassed, and Sokka had to call for Zuko to come help him and once Zuko stopped laughing it turned out he was as handy with picking locks as he ever was.

That relationship was short-lived. But Sokka still has the pair of safe-release handcuffs Zuko gave him for his next birthday in front of Katara and Mai and Ty Lee and *everyone*, his face solemn and earnest and eyes gleaming with wicked laughter.

And there on the wood plank that formed the side of the bed, the stain near the headboard from when Sokka was sick and Zuko tried to bring him soup in bed and tripped over the mess on the floor and soup went all over the bedframe, and even feeling like total crap Sokka couldn’t help but laugh and feel touched and—

“I’m all good,” Sokka hears himself say, forcefully cutting off the flow of memories before he really does just curl up and cry and making himself muster up a spark of humor. “I’m just realizing I’m about to be in for some camping,” he says, rueful, waving a hand toward the mattress. “Just like the old days, right?” he adds, giving Aang a nudge.

“A bit more comfortable, maybe,” Aang laughs, and Sokka pretends not to see how naked the flash of relief over Aang’s face had been. “I don’t remember hauling mattresses around.”

“I don’t know,” Sokka muses, “Appa is practically the size of a mattress, and you definitely fell asleep on him more than once.”

“Who wouldn’t?” Aang grins, “But at least there will be fewer mishaps with fire with Sifu Homan around now.”

“Yeah. Maybe.” Sokka feels like his chest wants to hollow out around that ‘now.’ “Depends on if you and Zuko have the same definition of a mishap,” he smiles, still with no idea what to do with the sense that for the first time in years he doesn’t actually know what might be going on in Zuko’s head to be able to say for sure.

&#45;-

***Three days and nineteen hours until the end***

Eventually Sokka and Aang—Sokka, really, Aang is fine—manage to get into a groove post-Bedframe Incident, focusing on organizing the—already organized, thank you very much—chaos of his room into something that makes sense for packing. They’re just figuring out what Sokka definitely won’t be storing that Aang can take now when Sokka pauses, turning away from a small mountain of tank tops and toward the barely-there pad of feet down the hallway almost before he registers the sudden absence of Zuko’s ever-present podcast floating from his room.

“Hey,” Zuko says, the red of the tattoo on the hand he wraps around the doorframe almost perfectly matching the red of his t-shirt, which makes it look like his scar is melting down his entire body in ways Sokka always finds fascinating. “Do you—Aang!”

“Zuko!” Aang calls back, and Sokka can feel himself genuinely smiling for what feels like the first time all day as they greet each other like they weren’t all in the same group text talking about when Aang would come by today.

“How are things?” Zuko asks, absently scraping his hair back into a half-bun as he leans against the doorframe. “How was the appointment last week?”

“Great!” Aang says, somehow managing to convey a sense of bouncing on his toes without ever leaving the floor. “The doctor said Katara was doing really well, and the baby, too. Want to see the ultrasounds?” he asks, already reaching for his phone.

“Always,” Zuko says, weaving around barely filled boxes to crouch down at Aang’s side. Sokka quickly scrambles out of his prison of clothes to slide over to Aang’s other shoulder, leaning in to see the screen along with Zuko and inhaling the faint smell of Zuko’s deodorant, eyes catching on the way wisps of hair brush against Zuko’s cheek and lingering over a streak of dirt along the edge of his jaw.

“See?” Aang is saying, grabbing back Sokka’s attention, his face lit with happiness as he flips through pictures. “They’re sucking their thumb! Isn’t that so cool? And we finally got a good view of the face, here, let me find it…”

***Four months and fourteen days before the end***

*“That’s a sonogram of* your *baby,” Sokka says, still marveling over the little photo* *and doing everything in his power not to point out that the colors make it all look like* *burnt cheese.*

*“Sokka, you’ve* known *I was pregnant,” Katara laughs.*

*“Yeah, but here’s* evidence*.”*

*“I assure you,” she says, dry, “There’s been plenty before this.”*

*“This is a* picture*, it’s like it’s* real*.” Like he can finally conceptualize it as a real,* *actual thing, something more than the abstract concept of a baby. A niece or nephew,* *made visible and currently the size of an apricot.*

*Not that Sokka knows how big an apricot is.*

*“Trust me, the morning sickness has been* very *real,” Katara says, flicking him on the* *shoulder as she gets up to grab the kettle off the stove.*

*“Are you okay?” Sokka says, head snapping up. “I was doing some research, and—”*

*“—and ginger helps and you need to get plenty of fluids,” Katara choruses along with* *him. “Yes. I know. You texted me that article. And emailed it. And printed out a copy.”*

*Sokka shrugs, pulling sheepishly at his dangling earring. She might be the medical* *professional—and fine, that might have been a* bit *of overkill on his part—but Sokka* *doesn’t know if he’ll ever lose the instinct to try to make the hard things better for her.*

*“Enough about that, though,” Katara says, grabbing the picture out of Sokka’s hand* *—*

*“Hey!”*

*—and replacing it with a mug of tea. Ginger, by the smell. Sokka still gives her a* *mock-annoyed look just on principle.*

*“You can look at it later, I’m not just a baby factory here,” she says, sticking out her* *tongue as she settles across from him again with her own tea. “You have news too! I* *saw the posts, you and Zuko were out celebrating?”*

*“We were,” Sokka says, straightening with excitement. “It’s official—he’s going to* *law school!”*

*“He got his acceptance then?” Katara grins, even though she must have known if she* *saw them out celebrating. “That’s awesome! I kept telling him to just give it a few* *more days to hear back, I knew he could do it.”*

*“Right?” Sokka shakes his head in fond exasperation. Zuko is one of the most* *competent people Sokka knows and somehow still never seems to realize it. “I’ve been* *telling him that all month. And get this,” Sokka says, pride bursting in his chest, “It’s* *his top choice* and *a full ride!”*

*“Oh wow,” Katara says, looking suitably impressed as she blows on her mug. “Iroh* *must be telling every single person who walks in the door about it.”*

*“Incessantly,” Sokka grins, fully understanding the impulse.*

*“Good for him,” she murmurs as she takes a sip of her tea, eyes thoughtful on his* *face. “So, it’s really happening then, huh.”*

*“Yeah!”*

*“I didn’t think it ever would,” she admits and Sokka blinks, thrown.*

*“Law school?” Katara helped him hype Zuko up to write all his essays, she’s been* *encouraging him nearly as much as Sokka right from the start. Why would she—*

*“You guys moving out,” Katara clarifies and Sokka stills as everything in him* *suddenly grinds to a stop, as her words suddenly become muffled, distant. “You guys* *have lived together what, eight years now?” she asks, the question barely penetrating* *the sudden buzzing in his ears.*

*“Nine, four months ago.” The words are automatic, somehow falling off numb lips* *without his conscious thought. Nine years and four months and they’d joked about* *what to do for their ten-year anniversary, but—but if—*

*“Do you even remember how to live on your own anymore?” Katara is teasing, as if* *this is a time for teasing. As if Sokka isn’t feeling her words slice through skin and* *sinew and muscle all the way to bone, as if all the enthusiasm and pride in his gut isn’t* *abruptly guttering into something hard and cold. “You did it for what, one whole* *semester of college and decided you didn’t like it?”*

*“I didn’t…” Sokka says faintly, not sure what he’s trying to say—that he didn’t even* *live alone, actually, Brent just functionally moved in with his boyfriend the second* *week of the semester? That he didn’t expect that he’d ever have to be alone? That he* *somehow didn’t realize that Zuko going to law school six hours away would mean that* *Zuko wouldn’t be* here *anymore, and fuck, how could Sokka* not *realize? How could he* *help Zuko sort through his options and pick the best schools and encourage him to* *apply and not once ever realize that it would mean Zuko was going to* leave?

*“When’s school start?” Katara asks, Sokka hearing it like he’s underwater and* *suddenly unsure which direction to swim for the surface. “August? You’ve got time to* *figure it out.”*

*Sokka clutches at his mug, trying to ground himself on the warmth and solidity of it* *and feeling his hands trying to shake. “Our lease is up at the end of March.”*

*Katara pauses, eyes flicking over his face before she shrugs. “Four months to* *remember how to fold your own laundry then.”*

*“Zuko doesn’t fold* all *my laundry,” Sokka protests, the defense automatic as the* *larger part of his brain points out that no, Zuko just does it most of the time, with a* *neatness and precision that Sokka has done plenty of wondering over but never once* *figured out how to replicate.*

“Honestly,” Zuko says, the familiar rasp of his voice tugging Sokka back into the present. “It’s unsettling how detailed it is.”

“I know, right?” Aang enthuses. “Like, those are cheeks! Those are fingernails!”

“I can’t believe you guys have managed to not know if it’s a boy or girl yet when you can practically count the eyelashes,” Sokka says, hauling himself back into the conversation.

“We want to be surprised,” Aang shrugs, and Zuko huffs a little laugh.

“I’m pretty sure whatever happens is going to be a surprise,” he says with all the world-weary amusement of someone who once had to watch his boss’ children at a company holiday party and came home with juice on his favorite shirt and marker on his pants and a vow to never do it again.

“Definitely,” Aang grins. “Like, my fatherhood app was telling me that at this stage…” Aang rattles off the day’s fact about burping or changing diapers or cracking nipples—that one had been horrifying—or whatever the latest is, but Sokka finds himself struggling to hold onto the words again. He’s more listening to the familiar rhythm of Aang’s voice and Zuko’s replies than taking in the words, staring at the two of them. The way Zuko’s lips are curled into a smile, his gaze steady with interest, his body relaxed in its crouch, Sokka feeling his heart pounding in his chest hard enough to hurt.

He isn’t usually prone to heartburn, but this has apparently been the month for it, and fuck, it better not be sticking around…

“I’m glad, man,” Zuko is saying, clapping Aang on the shoulder in one of those easy little gestures that took so long for him to do naturally. “I’ll have to swing by before I hit the road.”

“Oh, don’t worry about it,” Aang immediately demurs. “You guys have a ton still to do.”

“Well, I already have the plans in place to be back mid-June,” Zuko says, firm. “My classes won’t be started yet, so I can be there to—”

“Don’t worry about it,” Aang repeats, laughing. “There’s plenty of time!”

Which is true in its own way, two and a half whole months until the little sucker arrives, so Sokka isn’t quite sure why hearing Aang say hits him in the gut quite so hard.

Must be that indigestion.

&#45;-

***Three days and eighteen hours to the end***

Sokka is back to despairing in his piles of clothes—he swears there was an organization system when he started dumping them onto the floor, but he’s lost track of it all somehow, his mind scattered, concentration shot, thoughts looping and looping and looping and—when he hears the apartment door open and the jingle of keys. Zuko barely makes any noise most days unless he wants to, so Sokka immediately perks up, grinning as Zuko pops his head back into the room.

“Quite the throne you’ve built for yourself,” Zuko observes, raising his eyebrow, and Sokka huffs and throws a t-shirt half-heartedly in his direction, both of them watching as it flops limply to the floor. “You could sleep down there if you wanted to,” Zuko adds, the ghost of a smirk on his face now that he knows Sokka is surrounded by useless ammunition.

“Yeah, yeah, laugh it up,” Sokka says, narrowing his eyes. If he could convince Zuko to come a little closer, he’d be in prime flannel-whipping range…

“I will,” Zuko assures him, mock-serious. “With great enthusiasm. You can let the sound of it lull you to sleep.” As if Sokka’s ever been able to push Zuko’s laughter to the back of his attention enough to sleep through it.

“A laugh track for my dreams,” Sokka rolls his eyes, a hint of satisfaction sparking in his chest at the way Zuko’s eyes crinkle in amusement.

“The laugh track *of* your dreams, you mean,” Zuko says, arch, tossing his hair in a picture-perfect Azula impression before relaxing against the doorframe again. “I forgot to ask before with all the pictures,” he says, the teasing still lingering in his voice. “Do you have anything to deal with an awkward nail?”

“A nail, a screw, or a bolt?” Sokka asks, because one time of making the mistake of not clarifying was more than enough. He still mourns losing that security deposit.

Zuko gives him a patient look. “Ask me like I’m five.”

Challenge accepted. “Did you put it together with a hammer—” Sokka mimes holding a nail and banging it in. “—a screwdriver—” Sokka spins in an imaginary screw. “—or an Alen wrench.” He sketches out the shape of it before giving his imaginary tool a few cranks.

Zuko is giving him a flat look, but his lips are twitching. “Alen wrench.”

“In the toolbox, bottom tray on the left,” Sokka says, feeling a burst of fond exasperation. “There’s a whole set.” A whole set that’s been in that same spot for the past nine years, even if the set itself has been upgraded over the years—Zuko being the one to gift him with the latest iteration.

“Thanks, dude,” Zuko says, shoving off the doorframe.

“Any time, hot stuff,” Sokka says, glancing up again when Zuko pauses in the doorway instead of disappearing back into his room and catching the lingering look Zuko is giving down the broken-down bed frame and the mattress against the wall.

“Efficient, aren’t you,” Zuko offers after a beat, giving Sokka a sideways, questioning look.

“Oh, you know,” Sokka says, cringing a little inside all over again at his own stupidity but finding that a smile comes easily enough when it can be a joke with Zuko, “The floor can be a vehicle for a mattress, too.”

Zuko’s lips quirk, recognition and laughter lighting his face before he disappears back into the hallway, the sight and sound of it sparking an answering warmth in Sokka’s gut that isn’t quite enough to cover the way it also presses against a thread of hurt that Sokka doesn’t know what to do with. Or enough to distract from the way his hands are shaking as he reaches for the next box to fill, as if he has any idea how to go about packing away what feels like his entire life.

&#45;-

***Three days and sixteen hours until the end***

“Hey, hotman,” Sokka calls as he shoulders his way into the apartment, his phone in one hand as he flicks through work emails and the other loaded down with takeout. “They ran out of that sauce you like but they had—” He pauses as Zuko lets out a loud, startled exclamation completely at odds with the degree of annoyance of having to settle for the second-best dipping sauce. “Zuko?”

No answer, and Sokka quickly dumps the bags and his phone, socks slipping over the wood floors as he hurries toward the back of the apartment as Zuko lets out another noise.

“Zuko?” he asks again, poking his head into Zuko’s half-open bedroom door in time to catch a blinding grin, so bright and shining with surprise and excitement that it leaves Sokka momentarily gasping—the quick shift from worry to relief, he supposes, coming into the room as Zuko beckons him forward.

“*Look* what I finally found!” Zuko crows, shoving a box across the rug, and it takes Sokka a moment but then he realizes—

***Six years, six months, and twenty-six days until the end***

*“Okay, and see here?” Zuko is saying, voice pitched to reach Sokka beneath the sound* *of Suki and Toph screaming down the beach. “You just want to light the end and take* *a few steps back—*don’t *run, there’s no need to run, there’s going to be plenty of time* *—”*

*“The fun box, they said,” Sokka mutters, grimly committing Zuko’s instructions to* *memory and highly conscious of the sand beneath their knees. Sand’s never been an* *ideal surface for moving quickly, in his experience.*

*“—and if you’re worried, we can even cut the fuse longer—”*

*“What might that be, I asked myself?” Sokka continues, eyes trained on Zuko’s* *fingers, calloused and speckled with scars and deft as they position the firework and* *arrange the fuse and demonstrate where Sokka should light it as if ‘at the end’ needs* *further explanation. “A box of party games?”*

*“—But this stuff is totally safe as long as you aren’t, like, holding it when it goes off.”*

*A loud whoop from Toph as she sets off her own firework, the sudden explosiveness of* *it making Sokka jump and then flush when Zuko absently gives his arm a soothing pat.*

*“The big stuff we would need to worry about isn’t any fun when it’s still this light out,* *anyway,” Zuko adds, shrugging, and if this* isn’t *the big stuff already—*

*“But no, not party games I was told,” Sokka continues mumbling as he pulls himself* *into a far more ready crouch than is probably warranted. “And surely it’s nothing like* Suki’s *fun box.”*

*Zuko snorts out a laugh, the only evidence that he’s listening.*

*“It’ll be great, they said,” Sokka continues as Mai and Ty Lee scream with laughter* *behind them, Suki cackling along with them, the sound interspersed with the burst-whoosh of roman candles.*

*“Are you ready?” Zuko asks, smoothly rolling from kneeling to crouching beside* *Sokka.*

*“It’s the fun box, they said.” Sokka winces as he hears Katara suggest a ‘let’s see* *who’s is bigger’ competition.*

*“*Louder,” *Toph snorts, “It’s all about* louder*, not bigger.”*

*“Don’t forget your safety,” Zuko teases as he gently pushes the safety goggles that* *Sokka had in his trunk from an old TAing gig down from his head onto his eyes. Sokka* *had determinedly handed them out to everyone the minute he realizes what the fun box* *actually entailed.*

*He’s pretty sure Suki is using her pair as a headband.*

*“Here’s my box of explosives, you said.”*

*“*Fireworks,” *Zuko laughs, glancing behind them to check on the others, eyes alert and* *face relaxed. “And mostly noise makers anyway, I’m not going to give Toph a light* *show for her birthday.”*

*“So you gave her a noise and explosives show instead.” Sokka takes the lighter from* *Zuko’s hand, focusing on the metal smooth with wear and warm from Zuko’s pocket.*

*“Exactly,” Zuko grins, then, “Ready?”*

*And Sokka takes a deep breath, grounding himself on Zuko’s confidence and* *excitement and steady hands and bright, eager eye, and feels himself nod. It’s good to* *see Zuko looking so assured and carefree after the stress of the past year with Azula* *and the looming trial, as if Zuko needed something more than the normal anxiety of* *graduation to deal with. And if lighting things on fire is what does it for him, well,* *then what’s a little minor arson between friends?*

*“I checked the ordinances,” Zuko reminds him as if reading his mind, “It’s legal as* *long—”*

*“—as long as we clean up the trash and ensure a proper radius and have a fire* *extinguisher on hand and don’t do any airburst fireworks without experienced* *supervision,” Sokka echoes along with him.*

*“And you’ve got the fire extinguisher,” Zuko says with a little shoulder bump. Sokka* *has had it within reach the whole time.*

*Sokka takes a deep breath and flicks a flame to life. “And you’ve got the experience* *lighting things on fire.”*

*“Yeah I do,” Zuko agrees with a fierce grin, his gaze steady as he watches Sokka* *finally light the fuse, his voice low as he murmurs reassurances and instruction, his* *hands confident and assured as he guides Sokka back a judicious distance and then* *the firework ignites, a burst of sound and faint press of heat and spray of sparks that* *has Sokka yelping in surprise and then laughing, his nervousness transforming into* *something giddy and exhilarated as the rest of the gang whoops behind them.*

*Sokka laughs again as the firework dies out, a quick half-minute blaze of glory that* *leaves him feeling more than a bit silly for his anxiety, his chest still fluttering with* *adrenaline as Zuko leans in to whisper, “Fun box.”*

*“Fun box,” Sokka agrees, watching Aang setting up some arrangement of fireworks at* *Toph’s direction. “You know, I always wondered where you were getting all those* *bottle rockets from back in college, hot stuff.”*

*“Oh, you have no idea what I’ve got,” Zuko grins, throwing Sokka a wink that sparks* *Sokka’s curiosity—and as it turns out, Zuko is more than happy to satisfy that curiosity* *once night falls, crouched next to Sokka again in the darkness as the girls scream* *through the waves, showing Sokka how to light up the night sky.*

“The fun box?” Sokka gasps, dropping to his knees as Zuko reverently lifts the lid.

“The fun box,” Zuko confirms, smile huge as they take in the neat rows of fireworks and sparklers and fuse cord and bottle rockets and things Sokka never managed to learn the name of except that he knows they’re a great time.

“Holy shit,” Sokka says, nearly awed. “I thought this went missing in the last move! Where was it?”

“Under the bed behind some storage boxes,” Zuko says, already poking through the box. “It blended in with my old Jasmine Dragon stuff.”

Sokka snorts. “That’s what you get for being over-organized and having too many storage options.”

“Shut up,” Zuko says without looking up, giving him a shove. “Shit, I wonder if any of these are still good…”

“What, you want to set them off on the roof?” Sokka asks, amused.

Zuko purses his lips, glancing at the window and the fire escape like he’s actively considering it. “It’s too light out,” he finally says, face wrinkling in annoyance about it.

“Which is of course the only reason not to set fireworks off on the roof of our apartment building,” Sokka says, dry.

“Of course.” Zuko looks totally serious. “The noisemakers might be good though,” he says, head cocked in consideration. “The light doesn’t matter for those.”

“Save it for Toph,” Sokka laughs, shaking his head. “You get this back for a minute and already you want to play with fire.”

“I bet she’d come over,” Zuko muses.

“I *know* she’d come over to blow stuff up on a roof.”

A pause, then Zuko gives him a sideways look lit up with challenge and daring, the same look he always shoots Sokka before he slams down the accelerator or decides to scale some piece of architecture not intended for scaling, a look Sokka feels like he hasn’t seen in months. “Want to take a packing break?”

Sokka opens and closes his mouth. “It’s the middle of the afternoon,” he says, slow.

“No one goes up on the roof.” Said with the confidence of someone who goes up frequently.

“It’s cold." His hands still feel the chill of carrying the food back to their apartment.

“You own a jacket,” Zuko huffs, rolling his eyes. “Unless you’ve packed it up along with your mattress, too.” Which only makes Sokka think of how much there still is to *do*, but fuck does Sokka not want to do it.

“…We’re moving out anyway, what the hell,” Sokka says, mentally tossing aside the idea of sticking to the progress he’d told himself he’d make today and oh, is it worth it for the grin that splits Zuko’s face, for the way they laugh as they scramble to their feet, for the way it suddenly feels, just for a moment, like everything is the way it’s supposed to be again.

&#45;-

***Three days and thirteen hours until the end***

Zuko feels *happy* as they make their way back down the fire escape, a little bit breathless from laughing as he jumps the last few steps down onto the landing, grinning when the metal barely clangs under his feet.

“Show off,” Sokka accuses, and Zuko just throws a grin over his shoulder because Sokka’s tone is all eye rolls and affection, because the familiar smell of smoke and sulfur is still clinging to his clothes, because exhilaration is still tingling through his limbs from the adrenaline of that moment when the spark catches and the tension builds and the startling burst—every time, even when he’s expecting it—when it finally ignites.

It's exhilaration from more than just that, Zuko knows, even if the other kind of excitement is something Zuko has plenty of experience pushing to the background of his awareness even as it makes his chest feel like it’s full of fluttering wings. The special kind of thrill that comes with knowing he’s the reason for Sokka’s laugh, that he’s the one filling Sokka’s tone with so much fondness, that he’s the object of the admiring, impressed look on Sokka’s face. The catch of his breath and beat of his heart that comes with seeing Sokka’s hair dancing in the wind and teasing the collar of his jacket, showing off his undercut and his earrings and the handspan of warm brown skin along the back of his neck that Zuko has touched before the way friends touch, especially friends like Sokka, that stretch of skin that his fingers itch to touch again, to stroke, to linger, to—

Zuko takes a deep breath as he swings through the window into his bedroom and shoves *that* warmth to the side before it can become heat, making himself focus on the warmth that’s always been there with Sokka, the lightness that comes from the fun and camaraderie and easiness of it all and fuck, has Zuko been missing it, like something in him has been parched for the feeling of not needing to *think* when it comes to Sokka. It feels like a gift Zuko didn’t even know how to ask for that they could find their way here after starting the day—the week, the month—in a way that just felt *off*.

And if Zuko should have learned anything from the past months, it's not to expect the feeling to stay. But then, Azula is always pointing out that he never does seem to learn when it comes to these kinds of things.

“Okay,” Sokka is saying as he comes into the room behind Zuko, his cheeks pink from cold and wind and laughter, “But when that—that what, the spinny screamy thing?”

“Catherine wheel,” Zuko supplies, biting back a smile at Sokka’s persistent inability to remember the names.

“Yeah, that. When the second one looked like it wasn’t going to actually go off? And then—*shit*,” Sokka breaks off as he trips, nearly eating it as his foot catches on the toolkit and Zuko lets out a burst of startled laughter at the exaggerated surprise on Sokka’s face, his hair flying and jacket flapping and arms wide as he stumbles for balance.

“Graceful,” he says as Sokka regains his footing.

“And you make a big deal about *my* floor being a tripping hazard,” Sokka grumbles, scowling at the toolkit and flipping open the lid to check that the contents are still neatly organized, the gesture so absent and familiar that the first time Zuko saw Hakoda do the same thing felt like a revelation.

“You better grab it now before it gets packed away,” Zuko teases, aware that his smile is fond and soft and probably silly and indulging himself for the moment anyway with Sokka’s focus on his tools and not him. “Everything on the floor is at risk in here.”

“Oh, that’s—” Sokka’s head snaps up so fast his earring smack against his face, his brow furrowing into a frown. “You don’t need it?”

***Seven years, five months, and eleven days until the end***

*“—And then,” Aang says as he leans to catch Zuko’s slightly-wayward toss, flicking* *the tennis ball back before pulling himself upright, “To assign* that *much reading on* *top of an assignment?”*

*“I know,” Zuko commiserates, tossing the ball back, the worn frame of the wide* *kitchen archway somehow already familiar against his back even though Katara has* *only lived here for two months now. “Professor Jeong is such an asshole about it.”*

*“I’m not trying to question his teaching style,” Aang says, scowling as he continues to* *do just that. “I just—”*

*“Mother* fu—”

*They both pause, Aang catching the ball with a solid smack and twisting to see behind* *his shoulder as Zuko leans into the kitchen as Sokka’s cursing trails off into* *indeterminate grumbles from beneath the sink. The cursing isn’t notable in and of* *itself, Sokka and this sink have been in battle since the start of the school year—* *probably why Zuko is already so familiar with this particular spot of floor. But* *Sokka’s legs where they emerge from the cabinet are still with a kind of tense* *agitation rather than his usual focused fidgeting as he works.*

*“…You okay there?” Zuko asks after a beat.*

*“…’m fine,” Sokka calls back, half-muffled and half-echoing within the cabinet and* *off the piping and plenty clear for Katara to hear as she lets out a snort from the living* *room.*

*“If you have so many opinions,” Sokka calls, managing to hear her back just fine,* *“Then you can come over and try to fix your sink yourse—”*

*“I told you I knew what to do,” Katara huffs, slamming her textbook shut. Aang twists* *back to Zuko, tossing the tennis ball with the air of someone who does not want to get* *dragged into it.*

*“Oh, of course, that’s why—”*

*“—I just need* tools*—”*

*“—call me out for the fifth time in two months!”*

*“—its* pipes *it’s not* rocket science*—”*

*“And yet your sink continues to break,” Sokka snaps back, the bite in his voice lost as* *he slaps around the kitchen floor for some tool. Apparently, he’s wedged in under* *there enough that it’s not worth coming out for. Good thing he took the hoops out of* *his ear shoving himself under there…*

*“And* you *continue to be the one who tries to fix it!”*

*“Do it yourself then!”*

*“Fine,” Katara huffs, tossing aside her homework and stalking over, barely* *acknowledging Zuko and Aang as she steps over their legs. “I will, if you—”*

*“No, there’s no* room*—”*

*“Well if you weren’t so—”*

*“Stop, that* tickles, hey!”

*Zuko coughs a little, nearly missing the toss from Aang as the siblings descend into* *squabbling.*

*“They made it twenty whole minutes this time,” he murmurs under the bickering and* *thumping and outraged noises as Katara apparently takes full advantage of Sokka’s* *vulnerable position to make her points via tickling.*

*“Almost a new record,” Aang says, checking his watch and catching the ball without* *looking. “Are you going to try to help this time again?”*

*Zuko snorts. “*I *can admit when I don’t know what I’m doing.”*

*“Hey,” Sokka says, voice suddenly coming loud and clear and Zuko gives a guilty* *start, looking over to see him out from under the sink, hair half-loose and t-shirt* *suspiciously water-spotted and eyes narrowed in Zuko’s direction. “What was that I* *heard?”*

*“Nothing,” Zuko says immediately, making his expression wide-eyed and innocent as* *he catches the ball Aang tosses back. Katara snorts.*

*“Did you say I don’t know what I’m doing?”*

*“I don’t know what you’re talking about,” Zuko says, making his voice earnest and* *concerned. “Maybe you should get your ears checked.”*

*“They might be full of water,” Katara mutters.*

*“From the sink,” Aang grins, and Sokka narrows his eyes, leveling a half-rusted tool* *toward Zuko.*

*“I’m going to prove you wrong,” he says, somewhere between a declaration and a* *threat as a thin stream of water suddenly shoots* up *from the sink behind him and Zuko* *can’t look at Aang or else he’s going to start laughing too.*

*“Alright,” Zuko says, raising his eyebrow and letting a bit of challenge curl in his* *voice. “Do it then.”*

*Sokka stares a moment, then he huffs, tossing down his tool and grabbing up a new* *one that looks suspiciously similar with new energy. “I’ll show you, hot cakes.”*

*“No, that’s a bad one,” Katara says immediately, wrinkling her nose as Aang nods.*

*“Agreed.”*

*“Everyone’s a critic,” Sokka grumbles as he wiggles his way back under the sink.* *“Fine. I’ll show you,* hot man.”

*“I’m on pins and needles waiting,” Zuko says dryly. “Do I need to put a timer on?”* *So far Katara’s sink hasn’t given them* too *much trouble, but Zuko will never forget* *their first apartment and Sokka’s ‘quick fix’ that lasted an entire weekend gone and* *involved three increasingly irate trips to the hardware store.*

*“What,” Sokka says, somehow contorting enough for half his face to become visible,* *“Like you have anything better to do on a Friday night?” Which earns a giggle from* *Aang and a laugh from Katara, but Zuko just shrugs and doesn’t try to fight it,* *because sitting here with Aang and joking with Sokka and teasing with Katara and* *pizza on the way—it’s true that he doesn’t have anywhere else he’d rather be. Not that* *he needs to admit it.*

*Though, “Don’t you?” Because Suki has a competition tomorrow if he’s remembering* *the schedule right, but Sokka just huffs.*

*“My girl appreciates a puzzle solved,” he says, diving back under, and Zuko can’t* *argue with that either.*

“I’m pretty sure I about used up the extent of my knowledge and abilities when it comes to the tools,” Zuko says ruefully, tilting his head toward the pieces of chair that it took him nearly forty-five minutes to disassemble.

“But you—” Sokka snaps his mouth closed, the frown on his face bleeding into something more like confusion. “I mean, you’re not done yet, right? You might need it.” He glances around the room, looking almost lost for a moment. “There’s still stuff that needs…?”

“Most of this I’m not going to take apart for the truck,” Zuko says after a beat, trying to parse the sudden stiffness of Sokka’s shoulders, the restless shift of his weight, the sudden shift from laughter back to the strange, tense brittleness that’s clung to Sokka the past few weeks. “There’s plenty of room so I wasn’t really going to take apart the stuff that can fit through the door easily, not all the way.”

“Oh, that—oh.” A long silence that Zuko can’t think of as anything but awkward, that he has to actively work not to sidle away from—in his *own room—*until Sokka suddenly blurts with startling urgency, “But you’re taking it, right?”

Zuko hesitates, searching Sokka’s face like the words he needs to say to bring back the teasing and easiness will be there, feeling their absence like a knife to the gut. He’s taking—what? His own stuff? The chair?

“To Boston,” Sokka says, and Zuko wonders if he’s imagining the flash of strain in that word. “You’re—you can’t be in a new apartment without a toolkit. What if—” He glances around again, looking momentarily helpless. “What if you need something fixed and the landlord isn’t fast about it?”

“I’m not sure what I can do *with* a toolkit in that situation,” Zuko says slowly, tentatively offering out a bit of self-deprecation and potential ammunition for teasing, some small, foolish tendril hope in his chest wilting when Sokka doesn’t even seem to register it.

“But you—there’s YouTube. Or you can always, you know. Call me. And I can talk you through it.”

***Ten months and eighteen days until the end***

*“And then—” Another round of choked-off giggles muffled into the crook of Sokka’s* *arm, the video swinging wildly to the sound of Sokka’s smothered laughter. “—you* *need—” Another burst of snickers.*

*“I’m going to make* you *wash all these towels,” Zuko mutters, tossing another stack of* *them at the base of the slowly overflowing toilet—clean, thank anything listening—and* *wincing as they land with a wet smack more like a puddle than a floor.*

*“Worth it,” Sokka wheezes before breaking off into another fit of laughter, and Zuko* *sighs and goes to get his laptop. He’d hang up on Sokka if all he’s going to get is* *laughed at, but at least he can listen to the background sound of Sokka’s giggles as he* *searches for a video to talk him through what to do.*

“I—I’d still do that,” Sokka says, tripping and stilted and fuck, things were just so *good.* “Obviously. I’d—I can still help.”

“Oh. Yeah,” Zuko says, floundering for something to make it better and wincing at how inane he sounds. “No, of course, I—you’d be my first call,” he finishes lamely, the overly-thick silence taking hold again on the heels of his words. Fuck, could he sound any more like he’s just humoring him? Could he sound any less enthusiastic, when he *knows* how much pride Sokka takes in being able to help out his friends like this?

“And anyway,” Sokka says, abrupt, just a shade too loud, like he was going for cheer and didn’t quite make it. “This isn’t all mine, right? The electric screwdriver is yours, and the good rubber mallet, and—”

“I don’t—I don’t mind if you have them,” Zuko cuts in, his turn to trip and stumble as something helpless and small struggles in his chest. “They’re—they can be yours.” Zuko might have bought some of them, but they’ve certainly never been *his*. And the idea of Sokka not wanting them—of having just those lone tools rattling around unused in some sad bag—

“No, I couldn’t—you shouldn’t be on your own without tools,” Sokka frowns, fiddling with his stack of bracelets. “That—that’s not right.”

“I can’t take *yours*,” Zuko starts, but Sokka is already speaking.

“Katara and Aang have their own stuff now,” Sokka says, firm and fast like he thinks he needs to race Zuko to get the words out. “And my dad and Bato have a whole garage full, and—”

“Sokka, I can’t take your tools,” Zuko repeats, hearing the words come out almost pleading. “You’ve spent like fifteen years building up this toolkit.” Slowly switching out cheap plastic and half-rusted metal for shiny chrome and fancy sets, every piece in there his pride and joy, so why is Sokka looking so stricken at the idea of keeping it? “And you can tell me what to buy, right?” Zuko adds, half-desperate to change the look on his face. “You can tell me what I need. I’ll facetime you from the hardware store so you can see the selection. You can send me spreadsheets, it—it’ll be just like yours, right?”

Except brand new and shiny, with none of the dings from Sokka’s projects or the history of how he used them—and unlikely to gain any, considering Zuko will probably never touch them except to take them out of the packaging so he can send a picture to Sokka.

But exactly the same, other than all of that.

“I…yeah,” Sokka says after a long pause, searching Zuko’s face and clearing his throat and slowly bending to take the toolkit and fuck, why does it suddenly feel so *final,* why does all of this have to feel so final? It’s not like Zuko is leaving forever, it’s not like anyone is dying, they’re still best friends, it’s not like anything’s *changed*. “Okay, I…I can do that. Totally. That—it’s a plan. Just name the date.”

“Awesome,” Zuko forces out, eyes fixed on Sokka’s familiar hands—no nail polish, there hasn’t been any for months, not that Zuko’s had the energy for it either—cradling the toolkit like it’s something precious, something fragile, something he hasn’t happily slung into the back of his truck at every possible opportunity.

“Yeah. Awesome,” Sokka echoes, giving himself a little shake before hitting Zuko so suddenly with a grin as he hefts the toolkit into a more familiar, causal hold under his arm that it’s almost disorienting. “Okay, well—let me know when you need help with that bed at a much more normal time and I’m your guy.”

Which Zuko can tell is meant to be a joke, but he doesn’t know what to *say* as it falls flat and heavy between them. “Yeah,” he manages, scrambling for any words to keep the conversation afloat. “I’ll do that. Not quite ready for floor time just yet though.”

Except Sokka just grimace-smiles in return like he’s been doing lately, hurrying out of the room muttering something about getting back into it and Zuko can only stare helplessly after him, still grasping with empty hands for something to say to call him back.

Fuck. Just…fuck. Zuko slumps, exhaling a long, slow breath that seems to take all his renewed energy with it and briefly shoving the heels of his hands against his eyes hard enough to see bursts of color against the blackness. Apparently, that was the wrong thing to say, like everything feels wrong lately, and he hates that somehow *now* feels even more awkward and uncertain between them than even before they started living together. Some reckless, stupid part of Zuko had hoped that this packing thing could somehow be fun, or at least light-hearted, something focused on and excited for what’s next, and instead…

Instead.

Zuko drops his hands with a short, hard breath, telling himself for who knows how many times that he can’t control his emotions, but he *can* control his reactions to them, and he isn’t going to spend his time fucking *wallowing*. Not now, at least,

Jaw set with determination, he goes to get another box from the neat pile in the living room, passing all the scattered, barely filled boxes littering the way, evidence of their prior attempts to start packing before it finally got down to the wire and they truly couldn’t put it off anymore. He grabs a flattened box and more tape, scrolling for a podcast because he doesn’t want to *think* right now. He’s going to have plenty of time for thinking on the six-hour drive to Boston. Right now, he just wants to lose himself in work the same way he used to lose himself in martial arts and then at the Jasmine Dragon and now at the gym, where his body is at work and his focus is on something so basic it barely requires attention and his brain can just turn off, because if he has to engage right now with what’s happening he thinks he's going to start crying, and he doesn’t think he’ll be able to stop one he starts, and he doesn’t know how he’d explain it to the one person he’s used to being able to tell everything.

Everything except one thing, of course. So really, Zuko should know exactly how to handle this.

&#45;-

***Three days and twelve hours until the end***

Zuko manages to find a groove soon enough, because not finding a groove isn’t an option, and once he’s in a body-on-head-off state—and ignoring the comments he knows Uncle would make if he saw him right now—it goes reasonably well.

He finishes up making sure his under-bed storage isn’t too heavy to transport as-is. He clears the tops of his dresser and the deep windowsills, carefully lowering his plants into the precisely cut and fitted cardboard dividers Sokka made for him a few moves back. Zuko’s always kept them tucked away for the next move, the next move, always the next move even though Sokka would happily make him a new set every time, and he firmly ignores the way it feels to know that he really does need to keep them tucked away this time. Sokka won’t be around for the next, might never be around for one again, and fuck, fuck, *fuck*, Zuko didn’t this whole thing was going to be *good* but he didn’t think it was going to be like *this*.

*It's not like the friendship is over*, he repeats to himself as he grabs a new box and a trash bag to start tackling his bedside drawer. He’s always been more ruthless about eliminating cluttering during their moves than Sokka, his ‘to keep’ piles usually dwarfed by the ‘to donate’ and ‘to toss’ ones. That this time he’s barely eliminated anything, that this time it feels so much harder to let go, is one more thing on the list to not think about until all of this is done.

And it’s not like he *can’t* get rid of things. There’s plenty to get rid of if he just sets his mind to it, if he just asks himself what he truly *needs*. And maybe he’s starting to throw things out with a bit more viciousness than is called for as he hears some stupid plastic corporate *thing* crack, but fuck, he just suddenly needs to get the feeling rising in his chest *out*, all the tangled things he’s been pushing aside and shoving down.

Sokka’s smile and the way it seems that much dimmer except maybe it’s exactly the same and it's Zuko that just can’t see the brightness anymore. The silences that no longer feel entirely comfortable, so fucking self-fulfilling he can barely even think about it. The fact that did this, he *did this,* to himself and to both of them and it can’t be undone, and he knows it had to happen, he’s told himself more times than he cares to count, but fuck, he still feels it knotted and ugly inside him just like he’s felt for days and weeks and months and shit, longer? And he just needs to it *out*.

And the gym this morning did nothing, too full of Sokka even without him there, and he doesn’t pick fights anymore no matter how much he wants the clarifying rush of adrenaline and purpose, and he can’t exactly hop in his car and race down the highway in the middle of winter because there could be ice and he’s *responsible* these days, and he can’t fucking bother Sokka to think up an activity, or just bother Sokka in general until his mind is engaged in something better.

No, all he can do is *sit here* and pretend it's *fine* the way he’s been doing for months—*years*, and it was, it was *fine*, why did he—why couldn’t he just—and throw out useless item after useless item. Empty note pads and broken hair ties—

***Two years, two months, and seventeen days until the end***

*“Shit,” Zuko mutters under his breath, hair tie snapping off his wrist as he goes to* *wrap it the last time around his bun.*

*“Here,” Sokka says, shoving his keys into his mouth to drag a tie out from under his* *stack of bracelets.*

*“Thanks,” Zuko says, bumping Sokka lightly with his elbow as he pulls his hair up* *and earning himself a flash of grin in response.*

*“I got you, hotman.”*

—why did he keep all this *crap*, why did he think he’d want movie theater tickets—

***One year, one month, and four days until the end***

*Zuko nearly falls out of his as they desperately try to muffle their laughter in the* *otherwise silent theater, Sokka slipping down right next to him making choked little* *snorting noises that only set Zuko off even more, silent gasps that leave him* *lightheaded and even more giggly.*

*They’re earning dirty looks from the people around them but neither of them can stop* *because the scene is* still *going, and, “It’s just—it’s just so* bad*,” Sokka wheezes* *before succumbing to another fit of laughter, leaning into Zuko to try to stay upright* *until Zuko doesn’t know if he’s shaking from his own amusement or Sokka’s.*

—and *corporate pens*—

***One year, nine months, and fifteen days until the end***

*“This is where your party is?” Zuko asks, skeptical, glancing around the decked-out* *space that is…unmistakably a corporate cafeteria.*

*“I know, right?” Sokka says under his breath, checking that his shirt is still tucked in* *for the tenth time in as many minutes, like anything about his appearance right now* *needs any work. “I told them we should rent out a restaurant or something again, but* *Jeff thought we shouldn’t spend the money.”*

*“What a way to celebrate Employee Appreciation Day,” Zuko says, dry, earning* *himself a quick grin.*

*“Jeff didn’t like that observation either,” Sokka says, reaching out to flick something* *off the shoulder of Zuko’s blazer as his expression turns wicked. “Know what else Jeff* *won’t like?”*

*“Your opinions on his management style?”*

*“If we play a little game.”*

*Zuko raises his eyebrow, intrigued despite himself. The best he’d hoped from agreeing* *to join Sokka at his work party was a night of moderately passable passed apps and* *finally getting to put faces to all the names from Sokka’s work stories—and maybe a* *little bit of discrete ogling that he didn’t quite admit to himself that he was looking* *forward to until he finally saw Sokka in his fitted blazer and tailored pants and* *coordinated earrings and briefly felt his higher level reasoning depart his body, and* *he might be trying to get past this but a little bit of looking just this once won’t hurt,* *right?*

*Like it’s ever just been once…*

*“You see all the corporate swag around here?” Sokka asks, unaware—always—of the* *direction of Zuko’s thoughts as he tilts his head toward the little tables set up with* *what looks like post-its and pens and lint rollers and—are those mousepads? Who still* *uses those? “There’s more of that all over this place. There’s supposed to be a limit* *on what you take.”*

*“So appreciative,” Zuko murmurs.*

*“I know, right? Jeff’s a disaster.” He gives Zuko a sideways look, his expression* *inviting, warm, and it takes everything Zuko has not to sway forward into the heat of* *him. “Want to see which of us can leave with the most without getting caught?”*

—and receipts to the fucking petting zoo, why—

***Two years, four months, and nine days until the end***

*“I can’t believe Seung couldn’t be around for your birthday,” Sokka grumbles for* *what has to be the fifth time, and Zuko turns from trying to catch any kind of clue from* *the passing road signs to give Sokka a dry look.*

*“It’s not exactly uncommon for people to have plans the day after Thanksgiving,”* *Zuko says, for what also has to be the fifth time.*

*Sokka blows a raspberry at the idea. “Boo,” he jeers, carefully signaling and double-checking all his mirrors before switching lanes. “She’s your* girlfriend*.”*

*“I am aware,” Zuko says, amused. He* is *the one dating her.*

*“A significant other should be there on your birthday!”*

*“We’ve only been dating for…” Zuko does some quick math. “Eight months.” Shit,* *has it really been that long?*

*“Which is* plenty *of time to know when your boyfriend’s birthday is!* I *know when your* *birthday is!”*

*Zuko bites back a smile. “You schedule everyone’s birthdays in your phone the minute* *you decide they’re your friend.”*

*“So I know it!” Sokka slows as they approach an exit, easing the car down to the* *posted speed limit to take the off-ramp’s gentle curve, yielding obediently at the base* *of the ramp before merging onto the completely empty road.*

*Zuko lets out a pointed sigh—which Sokka equally pointedly ignores—and takes in* *their surroundings. Lots of trees, lots of fields, lots of…hay? “I can’t believe you* *ditched your family to take me on some road trip to the boonies,” Zuko says.*

*“I ditched my family for your* birthday surprise *since apparently no one else was going* *to do it!”*

*Zuko has a feeling it’s going to take more than a few days for Sokka to stop being* *outraged by that fact. “Iroh—”*

*“Would have made you a lovely gluten-free vegan keto sugar-free cupcake. Or else* *mixed it up with the special muffins he makes Jet.”*

*“…Okay, well Azula—”*

*“Would have taken you on a birthday workout.”*

*…That* had *been her suggestion.*

*“You can do those later,” Sokka continues, scoffing at the idea of them. “And my* *family will be around later today, anyway, for the rest of the surprise.”*

*Zuko raises his eyebrow. “Let that one slip, did you.”*

*“Like you didn’t know it was happening and that I’m getting you out of the apartment* *for a reason,” Sokka says. “You need a proper, full shebang birthday. You only turn* *twenty-seven once!”*

*“Generally, you only turn each age once.”*

*“Which is why it needs to be so special! Did Seung even get you a present?”*

*“Of course she did,” Zuko huffs. They’ve been dating for eight months, apparently,* *that’s what you* do*, she’s not some terrible girlfriend just because she has family to* *spend the holiday with and didn’t invite Zuko to come, not that he particularly wanted* *to drive two hours into New Jersey with her.*

*“What was it?” Sokka asks, and Zuko should have known he would because the only* *thing Sokka likes more than getting presents is giving them. Zuko knows that all of his* *birthday presents go into some list or spreadsheet or note somewhere to track for* *future gift-giving occasions.*

*But the thing is…*

*“Zuko!” Sokka exclaims when he takes too long to answer. “She* didn’t*?”*

*“She didn’t give it to me yet!” Zuko protests, wincing at Sokka’s high squawk of* *outrage. “I’m seeing her when she gets back! She’s going to give it to me then, it’s not* *a big deal!”*

*“The first birthday she celebrates with her boyfriend, and she doesn’t even make sure* *he has his present to open for it!”*

*“Maybe not everyone has your strict standards,” Zuko huffs, rolling his eyes.*

*“Well, it’s a good thing you have me, then, isn’t it? If this is what you’re dealing* *with.”*

*It is, it is, and Zuko smothers that down and reaches for lightness and a subject* *change. “Not that I don’t love car trips with you—”*

*Sokka snorts. They both know it drives Zuko nuts not to drive in general and in* *particular when Sokka is the one driving, with Sokka’s careful observation of all* *posted speed limits and his diligent obedience of all local, state, and federal traffic* *ordinances.*

*“—But if you* told *me, then—”*

*“Curiosity killed the cat,” Sokka says immediately, prim.*

*“And satisfaction brought him back.”*

*“And he has nine lives anyway so what’s one here or there,” Sokka agrees. “But you* *just got the one, hotman, so you’re going to have to wait. Choose the music,” he adds* *when Zuko makes a show of pouting, which Zuko is happy to do, purposefully picking* *songs he knows will aggravate Sokka. Not the really bad ones, but the kind of that* *Zuko knows get into Sokka’s head, the ones Zuko knows how to sequence until Sokka* *can’t help but shoot him an aggravated look and sing along. And it’s short work from* *there to pick a few that really hit an itch Sokka can’t help but scratch no matter how* *ridiculous he sounds belting it out alongside a sixteen-year-old pop sensation, and the* *car is full of Zuko’s teasing and giggles and Sokka’s pointedly louder and louder* *singing when their momentum suddenly slows, Zuko quickly straightening in time to* *see them pass a sign for—*

*“A farm?”*

*Sokka grins.*

*“Sokka. You brought me to a farm in the middle of November for my birthday?”*

*Sokka’s grin takes on a decidedly smug look.*

*“Isn’t this more of a—*a baby llama!” *Zuko shouts, pressing up against the window as* *they slowly cruise past, breath caught at all the* fur *and* fluff *and—a* lamb! *And* another, *and holy shit is that a gigantic rabbit hutch in the distance?*

*“It’s not a farm,” Sokka says, audibly smug as they pull into a gravel parking lot.* *“It’s a petting zoo.”*

*And Zuko isn’t even embarrassed for the noise he lets out—he gets to* touch—*them?* *All of them?—finally tearing his eyes away long enough to get out of the car to see* *Sokka’s hands full of scarfs and hats and mittens whose tops can fold back, his face lit* *with triumph and delight.*

*Zuko can’t help but smile back, laughing a little as he pulls on a hat in his favorite* *deep red and not even thinking as he reaches out to tug Sokka’s blue one down to* *cover his ears so his piercings don’t get cold too fast, Sokka’s eyes crinkling in* *happiness. The air is crisp enough to make their breath visible and to nip at his cheeks* *but his entire body feels so warm in this moment. So warm and loved and cared for* *and shit, he needs to break up with Seung. And fuck is this not fair.*

Seung got him…a macaroni and cheese cookbook, Zuko thinks, wracking his brain to try to remember. He gave it away to David at work almost before he ended the call breaking up with her. He still has the receipt from that day, though. And not even his fucking receipt, Sokka never lets him pay for anything on his birthday and he feels a sudden, furious kind of grief as he carefully sets the crinkled paper on the bed alongside the things to keep. Why can’t he just make himself put it in the fucking trash pile and behind him? Isn’t this what this whole thing is about? Moving on? Moving forward?

But all he can do is begin dumping the rest of his bedside table drawer into the trash with a force that suddenly feels perfectly necessary, his jaw set and tears fighting to break free and hating that even though he knows—has known—that he can’t keep dragging this with him, he still can’t seem to find a way to let go.

&#45;-

***Three days and ten hours until the end***

Going through the motions of cooking dinner does something to loosen the tight knot between Zuko’s shoulder blades the way it has ever since Uncle first suggested he could make his own breakfast if he was going to be so picky about it. The heat and flame, the thought-free focus of the knifework, watching a set of ingredients come together into a new whole because of his effort. And the flexibility of it, too, where there’s almost always a way to fix something going wrong, to tweak a flavor or work around a missing ingredient, and when there isn’t, there’s almost always a clear, consistent explanation for what went wrong, something obvious that Zuko can plainly see or one of those things that makes Sokka rattle off about chemistry and bonds and interactions and that’s why they have curdled cream in the soup.

And cooking dinner with Sokka—it’s different, of course. More, in a way so many things are more when Sokka is involved, in a way Zuko eventually stopped trying to fight and just appreciates for what it is because it’s not just cooking with commentary and jokes and stories about Sokka’s day filling the air. It’s the rhythm of navigating around each other like their bodies have memorized the steps to some silent dance. It’s Sokka knowing what Zuko is making without even needing to ask and folding himself into the process with nothing more than a sideways smile because he’s done it so many times before. It’s Zuko adjusting to fit Sokka in as easy as breathing.

And it eases something sharp-edged and tangled in Zuko’s chest that it’s *still* easy today after everything else has felt so stumbling and dissonant, relieves some anticipated hurt Zuko didn’t even realize he was bracing for until it fails to land. And it might just be a reprieve, a temporary stay, but Zuko will take whatever he can get, even if it means there’s still some tight, ugly thing full of regret whispering in the back of his mind that if he’d just held it in, sucked it up, kept quiet like he’s done before, kept his *shit* together and not caught feelings in the first place and then just not let them get in the way once he had them, because if Zuko knows anything it’s how to ignore the way things make him feel, even now after so many years of working on it—if he’d just done all of that, then maybe Zuko could still have this unconscious ease all the time and not just in bursts and hold-his-breath-waiting-for-the-end stretches. This unthinking way they occupy each other’s space that Zuko didn’t realize how much he’d grown around and grown to rely on until suddenly he was yanking it out from under himself.

But he did do it, as thoroughly as he could manage. And Zuko knows better than to think that spending energy lamenting what’s already been done has ever been anything but useless.

“The jalapenos were spicy this time,” Sokka says around his first mouthful of curry once they finally settle in to eat. He’s seated like a gremlin in his chair like he always is, of course, and Zuko would point out that this is probably why Sokka finishes his workday with his back so sore if Sokka pretzeling himself in those shrunk-in-the-wash gym shorts wasn’t currently treating Zuko to a stretch of calf and thigh that he really shouldn’t be staring at.

At this point in the day, though—at this point in *this* day—Zuko doesn’t know if he has the willpower left to stop himself from peeking through his lashes at the flex of Sokka’s forearm where his Henley is shoved up over his elbows, at the press of his chest against his shirt as he reaches for a napkin, at the way his earrings catch the overhead light and tug Zuko’s attention to the line of his cheekbone. And at all that warm brown skin, skin, skin where his shorts have ridden up.

“It probably didn’t need the triple helping of chili oil,” Sokka adds, thoughtful, lips wrapped around his spoon in a way that would be obscene if it weren’t so unconscious. “It’s a bit much.”

Zuko hums, eyes lingering, and after a moment he realizes that probably warrants a response. “But it’s good this way,” he says, raising his eyebrow as he dashes a bit of extra oil into his bowl, grinning when Sokka snorts but doesn’t disagree, just reaches out to grab the bottle when Zuko is done—along with the bowl of extra rice—and Zuko doesn’t get stuck thinking about the warm brush of Sokka’s fingers against his own, he doesn’t, he doesn’t, he doesn’t.

The space around them is more full of quiet than chatter, but not unusually so. It’s more the quiet after one of Sokka’s harder workdays when he just needs to decompress, and Zuko is happy tonight to just inhale along with him and feel the knotted tension through his shoulders unwind for the first time in…a while. And if its more basking in Sokka’s presence and stealing glances than necessarily relaxing—fuck it. It’s the end of a long day and a long week and a long fucking year, and there are only three days left, and at this point what’s the harm in looking?

What extra harm can Zuko do after all he’s already done to them?

“Seconds?” Sokka asks when Zuko finishes his bowl, tugging Zuko out of his thoughts—probably for the best—with a gentle nudge against his foot for attention. “There’s a ton of rice left. How many meals were you planning?”

“I wanted to try to finish it,” Zuko says, passing over his bowl as Sokka rises to his feet. “Moving a bag of rice is like moving pure weight, it’s such a waste.”

Sokka grunts as he ladles out more curry, something that might be agreement, wooden spoon tapping against the pot a moment before he abruptly says, “Hey, do you remember where all the magnets are from?”

Zuko blinks, but he’s more than used to Sokka’s sudden trains of thought, twisting to follow his gaze to the fridge. “Top left is from the swag grab—”

“Well obviously,” Sokka snorts, leaning his hip against the counter. “And next to it is undergrad orientation—”

“Then that time we went to the aquarium with Hakoda and Bato—”

“Did Aang and Katara give us the middle one after their honeymoon?”

“No,” Zuko snorts, taking a steadying breath at the way his stomach flips as he stares at the mishmash of swirling reds and golds and oranges that make up no discernable shape. “That’s the one *you* got me when we went to Greece.”

***Five years, six months, and thirty days before the end***

*“Okay,” Sokka is saying, pretzeled up at the kitchen table, tapping a pen down his* *clipboard, another tucked behind his ear and a third shoved through the base of his* *wolf tail that Zuko’s pretty sure he’s forgotten are there. “You packed your* *toothbrush?”*

*“Mhm,” Zuko says as he pulls out jars for overnight oats. It’s no one’s favorite, but* *they’re going to want a quick breakfast tomorrow on the way to the airport. He is* not *doing the security line on an empty stomach. No one needs that.*

*“A normal one, not the electric one? Because if they get weird about the battery again* *—”*

*“A normal one,” Zuko assures him, getting the milk out of the fridge and considering* *a moment before grabbing some berries, too. They’ll definitely be bad by the time they* *get back.*

*“And sunscreen? Because all your tattoo aftercare—”*

*“I have so many three-ounce bottles of sunscreen packed I could last a month.”*
