*“Great,” Sokka says to the sound of his pen against the real actual printed-out* *checklist he came home from work with. “And extra socks? Because we might change* *after the city tour and the museum tour, not just the hike, and—”*

*“Did you remember your knee brace?” Zuko interrupts.*

*“…Be right back.”*

*Zuko bites back a smile as he grabs the measuring cups to the sound of Sokka’s chair* *scraping back and the slide-thump of him taking the corner too fast in socked feet, the* *familiar noise of him tossing his bedroom to find something beginning to drift down* *the hallways.*

*“…Wow,” Toph says, letting the word hang in the air, and Zuko glances back over his* *shoulder, looking for the source of her commentary.*

*“Huh?”*

*“*Wow*,” she repeats, letting the front legs of her chair drop back to the floor with a* *thump. “Really?”*

*Zuko frowns, trying to figure out what has her so worked up. “…Do you want some* *berries too? There aren’t a lot, but I could drizzle them with honey the way Uncle did* *that one time—”*

*“You’re like a married couple over here at the ripe age of twenty-four, aren’t you,”* *she interrupts, incredulous. “Toothbrushes and socks and a vacation for two? What’s* *next, are you going to fold his underwear for him?”*

*“Just my shirts,” Sokka calls from down the hallway, which…isn’t exactly* un*true. But* *if Zuko is doing his own why not do Sokka’s too? Leaving him a pile on his bed just* *feels rude. “It’s because I’m only twenty-three, I’m a child bride. I’m not allowed to* *use the appliances.”*

*“Folding classically involving so many appliances,” Toph snorts, her focus staying* *pinned in Zuko’s direction, though, something weighty about her attention that he* *doesn’t know what to do with.*

*“Maybe we’re just best buds,” Zuko shrugs. It’s not exactly the first time one of their* *friends has joked about third-wheeling or going on a double date with them, a teasing* *that’s always made something warm and happy fizz through Zuko’s chest with the way* *it so casually lays claim to him in so many ways, folding him into so many different* *kinds of caring and wanting.*

*“The bestest,” Sokka agrees as he comes back into the kitchen, slinging an arm* *around Zuko’s shoulder and smacking a kiss onto his cheek with a loud “mwah” that* *Toph is sure to hear. “My husband here is letting me be a stay-at-home wife, after all,* *and still does my laundry,” he says, throwing an exaggerated wink when Zuko rolls* *his eyes. “All I have to care about are his toothbrushes and socks.”*

*“You worked like seventy hours last week,” Zuko says.*

*“It’s why I’m so excited to stay at home!”*

*“Until you get an email from your work-wife—”*

*“Gina is a goddess.”*

*“—and then you’ll throw me over in an instant for her project management and* *documentation.”*

*“Do you know how hard it is to find someone who* likes *documenting SOPs?”*

*“And then what am I going to be left with?”*

*“Toothbrushes and overnight oats,” Sokka says, nodding gravely and reaching to* *steal a berry, sticking out his tongue when Zuko smacks his hand away. There aren’t* *enough to sustain breakfast* and *Sokka’s snacking.*

*“Toothbrushes and overnight oats,” Zuko agrees. “Eat some grapes if you’re hungry,* *those are going to get all weird when we’re gone, too.”*

*“Grapes?” Sokka pouts, putting on the puppy dog eyes. “When you have fresh* *blueberries right there?”*

*“Grapes,” Zuko says firmly.*

*“…You don’t even need me here, do you,” Toph cuts in, dripping sarcasm, which is all* *the invitation Zuko needs to pretend to not even hear her.*

*“Hey, I bet if we pretend to be on a honeymoon, we’ll get free champagne and* *dessert.”*

*“You had me at ‘free,’” Sokka says around a mouthful of grapes, managing to hold a* *straight face for another few seconds before bursting into laughter and taking Zuko* *along with him.*

*“Yeah, yeah, put on your show,” Toph mutters, holding out a demanding hand.* *“Gimme some grapes. I’m going to need sustenance to deal with you idiots for the rest* *of the night.”*

“Huh,” Sokka says, tilting his head as he takes in the magnet like a new angle will suddenly make it more Greece-like. “I thought that was the one on the bottom.”

“One would think that,” Zuko says as he flicks a look at the stylized torch. “That’s a knock-off Olympics magnet from Aang.”

Sokka purses his lips, absently licking the back of the curry spoon before dishing himself another serving. Zuko sighs and mentally consigns all the leftovers to Sokka. “It really is a bad magnet.”

“Yup.”

“Like, it’s not ugly or anything.”

“Nope.”

“But it could really just be from anywhere.” Another lick. Definitely Sokka’s leftovers. “What a shitty souvenir.”

“There’s a reason *I* didn’t buy it,” Zuko says, dry.

“You liked the colors, though,” Sokka says, like the decision to buy it for him was that easy, that simple, and Zuko clears his throat, busying himself with brushing grains of fallen rice off the table.

“Yeah,” he says, forcing his voice to come out even. “I still do.” And Sokka shoots him a little half smile before getting back to dishing out second helpings, and after a moment of wavering, Zuko shoves away the awareness that he shouldn’t taunt himself—tempt himself—with things like things, with memories of how it used to be and never will be again. He knows that whatever this moment is that it’s finite, temporary, that indulging will just make the end hurt all the more.

But he’s just as good at shoving things aside these days as he used to be, as it turns out. Particularly the things he doesn’t want to have to face.

&#45;-

***Three days and eight hours until the end***

It’s somehow simultaneously later than Zuko wants it to be and far too early when the apartment finally quiets for the night, Sokka retreating into his room though a golden glow still seeps out from under his door. And Zuko might normally knock, might normally peek his head in, shoot the shit or ask about whether Sokka wants to go to the gym with him in the morning or ask about his poetry or his art if it’s one of those more thoughtful, introspective nights.

But tonight Zuko just takes himself to his own room, pretending like it doesn’t make him cold in a way he doesn’t know what to do with to walk away from that strip of light.

The space feels uncomfortably barren with the art off the walls and the plants in their box and the little trinkets and knickknacks gone from sight and packed safely away. Impersonal. Lifeless. Like maybe Zuko never actually lived here for the past two years at all except for the neat pile of clothes he’s set aside, enough for the next few days and the drive up to Boston, a few extra changes to account for unpacking and stopping over to visit Mai and Ty Lee. Though considering how he’s suddenly feeling choked up just from looking at his extra socks, maybe he should just drive straight through. He’s not sure he’s going to be able to handle company immediately after this, especially not people who know him so well…

Zuko does a few of the deep, meditative breaths Iroh showed him when he was a kid, trying to empty his mind even though he was never good at it unless he was in motion. Then he focuses on busying himself getting ready for bed, showering and changing into sleep pants and brushing his teeth, trying to occupy himself with thinking of nothing more than the immediate task at hand and what he’ll do tomorrow—or no, what he’ll be doing less than half a year from now. That’s a better thing to focus on, starting law school somehow an easier thing to conceptualize than getting through the next day.

He imagines the mix of anxiousness and excitement that will fill his chest the night before the first day, pictures himself in those first classes and studying for the first set of examples in the picturesque libraries and coffee shops that fill up the school website and social media, tries to envision himself cooking into the homey looking kitchen he’s looked at over and over in the apartment listing.

The prospect of it all fills him with a mix of trepidation and anticipation that carries him safely through his evening routine until he’s finally laying down in bed and reaching to turn out the lonely-looking lamp on the nightstand, stilling as he catches sight of something on the floor.

***Six years, two months, and fourteen days until the end***

*Zuko rocks to a stop, looking across the forest suddenly spilling out before him as the* *path abruptly dumps them onto a broad, jutting cliff like mother nature set out to* *design the perfect observatory point. He can feel the good-tired sensation of muscles* *well used now that he’s still enough to be aware of them, suddenly noticing he way the* *sweat on his skin prickles in the crisp air sneaking through the open zipper of his* *jacket, sucking in a breath and smelling dry winter air and slowly rotting leaves and* *damp wood and something earthy underneath it all that makes his brain say ‘nature.’*

*It's totally silent except for his own panting breaths—except it’s a silence full of the* *rustle of pine needles and brush of twigs in the wind, full of a distant river somewhere* *beneath his feet and some hawk too small to see crying overhead, overflowing with the* *noise of a thousand things all in harmony and none of them human and fuck, but it* *feels good to close his eyes and just…breathe.*

*A deep inhale, feeling his chest expand, his shoulders lift, concentrating on letting the* *air fill his belly, visualizing it going all the way down to his toes. And then the exhale,* *letting it whoosh out of him and feeling a tiny piece of the tension that’s been clenched* *around his spine for the past week slowly go out with it.*

*Zuko doesn’t know how long he’s been just standing there and breathing for—long* *enough to think about maybe zipping up his jacket—when he hears the soft noise of* *leaves shifting over the ground as Sokka approaches. Then there’s a gentle tap against* *his wrist, light enough that Zuko could ignore it if he really wanted to. But he opens* *his eyes instead to see Sokka waggling a protein bar in his direction. “Snack?”*

*“Thanks,” Zuko rasps, his voice coming out hoarser than a day of so little talking* *should warrant but maybe as much as a week of pent-up screaming deserves.*

*Sokka hums in answer, silently offering a water bottle from one of the many pockets of* *his backpack. Then he scrambles up onto a nice, wide boulder, settling himself as if he* *has nothing better to do with his day than sit on this mountain without cell service or* *people and barely even a trail and soak in sun for as long as Zuko cares to stop here.*

*Which implies a decision, of sorts, even though he knows Sokka doesn’t mean to lay* *one out, that the day is all about* not *having to weigh any major decisions for Zuko,* *and he shifts, abruptly restless again. He wants the quiet, the distance from people, the* *not having to think about his words or his face or how he’s coming across, but he* *suddenly finds that he needs to be not quite this alone with his thoughts. “What else do* *you have in there?” he asks, jutting his chin toward Sokka’s bag.*

*“Whatever you need,” Sokka says, unzipping his vest and leaning back on his hands,* *shooting Zuko a quick smile as he tosses the loose strands of his wolf tail out of his* *face.*

*Zuko grunts, scraping Sokka in his sweat-damp, dirt-smudged sweatshirt with his* *battered, well-loved backpack with a look that Zuko can feel is too sharp, too hard.* *“For the jury to finally stop fucking around and hand down a conviction?” he bites* *out, hugging his arms tightly over his chest as he tries to press down the jagged thing* *suddenly trying to claw out of him.*

*“For you to realize that regardless of the outcome, testifying against your dad was* *really brave and really important?”*

*Zuko snaps his mouth shut, fists loosening as the prickly, stinging edges on his tongue* *melt away in the face of Sokka’s quiet seriousness.*

*“Or maybe just some peace and quiet,” Sokka adds after a moment, making a show of* *settling himself to take in the view. “That’s pretty nice too, in a place like this,” he* *muses, and Zuko huffs out a reluctant laugh, exhaling hard to try to blow the* *misplaced, scared anger out of him again before climbing up next to Sokka.*

*“Maybe not quite that,” he says, stretching out his legs alongside Sokka’s and giving* *his boot a nudge of silent apology. “I wouldn’t want you to hurt yourself.”*

*“I’m sure I could survive a few hours,” Sokka says, his smile relaxed, unbothered, his* *gaze somehow questioning without crossing the line to the pity and concern that Zuko* *has found so hard to take from everyone else as the trial has dragged on.*

*“Better not risk it, though,” Zuko says, clearing his throat a little as he’s suddenly hit* *with an unexpected wave of relief. Which maybe he* should *expect—his emotions have* *been all over the place for weeks now—but fuck, he really doesn’t know how he’d be* *doing this without Sokka.*

*Aang has been great, Katara and Toph, Mai especially, Uncle, but Zuko knows* *Sokka’s been leaving work early and making sure someone else is always swinging by* *when he can’t be around. That he’s been dragging out Zuko’s favorite recipes and* *movies and shows and playlists, quietly doing his laundry in a way he thinks Zuko* *won’t notice except Zuko* does*, he does, and fuck, just the idea of needing to do it all* *on his own right now—*

*“Better not,” Sokka snorts, tipping his face back to the sun, his voice level like Zuko* *only ever really hears him in a moment of crisis which is, he supposes, what this is, in* *a long, drawn-out way. “I’ll just keep myself to easy observations, hm?” A quick peek,* *a flash of humor. “No sharing any of the fan theories I read last night about—”*

*“Hey! We said no spoilers!”*

*“—no life philosophy—"*

*“Please, no,” Zuko mutters.*

*“Just us and nature, right?” Sokka bumps his shoulder, clearly tucking away a smile.* *“Look at those nice clouds. Spectacular, aren’t they?”*

*“Fluffy,” Zuko says, rolling his eyes even though part of him is so, so eager for* *someone to not be walking on eggshells around him even though he knows he’s a pent-up explosion waiting to happen, one that no amount of therapy over the past months* *has managed to diffuse.*

*“And this rock?” Sokka says, reaching to grab up a small stone. “Great rock. Look at* *all those different shades of grey. It’s gotta be one of a kind.”*

*“Beautiful,” Zuko says, pointedly looking at all the identical rocks on the ground all* *around them.*

*“Here.” Sokka makes a big show of polishing the rock against his vest before* *dramatically holding it out with a flourish and a half-bow. “Have it as a gift.”*

*“So generous.”*

*“When am I not, hot man?”*

*“Want a list?”*

*“Hey!” Sokka protests, smacking Zuko’s chest with the back of his hand. “I am a* *prince among men! Give me that rock back, you don’t deserve—”*

*“Nope, it’s mine now.”*

*“All these insults and you think you can just—”*

*“A gift's a gift! No take backsies!”*

*“I’ll take backsies you right off this—”*

*Zuko laughs and holds the rock out of reach, squirming away from Sokka’s tickling* *fingers—for all his complaints about it, he fights exactly like Katara does—and using* *his longer reach to keep the stone away from Sokka’s grabbing hands as they wrestle* *without any real heat, shoving and pushing but not enough to threaten to dislodge* *either of them from their perch. And fuck, but it feels good to let himself just let go like* *this, feels good like he hasn’t felt in weeks. Feels good like he could finally be* *grabbing onto a little inkling of what Uncle’s been promising, a little bit of the belief* *that maybe, no matter what the jury finally decides, Zuko is going to find a way to be* *okay.*

Zuko blinks rapidly, reaching down between the bedside table and the mattress, the weight and shape and smoothness of the rock still familiar even though Zuko hasn’t felt the need to hold it in years. It must have fallen out of his bedside table drawer in his flurry of sorting, earlier. Or maybe it got knocked from the ‘to keep’ pile by his less-than-gentle additions to the stack.

He hefts it in his hand, carefully tracing his thumb across one of the lighter veins of grey. It’s such a little thing. Such an *important* thing. But innocuous to anyone else looking at it, probably easily mistaken for trash, for something to toss away, and for a moment Zuko is overwhelmed by how easily he almost lost it, the grief of it sweeping up out of his gut faster than he can catch it.

Fuck, it would have been so easy to leave behind, this thing that matters so much to him in ways he can’t articulate except just that it *does*. This tangible, physical reminder that Zuko has faced things before that he didn’t know how he was going to do and found the way through them. And fuck, as he stares down this impossible thing he has three more days to do, maybe he needs the reminder now more than ever. As he stares it down without his friends rallied behind him this time, without being able to lean on Uncle because that would mean sharing more than Zuko knows how to share.

Without Sokka knowing what he needed almost before Zuko realized he needed it.

But Zuko has done hard things before. Things he knows were worse than this, no matter what it feels like right now. And he knows, as he clutches the reminder in his hand, that just because he can’t see how he’s going to get through the next few days, that just because he can’t imagine how he’s going to find it in himself to actually leave when the U-Haul is packed and the apartment is empty, that just because he can’t picture turning the keys and actually leaving Sokka behind him— that doesn’t mean he won't be able to do it when the time is finally here.

## Chapter 2: The Way It Used To Be

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Warnings this chapter for some horny pining, my self-indulgent love of dialogue, boys engaging in intricate rituals, and a whole lot of oblivious idiocy.

### Text

***Two days and twenty-three hours until the end***

Zuko finally gives up on sleeping when the clock turns over to 8:00am. He tries to stay in bed these days instead of giving in to the itching urge to wander the apartment the way he used to when he and Sokka first started living together, back before he realized that Uncle always happening to make his way into the kitchen or living room a few minutes after Zuko wasn’t because it was normal for everyone to just be up in the middle of the night, but because Uncle didn’t want Zuko to be alone.

Zuko isn’t filled with so much itching restlessness these days, but he’s still used to being the only one up for hours without work or pestering about gym time to drag Sokka out of bed. So Zuko does an actual double take, rocking to a stop, when he walks past the living room on the way to the kitchen and sees Sokka already in there.

He’s sitting on the floor surrounded by piles of flattened cardboard and a wall of assembled boxes, looking tired and sleep-mussed and gorgeous. There are dark circles under his eyes, his shoulders slumped in a way that speaks of more than just the early-for-him hour, and Zuko isn’t prepared to stop himself from lingering over how long Sokka’s hair has gotten, even his undercut beginning to grow out loose and wavey and Zuko can practically feel what it would be like to have the strands running through his fingers. He isn’t prepared to look away from Sokka’s familiar sleep clothes, the fabric bright and thin from washing and clinging in a way that makes Zuko want to press against the expanse of Sokka’s chest, makes him want to test the strength of Sokka’s thighs, makes him want to find out just how well-tied the drawstring of those pants really are.

It’s nothing Zuko hasn’t seen before but somehow first thing in the morning when he expected nothing but quiet space to gather himself ahead of the day, it hits him right in the gut, low and wanting. It catches him before he has the chance to carefully barricade his thoughts against things like this and leaves him suddenly breathless and overly warm and overly conscious of his own bare chest, of the pants clinging low on his hips, and fuck, he needs to stop thinking about this, about all of it, before his own thin PJs start to become a problem.

“You’re up early,” Zuko rasps out, dragging his gaze up and firmly fixing his eyes on Sokka’s face, which…is a marginal improvement. Zuko’s fantasies have wrapped one too many times around the image of that face looking down at him or up from the mattress or from between Zuko’s legs for it to be entirely—*fuck.*

Sokka offers a one-shouldered shrug and grumbles something unintelligible, eyes barely dragging up from the box he’s assembling, which is a small favor as Zuko yanks himself firmly into the now and away from all the useless will-never-bes. Sokka might be up but he clearly isn't awake, or at least isn't wanting to be, and that's familiar and normal and *easy* enough for Zuko to seize onto, to find his way to the comfortable space of teasing. “A bit hard to sleep in when you’re sleeping on the floor like you’ve still got teenage bones, is it?”

***Eleven years, four months, and twelve days before the end***

*Zuko watches, feeling a small smile tugging at his scar in a way that doesn’t feel all* *that weird anymore even if it’s still noticeable, as Sokka meticulously arranges throw* *pillows and a pile of sweatshirts into what he assures Zuko is going to be the best bed* *of his life.*

*“Trust me,” Sokka says, stepping back to eye his handiwork and somehow knowing to* *raise a hand to deflect the pillow Katara sends swinging toward his face. “You’re* *going to feel like you’re lying on clouds.”*

*Zuko just hums, tucking his arms around his knees and trying to hold himself out of the* *way of the chaos of fitting twelve people into a single hotel room, a feat in and of itself* *even before those twelve people are all still wired from the concert. He thinks about* *offering that he and sleeping don’t really get along to begin with, but he’s learned by* *now that that’s the kind of comment Aang will manage to hear from across a room* *and over the sound of nine other people all talking over each other and refuse to let go* *of until Zuko explains it.*

*And tonight, with the buoyant energy of the crowd still bubbling through him, with* *Toph and Suki scream-singing the chorus of their favorite song as if they’re trying to* *make their voices heard all the way up on the stage again, with every moment so* happy*, Zuko doesn’t want to have a heart-to-heart. He doesn’t want a deep* *conversation, an explanation that he knows from experience will make everyone force* *their smiles around awkward silences. He just wants to enjoy the night just like* *everyone else, along with everyone else.*

*And if sleeping on a pile of sweatshirts on a hotel room floor is part of the experience,* *Zuko wants that too.*

*The best bed of Zuko’s life, if he had to pick one objectively, was the memory foam* *mattress he had when he was twelve. It was one of the fancy ones with the cooling gel* *since Zuko’s always run hot, with a pillow topper over it and sheets soft enough they* *bunched and slipped like liquid whenever he moved, and more blankets than Zuko* *would ever know what to do with and enough pillows to drown in—or at least to* *immerse his whole body in.*

*But the best sleep? That’s always been harder to come by than fancy mattresses. But in* *the midst of all the happy chatter and giggling, Katara beginning to put on a dramatic* *re-enactment of her favorite backup dancer and Ty Lee and Aang jumping to their feet* *to join in and Sokka flashing him a triumphant smile and stepping back to admire his* *handiwork—Zuko has a feeling that tonight, if any of them manage to actually close* *their eyes, might be a contender.*

“…I wasn’t on the floor,” Sokka is muttering without any real heat. “There was a *mattress*—”

“On the floor.”

“—and you know what, we all can’t be as freakishly flexible as you,” Sokka finishes, dire, and Zuko can’t help but smirk and make a show of stretching out his shoulders and reaching for the ceiling, tempting more things than he cares to think about as he feels his sleep pants slide a dangerous inch or two downward with the motion and fuck, this is why Zuko *needs* that quiet time in the morning. So he can talk himself out of things like this.

“Show off,” Sokka huffs, giving him a pretend glare, and Zuko can't help but grin, smug, and stretch an extra inch, part of himself going breathless with something dangerously like anticipation, close enough that he finally lets his arms drop.

“You’re just jealous,” he says, skin burning from Sokka’s gaze, from the way being looked at with familiar envy can feel so much like being looked at with admiring eyes.

“I’m not the one just standing there watching the show,” Sokka says, plopping another box onto his tower.

And Zuko can’t exactly deny the watching or the show part, and he can feel the telling flush suddenly trying to crawl up his chest, so he ducks back into his room in lieu of coming up with an answer that won’t sound horrifically flirtatious, grabbing the first shirt on hand before darting back like maybe Sokka will disappear from the living room if Zuko isn’t fast enough.

“And he’s a thief on top of being a showoff,” Sokka murmurs as Zuko slips back into the living room, dragging the shirt over his head, and Zuko glances down and after a belated moment realizes that it must have originally been Sokka’s.

“Call it a trade,” Zuko says, as if it was ever anything so deliberate. “I’m pretty sure your pajamas started off as mine.” From a gift exchange, if he’s remembering correctly, his and Sokka’s gifts going into one pile and one bag and then to their one apartment almost as soon as they were unwrapped.

Sokka pauses, leaning back to give himself a considering look, torso flexed and one leg extended and Zuko isn’t going to look to, he isn’t, he isn’t, he is, fuck, today is going to be one of those kinds of days, isn’t it?

“Green looks better on me anyway,” Sokka finally shrugs, shooting Zuko one of his broad, winning smiles, and it's near instinctive for Zuko to raise his eyebrow back and find his driest tone.

“I wouldn’t know. Seeing as I never got a chance to find out.”

“You would if you were brave enough,” Sokka says, and Zuko’s mind immediately leaps to shoving Sokka the rest of the way down onto the floor, to stripping the thin flannel right off him. To the way those legs would settle on either side of Zuko’s hips, the way those thighs would feel in his hands as Zuko hauled Sokka closer, and fuck, fuck, *fuck—*

Zuko hurries to snatch up a box, body burning, and put himself to work.

He starts in the far corner of the room because he needs a bit of space and distance to breathe and to hide without retreating, to settle himself, and he knows that the easy work of packing up the trinkets along the windowsills is just the kind of distraction that will pull his focus to safer ground. And once he manages to get his head on straight, he finds that there’s something nice, something special, about being up so early with Sokka. They’re each focusing on their separate projects, not really talking—Sokka still isn’t awake enough for that, from what Zuko can tell, which begs the question of why he’s even out of bed—but they’re still sharing each other’s space, and something about the rarity of the occasion makes Zuko feel like he’s somehow stolen this time just for them.

He smiles over the random collection of things they’ve chosen to display on the windowsills over the years, trinkets and knickknacks and mementos organized in some haphazard melding of Zuko and Sokka’s preferences. Some of it makes sense, the kind of stuff anyone would display—the small painting Sokka picked up on a work trip to Phoenix, the fake plant that they’ve had for longer than Zuko can remember. A framed photo of Suki and her Kyoshi Warriors performing, Toph looking fierce and confident on drums. An old pocket watch from Hakoda hanging in a glass case and meticulously wound by Sokka every few months, surrounded by a decorative tea set in swirling reds and blues and purples that Uncle picked up in Japan because it made him think of them, whatever that means.

Other things are significantly less decorative but no less sentimental in their sideways way. The abstract statue from Katara that she said reminded her of Sokka—said after seeing Uncle’s tea set and delivered with *almost* a straight face, and Zuko absolutely can’t see it but continues to insist the resemblance is there just for the way it makes Sokka sputter. A tarot deck Ty Lee made that she gravely informed him matched his aura, which, considering the heavy use of black—and gold, Sokka always points out, but mostly black—Zuko has never known quite how to take. And the tiny little plastic carnival squirt gun that Zuko barely managed to win that Sokka’s diligently moved from apartment to apartment, finding a prominent place to display it each time. The stack of coasters from all of Zuko’s favorite brunch spots. The crocheted animal thing from Aang’s brief brush with needlework. Each thing with its own memories to tug a smile to Zuko’s lips, its own reasons to linger over and hold carefully before packing away.

“Don’t skimp on the bubble wrap,” Sokka comments as Zuko starts on the next window where a hand thrown…vessel has pride of place, his first unprompted words of the morning. Apparently, he’s ready to talk. “I wouldn’t want Azula to find you in the night and shave your eyebrow off because you broke the Pot of Self-Improvement.”

Zuko stills, eyes frozen on Azula’s first attempt at pottery as he swallows back the response that Azula would just glue the misshapen pot-mug-thing back together again if he broke it. There’s no way he could explain why she would consider that a fitting enough punishment all on its own. Not to Sokka.

***Four years, two months, and six days until the end***

*“And what about you, brother mine,” Azula says, her voice all familiar affected irony* *and tartness as she leans back to examine her pottery wheel with narrowed eyes, her* *clay-covered hands held carefully in front of her like she’s still skeptical of the whole* *endeavor even an hour into it. “Surely you can’t wish to hear more about the drudgery* *of business school.”*

*“I don’t know,” Zuko says, braving gentle pressure onto his spinning lump of clay and* *startling when it flattens out faster than he can react to into something significantly* *more plate-like than he intended. “It sounds like you’re having a great time.”* *Listening to her lambast every professor in her radius certainly doesn’t make it seem* *like* drudgery. *Not for Azula at least, no matter how much she’s scowling at him.*

*Zuko doesn’t take the glaring personally. She’s always in a touchy mood at these* *monthly classes of hers, pottery or painting or glass blowing or welding one* *memorable time, each only lasting a few sessions at most because the only consistent* *thing about them is that Azula is hopelessly bad at whatever it is—or hopelessly* *unable to tolerate being bad at it in public, depending on the instructor. But she keeps* *signing up for a class every month, working her way through the city and across crafts* *and hobbies, diligently trying to find something to replace martial arts—and a little* *light arson—as a way to ‘express her creative interests,’ as her therapist keeps saying.*

*Zuko’s observation that maybe she wasn’t very* artistically *creative had not been well* *received, the one time he pointed it out.*

*“You’re awfully quiet over there, Zuzu,” Azula says, prodding at her lump of clay.* *“You aren’t here just to show me up.”*

*Barely, but at least Zuko’s clay isn’t shearing off in bits and pieces. “There’s not much* *to say,” Zuko shrugs. “Work is work.”*

*Azula snorts. “That job is beneath you.”*

*Zuko gives her a sideways look. “There’s nothing wrong with being in compliance.”*

*“No, there isn’t.” Which is a relief to hear considering her prospective career path.* *Zuko would hate for another member of the family to be arrested for running a Ponzi* *scheme. Among other things. “But at* that *firm?”*

*Zuko grimaces. He unfortunately can’t disagree, but a job’s a job, and working on the* *audit side of things certainly beats his brief stint in financial sales. And, “Hey, it could* *still be that nonprofit I was with right out of undergrad.”*

*“Ugh,” Azula says, tsking in the back of her throat when her hand clenches in* *annoyance and sends her lump of clay listing sideways, somehow in two pieces. “That* *was such a* scam. *And stop distracting me,” she adds, apparently deciding to scrap the* *whole thing and start over as she tosses her no-longer-a-pot back into the bucket, nose* *wrinkling as she claws out a new lump. “You must have something going on in your* *life if you were unavailable last week like I initially proposed.”*

*“Oh,” Zuko says, blinking. “I—yeah. I had plans.”*

*“Yes,” she says patiently. “I gathered. That is what I am trying to ask about.” She* *makes a face as her new wet clay dropping onto her wheel sends a spatter of droplets* *across the front of her apron. Zuko has the feeling they will not be doing pottery next* *month. Considering the amount of time he's going to spend picking mud out of the* *rings he forgot to take off, he's fine with that. “So what were you and Kinalik up to this* *time? Another movie marathon? Trying to compare and rank every nacho in the city* *again?”*

*Zuko opens his mouth and hesitates, surprised to feel himself blushing slightly and* *wincing when Azula’s eyes immediately narrow on the spot of color on his cheek.*

*“What?” she presses, intent and momentarily ignoring her pot in favor of leaning to* *get a better look at his face. “Something else?”*

*“It’s not a big—”*

*“You’re blushing, Zuko, don’t act like you aren’t. Do you really want me to start* *listing off the reasons you blush until we see which one makes it worse?”*

*No, he doesn’t. “I had a date, actually,” Zuko says, sighing a little as he hears the* *words come out stiff despite his attempt to sound casual. “If you must know.”*

*“…A date with Sokka Kinalik?”*

*Zuko takes a careful breath and ignores the tendril of hopeless wanting at the idea just* *like he’s been ignoring it for months now. “No. With someone from the gym. He asked* *me out a time or two before,” Zuko finds himself explaining in the face of her flat* *stare. “And this time I didn’t have a reason to say no.”*

*“Didn’t have a reason,” Azula echoes.*

*“Nope,” Zuko agrees, making a show of focusing on turning his pot into something* *more pot-like.*

*“When before there was one.”*

*…Zuko walked himself into that one, didn’t he.*

*“Zuko.” He ignores her. “*Zuko,*” she repeats, sharp enough that he reluctantly gives* *her his attention, wincing at the incredulous look on her face. “You* cannot *be this* *blind and unaware,” she hisses, eyes narrowed in suspicion like she’s trying to find* *the joke. “You’re dating some random from the gym? When you’re in love with* *Kinalik?” Zuko flinches to hear it out loud. “I will allow for some degree of* *unawareness considering the ass-backward way you two have gone about it, but to not* know? *Zuko,* how*—”*

*“I know, okay?” Zuko cuts in, sharp, hard, forcing himself to take a deep breath* *before repeating, quieter, “I know.”*

*“…Do you now.”*

*“I’m not a total idiot,” he huffs, annoyed, though he can’t exactly refute her scoff* *considering his current situation. “And anyway, it doesn’t matter.”*

*“Doesn’t* mat—”

*“It’s not going to happen Azula.”*

*Her whole body stills for a long moment before she says low, tight, “He turned you* *down?”*

*“What?” Zuko straightens at the threat in her voice, blinking in surprise. “No, why do* *you..?”*

*“…Then he said yes,” she says, tight, the words clearly meant to be a question but her* *tone saying she already knows the answer and is hoping to be wrong.*

*Zuko sighs, rubbing the heel of his hand against his scar and remembering too late* *that he's covered in wet clay up to the elbows. “I didn’t ask, Azula. And you know I* *didn’t, so just—”*

*“Why* not? *Do you honestly, truly think he’d—”*

*“Because I don’t want to mess it up!” Zuko snaps, overly loud as heads suddenly jerk* *toward them in surprise, the instructor straightening in alarm and beginning to hurry* *toward them before Azula waves her off.*

*“Looks,” he says at a more normal volume, flexing his fist and focusing on the clay* *streaking up the lines of his tattoo and not the way his words are coming out too tight,* *too fast. “It’s just a crush, okay?”*

*She makes a skeptical noise that he isn’t going to bother responding to right now.*

*“Crushes come and go all the time, it’s not worth blowing things up over. Not just* *with him,” he adds quickly when she opens her mouth to interject. “With everything.* *Aang is married to Katara, if something went wrong—and Toph is as good a friend* *with him as with me, and Suki, and—and it’s not worth it. Okay? All of that is* *something special,” he says, clenching his hands together to keep the shake out of his* *voice and aware that he’s revealing himself in more ways than one with this, “And I’m* *not going to be the one to mess it up.”*

*“And so,” she says after a long, agonizing pause, “You are going to date someone* *else.”*

*“Yes.”*

*“To try to get over the person you never dated in the first place.”*

*“Because I* want *to date him,” Zuko glares. Because Zuko* is *dating him. They already* *went on a first date, which was perfectly fine, and they have plans for a second and* *it’s* happening*.*

*“Which is why you accepted all the prior times he asked. Because of how much you* *want to date him.”*

*Zuko grits his teeth, feeling something ugly and full of spines trying to take root in his* *chest. “Focus on your pot,” he snaps. “It’s going sideways again.” Which is enough* *to distract Azula for the rest of the night, apparently, even though Zuko knows is only* *because she decided to let him have his retreat. Which he doesn’t care about because* *it’s not* her *he needs to prove anything to.*

*Not that he needs to prove anything to anyone, that is.*

“What’s she on now again?” Sokka is saying, voice relaxed and casual behind him. “Wreath making or something?”

“Candle making,” Zuko says, automatic. “She ditched the wreaths two months ago.”

Sokka hums. “All that fire? What, is she trying to test her self-control?”

Always, but, “She has other people light them for her to test them.”

“That is not weird at all,” Sokka says, amused, and Zuko suddenly, desperately, needs to feel a shred of that amusement. Needs to shake off the memories that cling to the Pot of Self-Improvement and think about something else, anything else.

“Hey,” he says, turning to Sokka, “What are you doing?” The cardboard sounds stopped a while ago, Zuko realizes, and he's abruptly too restless, too tight, too *everything* to keep going with this. “Do you need help?”

“Sorting video games,” Sokka says, holding up a case. “Remember this?”

***Nine years, six months, and twenty-eight days until the end***

*“No! How did that—”*

*“Suck it, you—”*

*“Bullshit!” Sokka shouts, dropping his controller to give Zuko a shove. “That was* *total bullshit!”*

*Zuko just smirks, bracing his core and barely rocking with the push as his character’s* *win-music begins to play. “Oh, I see, so it’s bullshit when* I *edge guard, but when* you —”

*“It’s not about edge guarding,” Sokka protests, throwing his hands wide in outrage.* *“You didn’t even use your own move!”*

*“What I’m hearing is, ‘I only care about items being toggled off when I get hurt by* *them.’”*

*“*I *was the* Pokemon trainer*, you killed me with a* pokeball!”

*“That does suck for you, doesn’t it?” Zuko smirks, earning a squawk. “There’s some* *kind of irony about that.”*

*“You did that on purpose,” Sokka accuses, and Zuko just grins, putting everything he* *has into making it as smug as possible, the kind of look that Azula used to shove him* *into fountains over, his pulse fluttering a little at being able to tease so easily with* *Sokka, at not second-guessing and hesitating in the face of Sokka’s performative* *outrage. It still feels a little bit daring, a little bit exciting, bold in a way he’d never* *admit out loud, to have something like this with a roommate*

*Sokka shoves him again, huffing, and Zuko actually lets out a laugh as he allows* *himself to actually get pushed over this time.*

*“You won’t do the level randomizer when it’s your turn,” Sokka complains. “You only* *play the same like, two characters when the* fun *is in* switching—”

*“The fun is in winning,” Zuko says serenely from his position on the floor and* *thoroughly in the lead.*

*Sokka pauses, eyeing him. “You’re keeping score, aren’t you.”*

*“Of course.” Wins, win differentials, overall damage dealt and received. Keeping the* *Jasmine Dragon’s books all through high school was worth something, after all.*

*“…You’re winning, aren’t you.”*

*“Of course,” Zuko grins, getting his foot up just in time to catch Sokka’s lunge,* *Sokka’s fingers curled in what Zuko quickly learned is tickling form, and it descends* *from there into the kind of wrestling that would have Katara rolling her eyes and* *calling them both children if she were here to see it.*

*“I bet I can win,” Sokka pants once they finally lose steam, splayed out on the rug* *next to Zuko.*

*“What, over the rest of the school year?” Zuko scoffs, delighting in the way he can* *push back without fearing any sharp edges. “It’s definitely not going to be tonight.”*

*Sokka makes a noise of outrage, but he’s also laughing as he does it and Zuko curls* *his fingers over the edge of the rug like he needs to anchor himself, to remind himself* *that this is real, to hold onto the fact something like this can actually be* fun. *Zuko is* *years out from living with Father, has nearly as many years of therapy under his belt,* *but still, the fact that he doesn’t have to actively remind himself that this is supposed to* *be just a game, the fact that Sokka takes his bursts of hyper-competitiveness in stride—* *Zuko doesn’t know if he could have imagined something like this when he agreed to* *live with Sokka, even being friends already, and it’s…nice.*

*“Okay, how about this,” Sokka says, rolling onto his side to face Zuko, huffing to blow* *the loose hair out of his face. “My laundry is up in thirty-seven minutes—”*

*“Fifty-seven. Those dryers are awful.”*

*“—and if I win the most games from then until now, you go get it for me.* And *fold it.”*

*Zuko stares, wondering if he’s missing something. “…Why would I do that?”*

*“Because I’m in my PJs and slippies?” Sokka throws up a leg to wiggle his fuzzy-sock-clad toes in demonstration, his face melting into puppy dog eyes as if Zuko* *doesn’t know exactly how he uses them. “And it's far away and cold?”*

*“It’s like sixty-five degrees out.” Sokka spent his childhood in* Alaska.

*“Brr,” Sokka says, shivering dramatically, lips twitching. “So cold.”*

*Zuko is just…going to not argue that point, because, “You still haven’t answered why* *I’d get your laundry for you.”*

*“Because I’m going to win,” Sokka grins, immediately dropping his begging voice.*

*“…I could just not do it, though.”*

*“Yeah,” Sokka scoffs. “If you want to renege on our bet!”*

*“That I haven’t even accepted yet? And how is it even a bet? What would I get if I* *win?” Oh, shit. He’s bargaining. He’s engaging, and years with Azula should have* *taught him that—*

*“Bragging rights,” Sokka says right away, proving that he knows Zuko too well.*

*“You’re not going to win anyway,” Zuko points out, slow, eyes narrowed as he looks* *for the trick in it all. “It’s a dumb bet to offer.”*

*“Then it shouldn’t be a problem to accept it,” Sokka says angelically, with a butter-wouldn’t-melt-in-my-mouth smile and a fucking* wink *and shit, Zuko* knows *he’s being* *played, he knows it even if he hasn’t figured out how.*

*But he still sits up and grabs his controller, something halfway between delight at the* *challenge and affront that his skills are being questioned fizzing in his chest.*

*“Fine,” he says, toggling into the game settings and ignoring Sokka’s whoop of* *triumph. “And I’ll do it with the items off*, *too.”*

*“Oh, it’s like that, is it?” Sokka grins, scrambling for his own kicked-aside controller.* *“I’d like to see you try.”*

“Is that—”

“It is,” Sokka grins, holding it out as Zuko comes over. “Almost like new, even.”

“Shit,” Zuko says as he kneels down beside Sokka, cracking open the case and seeing the disk still shiny and barely-scratched inside, a feat considering they used to play every day all through school, eventually branching into other games but always coming back to this.

He doesn’t know how he feels about the fact that he can’t quite remember when they stopped…

“Do we own a Wii remote that works anymore?” he asks instead of dwelling on it, glancing around like one might magically appear.

“Not after the Second Baseball Incident,” Sokka says ruefully. “Though there might be some extras in my dad’s basement.”

“Maybe for the best,” Zuko says, easily batting aside the flare of regret as another case catches his eyes. It’s not like they have time for things like playing games, anyway, no matter how fun it would be. “Remember when Suki lost it over *Mario Kart*?”

“Rainbow Road is her nemesis,” Sokka winces. “Katara maybe took the gloating a bit far.”

Zuko…is aware that his childhood didn’t exactly give him a normal barometer for appropriate levels of gloat, so he nudges through the piles, grabbing another case instead. “Remember the *Mario Party* tournament?”

“Jeez, that took forever,” Sokka laughs. “What did we think it would take, a week?”

“Something like that.” Zuko is still unsure how they so egregiously miscalculated. The bracket-style tournament ended up spanning the better part of the semester. “In retrospect, I don’t know why we even bothered.”

“Hope,” Sokka says, lips quirked. “Statistically, Aang couldn’t win *all* the time.”

“That’s what you said before the First Baseball Incident, too,” Zuko points out, biting back a smile. They were on a months-long *Wii Sports* kick that ended with a broken window, a night spent searching the lawn for a controller they never found, and Aang once again cheerfully triumphant.

“He’s freakishly good at that kind of stuff,” Sokka mutters, and Zuko wonders if always losing was part of the reason he and Sokka slowly transitioned over to the fighter games Aang didn’t have an interest in. But still the kinds of games where they could work as a team or against each other, more nights than he can count in their first apartment spent side by side on the couch or sprawled across the floor, controllers in hand, and Zuko has a video he knows he will never delete no matter what of Sokka lying on his stomach, eyes intent on the TV screen, tongue caught between his teeth and heels slowly kicking in concentration.

“Hey,” Sokka says, the grin in his voice tugging Zuko off the edge of memory. “Remember this one?”

There are more games stacked before them than Zuko realized they had, some easier to recall playing than others. Sorting through the cases is likely slowly stepping back in time, through releases and editions and birthdays and holidays and one never-to-be-repeated midnight release until they start reaching games that Zuko’s never played before, and ones that Sokka’s never heard of but Zuko used to cherish as some of the first things Uncle ever gave him that truly felt like they were *his*.

“I guess this goes with all the rest I can’t remember,” Sokka says, shaking his head over an inexplicable GameBoy Advance cartridge when neither of them can remember owning one past middle school. “We can figure it out later. Call dibs or something.”

“What?” Zuko blinks, glancing around and feeling like he's missing something and slowly realizing that all the games have been grouped into three distinct piles.

“Yours, mine, and who knows,” Sokka explains, nodding to each pile in question. “Want to take a stab at it?” And it takes another bit of staring for Zuko to fully process Sokka’s meaning: that the piles aren’t just about packing or donating, they're about *separating*. And then right on its heels the realization like a punch to the solar plexus, leaving him gasping and lightheaded, that Zuko just packed everything on the windowsills as if the contents were still *theirs.* When this time it’s one or the other. Sokka’s *or* Zuko’s. No longer both.

“Let me get coffee first,” Zuko blurts, scrambling to his feet and ignoring the way Sokka’s eyebrows shoot up at his messy rush. He doesn’t think he can deal with Sokka’s surprise right now, doesn’t think he can deal with having this realization in real-time in front of him. And fuck, he suddenly needs those few moments of quiet he usually has in the morning, needs to fortify himself for the rest of this day, needs to do it with something bitter and harsh and dark that would make Uncle frown in disappointment, because otherwise Zuko thinks he might cry over *Mario* *Galaxy* and there’s no way he can explain that.

“Alright, sure,” Sokka says after a beat, tugging uncertainly on his stack of bracelets and sounding vaguely startled but staying in the scatter of game cases in Zuko’s wake, which is all Zuko needs right now as he goes to grab his keys and the first pair of shoes at hand—a pair of Sokka’s flip flops, of fucking *course.* But fuck it, he doesn’t want to risk the time to find something else and it’s not a long walk even if it’s cold. And maybe the bracing reminder that a world exists outside this apartment—outside this morning—will help him get his head on straight because how could he *forget*. “Will you get me—”

“A toasted coconut mocha with whole milk and extra coconut,” Zuko choruses along with Sokka, because Sokka orders whatever the sugariest thing currently available on the menu is every time.

“That’s the one,” Sokka grins, turning back to re-sorting the pile of games. “Thanks, hot stuff.”

And Zuko takes a deep breath and exhales long and hard instead of replying, and reminds himself as the door swings shut behind him that there’s a reason why he’s leaving.

&#45;-

***Two days and twenty hours until the end***

The ottoman might be a bit worn and stained after ten years—or nearly ten years, rather, because it hasn’t been ten years yet and maybe it never will be now, and then Sokka immediately feels silly because it’s not like time will stop passing just because he won’t be there to see it—but it’s still soft and luxurious, still brings the room together exactly like Sokka envisioned all those years ago in Ikea when they first got it. And it turns out it can store even more than Sokka ever thought could fit inside it.

It’s one thing to know that storage space was Zuko’s primary requirement—which Sokka had happily agreed to, because what apartment doesn’t need a general dumping ground for all the stuff that doesn’t really have a place anywhere else? It’s another thing to actually have to sort through the dumping ground instead of just shoveling it all into a bag and then back into the ottoman on the other side of the move…

“Did you keep every electric bill we’ve ever had?” Sokka asks, incredulous, as he pulls out envelope after envelope after envelope, some of them not even opened.

“You never know when we’ll need one,” Zuko shrugs. Which somehow just draws Sokka’s attention again to the fact that Zuko is still wearing the t-shirt he pulled on this morning, the one that Sokka is almost entirely certain is one of his. A hazard of Zuko doing most of the laundry, he supposes. And one he won’t have to deal with anymore, the reminder bitter on the back of his tongue.

“You realize these are online,” Sokka says, shoving those thoughts firmly away. The day has been fine enough so far and there are so few of them with Zuko left. Sokka can have his breakdown or whatever this is trying to be later, when there’s no time to waste having it.

Zuko hums. “The internet goes down.”

“…This is from four apartments ago.”

“Gotta compare typical monthly usage over time. The only way to know if you’re leaving too many lights on.”

“If *I’m—”* Sokka pulls himself up in outrage which apparently is all that’s needed for Zuko to break, laughter bursting out of him as he ducks his head, stealing a sideways look at Sokka’s face that only makes him laugh harder, lips curved into an open, wide smile and for a moment all Sokka can do is stare at it, breath caught.

Then he scrunches up his face and lunges over to give Zuko a shove. “You little—”

“Oh, I’m sorry, who’s taller here?” Zuko giggles, shoving back.

“I’ll show you taller—”

They wrestle briefly for leverage, more slap-fighting than anything else, even that quickly devolving into throwing old electrical bills at each other like some parody of a snowball fight. A very ineffective parody, though, because paper—at least until Sokka balls one up and bounces it off Zuko’s face. Because aimable ammunition means dodging, which means diving behind furniture and darting around boxes and chasing each other around the living room, Zuko jumping over obstacles and Sokka skidding in his socked feet and both of them calling half-breathles taunts and mocking each other’s accuracy. And Sokka might not be quite sure how they end up transitioning away from paper balls and to paper airplanes, and then from frantic laughing folding on the move to trying to one-up each other’s folding skills using their foreheads as targets, but all in all, it’s a rather typical late morning, really, the kind of determined silliness that so often fills the time when they don’t have anything much to do with their day—except they do, they do, but fuck if Sokka is going to be the one to bring it up.

Though the knowledge is definitely affecting him even if he’s refusing to say it. His aim is significantly worse than usual, his paper planes hitting the worn fabric of his shirt on Zuko more often than not. It’s like he can’t seem to keep his eyes focused on anything else, though, with the way his hand keeps hitching in weird ways when he tries to look at his target between Zuko’s laughing eyes instead. And somehow Zuko’s good-natured mocking isn't so much goading Sokka into the competitiveness he needs to actually concentrate as making him want to just draw this whole thing out, missing again and again, to keep seeing the smile on Zuko’s face.

Which...Sokka doesn’t quite know what to do with. But he’s just going to add it to the list of things to figure out later because god, watching Zuko finally turn back to the ottoman when they run out of paper, Sokka can already feel how he isn’t going to be able to look at an electric bill ever again without remembering his moment. He can already see a glimmer of how his days are going to stay full of Zuko long past when Zuko leaves and he doesn’t have space to think about things like why he so desperately needs Zuko’s last days here to be happy ones when the awareness that Zuko is going to leave but he won't be *gone,* not for Sokka, is hitting him with such a gasping bolt of grief.

“Are these *nametags*?” Zuko asks, laughter still in his voice as he roots through the ottoman, unaware of the way Sokka is struggling for air behind him, of the way Sokka latches onto that laughter, that normalcy, sucking in a sharp, little breath and forcing himself back into the moment and out of his own head. “There’s like *forty* of them,” Zuko is saying, shoving things aside in the ottoman and pulling out one after another aftter another. “What the hell, Sokka, did you forget your own name?”

“I go to like, four conferences a year!” Sokka defends himself, finding his footing again in letting himself be the butt of a joke and playing up his outrage to smooth over a moment and fuck, Sokka hasn’t consciously done this since…Katara graduated undergrad? Earlier? “And all those networking events they throw at work, and the parties—”

“So you kept them all?” Zuko asks, incredulous as he brandishes a nametag. “You went to Seattle like six years ago!”

Five years, actually, but, “They’re nice!”

“They’re *nametags.”*

“It’s nice paper, and good holders, some of them have lanyards,” Sokka rattles off, making a show of counting off his points, playing it up for the amusement on Zuko’s face. “What if I need one some day?”

“Yeah, one. *One.*” Zuko pulls out another fistful, which…Sokka can allow does feel like a lot, actually, seeing them all together now.

“Okay,” Sokka says, dropping down next to the ottoman again so he can dig through the clutter, feeling something adrenaline-filled and bright taking hold in his chest and burning through the lingering weirdness of before. Where is—there. “But am I the one who owns both *The Complete* *Book of Houseplants* and *The Compete Houseplant Survival Manual?”*

“Those are dif—”

“And *A Guide to Keeping Happy House Plants*—”

“Okay, well that’s a subtitle.”

“—and *The Indoor Garden Book. Indoor Gardening the Organic Way. Gardening Indoors. Indoor* *Gardening Growing Air Pl—”*

Zuko catches Sokka’s eye and reaches into the ottoman to silently and pointedly hold up a single paperback book, one that Sokka doesn’t even need to see to know the title of, the memory of unwrapping it seared into his brain.

“That was a joke!” Sokka immediately protests, feeling himself blushing hot. “I was *given it* as a *joke*.”

“And yet here it is, years later,” Zuko says, eyes gleaming wicked. “*Buttageddon: The Final Days* *of Pounding Ass*.”

“It was a *gift*.”

“Yes,” Zuko says, all polite thoughtfulness in that sarcastic way of his. “All my girlfriends give me books about being pounded in the ass, too.”

Sokka glares and snatches the book. “You know this is by a Hugo-nominated author,” he grumbles, feeling his flush extending through his entire body at hearing those words in Zuko’s low, raspy voice, something about it making his gut warm with embarrassment.

“Hm. Know what else is nominated for a Hugo award?” Zuko asks, still all polite inquiry, and Sokka rolls his eyes and throws a gardening book at him, laughing, his whole body tingling from the combination of teasing and embarrassment as he reaches into the ottoman—it’s like a game, at this point—and pulls out—

“…Is this a stack of Dubai-branded to-do lists?”

“Um.” Zuko kneels up to see better, blinking. “Yes?”

“Where…” Sokka doesn’t even know where to start.

“…Azula?”

“Why…”

“I don’t know,” Zuko admits, scratching the back of his neck. “From one of her trips, I guess? I think during school?”

Sokka silently pulls out a matching brand magnet grocery organizer, a notebook, post-its, bookmarks—

“…I don’t know why we have any of that,” Zuko admits.

“So your commitment to culling unnecessary clutter during moves doesn’t extend to the living room, we’re learning,” Sokka says, carefully setting the stack of office supplies by Zuko's pile. He has a feeling this is going to turn out to be one of Azula’s weird pranks that make sense only to her.

Zuko huffs, giving Sokka a sideways look, then reaches into the ottoman again—oh, it really is a game now—digging a moment before he pulls out…five identical programs from a baseball game they went to years ago.

Sokka immediately grabs them out of his hand, pointedly ignoring Zuko’s raised eyebrow as he presses them carefully flat and examines the edges and corners. “It’s not like I bought them all,” he mutters as he sets the best one aside and makes a show of putting the others into the ‘to throw away’ pile growing next to the couch.

“You did, actually.”

“For everyone else!” One for him, and one each for Katara and Aang and Bato and his dad!

“Yet here they all are.”

“Like you don’t have yours tucked away in some memory box somewhere,” Sokka rolls his eyes, glancing over when Zuko is unexpectedly quiet and blinking in surprised pleasure to see Zuko flushed unexpectedly pink.

“It was a good game,” Zuko says, clearing his throat and not quite meeting Sokka's eyes almost like he’s been caught out in something, which…Sokka has *five* of them tucked away, why would Zuko…

“It was,” Sokka agrees after a beat, wondering why his confusion suddenly makes it feel so difficult to make the words come out level. “A really good game.”

“A good day,” Zuko adds, head ducking as he sorts through the Dubai office supplies again, and the difficulty does *not* go away because why—it almost feels like Zuko is—is Sokka missing something here?

“Yeah. A great one,” Sokka says after a beat, feeling an unexpected bolt of shyness too, which he doesn’t—why—the moment extending out long and weird and not *bad*, just…since when are they *shy* with each other?

Sokka finally turns back to the ottoman, feeling strangely awkward and fumbling as he straightens the stack of bracelets and hair ties on his wrist before peeking into the barely-organized mess inside, looking for an opportunity to find the fun, joking mood again, the one he feels like he’s been chasing after for months. Not more bills, not the pile of roadmaps from Bato, not—*that.*

“Oh, hotman,” Sokka crows, delighted, and digging out his find. “You want to talk about holding onto things?”

Zuko gives him a wary, sideways look. “I think that’s been the whole conversation.”

Sokka just raises his eyebrows and triumphantly brandishes a set of furniture assembly instructions. Then another. Then a third. Then a fourth.

“Oh,” Zuko scoffs, flicking Sokka a rude gesture that really is well-framed by his rings, “*Apologies.* Not everyone can just look at a pile of chair parts and figure out how to put it together.”

“Think there are six different ways?” Sokka asks, throwing down six identical instruction packets for their six identical chairs. “Got one of those in your memory box, too? Just in case they have a choose your own adventure option?”

“You never know when you might need them for *this*,” Zuko says, suddenly snatching up a packet and swinging it toward Sokka’s face, Sokka ducking back with a startled laugh, shielding himself and fumbling for his own instruction set and its as easy as breathing from there to fall into tussling and wrestling again, this time with an impromptu paper swordfight. Paper is the theme to the morning, apparently, and Sokka is happy to stick to it if it leads to things like this, his exhaustion barely a memory as they strain for the upper hand against each other, breathless and giggling and grappling and throwing their weight at each other, the lightness of it all lingering even after they finally settle and straighten their clothes and their hair and get back to what they’re supposed to be doing.

“What is this,” Zuko asks, voice still full of laughter as he leans into the ottoman, which...really has a *lot* of storage, “Old renter’s insurance? And old leases? Did you keep all of these?”

Sokka shrugs. “You never know when you’re gonna want to compare rates.”

Zuko snorts, giving him an unimpressed look that’s ruined by the twitch of his lips. “And you were giving me shit about the electric bills? I’m pretty sure they don’t let you compare rates across time.”

“Hey,” Sokka says, his chest already fluttering with preemptive victory. “Remember when there was that class action lawsuit against your health insurance?”

Zuko groans and Sokka lets his grin spread across his face.

“But you’d tossed all your old membership cards and letters and deleted your emails and couldn’t remember your passwords?”

“Don’t remind me,” Zuko mutters, pressing the heels of his hands to his eyes.

“This way,” Sokka says as he plucks the papers from between Zuko’s fingers, “We’ll always know.”

“I don’t suppose I can say that applies to the electric bills,” Zuko sighs, reaching back into the ottoman.

“No,” Sokka says, smug and warm with amusement. “You can’t.”

“I’m surprised you don’t have a whole section in here for them if that’s how you’re thinking,” Zuko mutters, and Sokka glances up, his heart suddenly dropping so fast he feels dizzy with it as he stares at the scuffed blue plastic of a familiar binder in Zuko’s hands. His apartment hunting binder.

***Three months and twenty-five days until the end***

*“…but I was thinking it might be more important to make sure I’m near a good* *grocery store and gym,” Zuko is saying, back to Sokka at the kitchen table as he chops* *onions. He said he didn’t need Sokka’s help as anything but a taste tester for his* *work’s chili cookoff—something about the ethics of not making his own entry—but* *fuck does Sokka feel restless without something to do, staring almost hypnotically at* *the flash of Zuko’s knife, quick and sure in Zuko’s relaxed, confident grip, his tattoo* *almost seeming alive as it shifts and flexes over Zuko's arm and god, Sokka wishes he* *could take a knife to something too, with an intensity that leaves him nearly nauseous.*

*…Maybe for the best he doesn’t have a sharp blade right now.*

*“…because the commute matters, right?” Zuko is explaining with the familiar* *cadence of testing an idea by saying it out loud. “But if the gym becomes a whole* *commute, I might end up skipping it.”*

*As if Zuko has ever let himself skimp on a workout in his life, and Sokka sucks in a* *shaky breath as he stares at the breadth of Zuko’s shoulders, the V of Zuko’s back, at* *his strong legs. The idea that Zuko might stop working out in this new life, that he’d no* *longer match the Zuko that Sokka will picture in his memories and daydreams—*

*“Isn’t there a gym on your fancy new campus?” Sokka asks, grabbing up his glass of* *water as the words scrape their way out of his throat. The heating has been pumping* *all day and man does it dry him out.*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says, flashing Sokka a quick little smile over his shoulder and Sokka* *coughs a little as the water doesn’t go down quite right. “But the good one looks like* *it’ll be a twenty-minute walk from all my classes. So if I can find one closer to my* *apartment…”*

*“Got it.” Sokka clenches his hands around his water glass to hold back another* *cough. “Makes sense,” he forces himself to add, somehow summoning up a smile* *when the bigger part of him wants to scream because there’s so little time left, so little* *time, and then Zuko won’t be in the kitchen when Sokka wanders in anymore. Or won’t* *be in* his *kitchen. Won’t be smiling at him like that or wrinkling his face up all* *skeptical and cute as he reads through the ingredients list or next step of his recipe, or* *pulling his hair back into one of his messy, absent buns, and—and—*

*“Plus all those undergrads,” Zuko adds, and Sokka can picture the face he’s making* *just by the tone of his voice. “Can you imagine trying to share a squat rack with frat* *bros again? No thank you,” he laughs.*

*“You might be doing that anyway in a college town,” Sokka points out, painfully* *aware that he’d* know *whether Zuko would be doing it during any other move. That* *he’d know it for any of his friends’ moves. Sokka loves this kind of thing—figuring out* *new cities and combing listings and hunting down that magical spot that has it all* *within the budget. He was the one to find Katara and Aang’s current apartment, the* *one who helped Suki find a new place in a matter of days after a breakup. Hell, he still* *knows more than he ever needed to about the housing in Rome because of Toph’s* *semester abroad. He helps all his friends with this all the time. He likes it. And* *normally he throws himself into it because deadlines matter with things like apartment* *hunting.*

*But this time? When it should theoretically be easiest because Sokka probably knows* *more about Zuko’s living preferences than his own? It’s like he can’t make himself* *even type Boston into a search bar. He’s used to the challenge of it filling him with* *excitement, some combination of the hunt and learning about someplace new, or even* *just a new corner of a place he already knows, and he doesn’t know what to do with* *the fact that this time all he seems to feel is something like dread, somethign horribly* *angry and painful and unsettling close to how it used to be every time Hakoda had to* *pack another bag even though Sokka* knows *he has no right to be anything but happy* *for Zuko.*

*“…avoid the student housing areas, you know?” Zuko says, and Sokka forcibly drags* *himself back into the conversation when everything in him just wants to fold over, to* *hunch in, to protect the soft core of himself that Sokka didn’t even realize was* *vulnerable until it got hit. “I’m not exactly looking to relieve the glory days,” he says,* *self-deprecating and shooting Sokka a wry little smile that Sokka needs to actively* *work to return because it’s suddenly occurring to him that if Zuko hates it enough…* *maybe he’ll come back. That maybe he’ll go and it will be so awful, he’ll be so* *homesick, that he’ll come back and—and transfer to an online program, because* *Sokka can’t see him going back to his compliance job. And Sokka could find a place* *for them again, with two bedrooms and an office and good light, something clean but* *cozy, with character and legal roof access. The kind of place that would make coming* *back totally worth it. The kind of place that would make Zuko never question his* *decision to put Boston behind him, and—and—*

*And Sokka can’t wish that on Zuko. He can’t wish for Zuko to have such an awful time* *that he walks away from a scholarship and his top choice school and this new life he* *seems to want for himself. Zuko will barely agree to go to a different grocery store* *when their usual one is packed to the brim, he knows far too well just how much Zuko* *is capable of enduring before he would ever actually quit something like that, and* *fuck, Sokka’s stomach is turning that his mind even went there, even for just a second.*

*Sokka could never let Zuko end up in a bad spot, and some self-loathing knot in his gut* *loosens as the truth of that resonates through him. Not when the mere idea of Zuko in* *some dump, some crappy neighborhood, some place where it’s an inconvenience to go* *grocery shopping and where the street noise will keep him up at night and where there* *won’t be enough light for all his plants, not when just the thought of it makes Sokka’s* *chest hurt for him. The idea of Zuko having a wonderful time in his new life without* *Sokka makes him feel like he needs to lie down, makes him have to grip the table* *against another of those rushes of feeling he can barely untangle except that they* *leave him gasping and struggling and bereft for something that’s still in front of him.*

*But the idea of Zuko having a* bad *time…*

*“Have you thought about radiator placement?” Sokka asks, grounding himself on the* *fact that Zuko is here now. Right in front of him and moving about the kitchen almost* *like he’s in a dance, so graceful—at least until something starts to go wrong—that* *Sokka feels like he could look at him forever. “You mentioned it a few apartments* *ago,” he adds when Zuko shoots him a questioning look. “How the radiator in the* *bathroom made showers awful in the winter. It sounds like you might be looking at* *older buildings. Could be worth thinking about again.”*

*“Good idea,” Zuko says, and Sokka hates the note of surprised pleasure he can hear* *in his voice even as he clings to it like a lifetime. That Zuko would be surprised…* *“Thanks.”*

*“Here,” Sokka says, getting up through sheer willpower and forcing out the words,* *“Let me get the apartment binder.” Sokka hurled it somewhere recently in a fit of…* *something, then shoved it away so he wouldn’t have to look at it anymore, but he’s* *pretty sure he knows where it is. “I can see what notes we wrote down from last time.”* *What things will make Zuko happy in this new life on his own.*

*Sokka certainly can’t face the idea of his own apartment hunt right now—the idea of* *looking for a one-bedroom for the first time makes him want to do literally anything* *else. But Zuko’s—he can do whatever he can to make sure Zuko ends up in a good* *spot. Because if he does a good enough job…maybe Zuko will stay there all through* *law school. Maybe even after, too. In a place that they found together, even if they* *don’t live that way anymore.*

“I guess you’ll be needing this pretty soon, right?” Zuko says, lips curled in a fond little smile as he flips through the careful sections of notes and printed-out listings and politely accepted but never used applications, through years of evolving wants and must-haves and never-agains. “Unless you want to help Aang and Katara with the baby forever,” he adds, teasing, and Sokka seizes onto that mood with everything he has, onto the flash of a lightness and ease he doesn’t feel between them anymore but god, does he want to.

“I’m very committed to my impending unclehood,” Sokka manages to say, the flash of Zuko’s grin letting him know he hit the right notes, the right tone, and if he can just keep doing that…He needs to keep things joking, goofy, because he doesn’t know if he can handle the tension of Zuko’s shoulders and the unhappy tightness around his eyes otherwise. He needs to make light of it all because if he doesn’t, he thinks he might hurl that binder through the window. So Sokka seizes on every bit of the ease Zuko is offering as he starts to tease Sokka about whether he or Aang is more nervous for the baby, something in him embarrassingly grateful to not have to do anything but be the object of Zuko’s playfulness until Sokka can manage to stop feeling so…

So much like this.

&#45;-

***Two days and seventeen hours until the end***

Zuko lets out a grunt as he leans to snag the last scallion pancake from the container by the coffee table and feels everything in his back briefly do terribly, awful things, the sound turning into more of a groan as he straightens. Fuck, he should know better than to sit so long hunched over on the floor like this, but it’s one of those things he never quite seems to learn until it’s too late again.

“Doing okay over there?” Sokka asks, eyes sharp and words muffled around a mouthful of sesame chicken. “Is your shoulder bothering you again?”

“Just my whole back and body,” Zuko huffs, twisting and feeling things pop and snap into place all down his back. “I don’t know how you sit like this all day.”

“I use chairs.”

“You sit even more like a gremlin when you have a seat,” Zuko mutters, lacing his fingers over his head to try to decompress every bit of his spine.

Sokka rolls his eyes but doesn’t deny it. “Here,” he says instead, hair swinging to kiss his jaw as he glances over his shoulder. “I don’t think we packed the foam roller yet.”

***Six years and thirteen days until the end***

*“I thought intramural sports were supposed to be casual,” Zuko complains, ignoring* *the way Sokka snorts from the other room. “It’s kickball! Do you know how* *embarrassing it is to tell people I tweaked my shoulder playing a children’s game?”*

*“You’re all adults playing it,” Aang points out, ever reasonable.*

*“And you could just make up a better story,” Sokka calls. “Say you saved someone* *from falling off a balcony or something.”*

*“I’m pretty sure that would dislocate my shoulder, not tweak it,” Zuko huffs, reluctant* *amusement sparking in his chest as he tries to stretch out the persistent ache streaking* *down his arm and back.*

*“Maybe you’ve got very strong shoulder muscles.”*

*“Maybe your very strong shoulder muscles need a* rest*,” Aang cuts in, familiar* *enough with Zuko and Sokka’s sidebars to know to ignore them. “If you don’t ever* *take a break, it won’t get better.”*

*“I do!” Zuko protests. “I wait and I ice it and it feels better—”*

*“And you hop right back in and immediately tweak it again because actually, it needed* *another week?”*

*Zuko sighs and flicks his hand to concede the point. It’s not like he can exactly deny it.* *It’s just so hard to hold himself back when he’s feeling good. He’s never been good at* *being still, not without something to focus on.*

*“Maybe you need a massage,” Sokka says, finally coming into the living room, pulling* *a t-shirt over his head—Zuko’s t-shirt, actually, shit, he must have mixed up their* *laundry again—and already looking more relaxed just by getting out of his work* *clothes. “Suki used to do this thing—”*

*“I do* not *want to know,” Zuko immediately says as Aang lets out an ‘eep’ and covers* *his ears, eyes comically wide.*

*“Not like* that*,” Sokka says, exasperated and brushing his hair up into a new hair tie,* *the newest piercing along his ear glinting gold amidst all the other silver. “You know* *my knee bothers me sometimes, she used to do this—thing.”*

*Zuko’s sure she did a lot of things. “My knee isn’t hurting,” he says, wary.*

*“A joint’s a joint,” Sokka shrugs as if he’s the medical professional in the family.* *“Can’t hurt to try.”*

*“…I feel like actually, it could hurt quite a bit,” Aang says, and Zuko makes a noise of* *agreement as he remembers the time Azula got a bad massage and couldn’t look to the* *left for a week until Ty Lee managed to pin her down and pressure point it out of her.*

*“You think I’ll hurt you, hotman?” Sokka asks, raising his eyebrows and catching* *Zuko’s eyes, and Zuko finds himself straightening without even wholly realizing why* *he’s doing so as he meets Sokka’s level gaze. “That I wouldn’t be careful?”*

*“No,” Zuko says after a beat, clearing his throat as he hears that one word come out* *more serious than he meant it to. But he’s unable to think of anything else to say as a* *follow-up, still weirdly caught by the steady blue of Sokka’s eyes, so he just clears his* *throat again and shifts up to sit on the arm of the couch and give Sokka his back.*

*“The right one?” Sokka asks, voice suddenly right behind him, close enough that Zuko* *can feel the heat of his body and feel the hovering barely-there pressure of his hand.*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says, not quite sure why looking at the wall suddenly seems so much* *more preferable right now than meeting Aang’s eyes. “Right abou—*fuuuuck.”

*“*Shit*,” Sokka says, yanking his hands away as Aang sits up in alarm. “That hurt?* *Your tattoo is supposed to be healed, but—*”

*“No,” Zuko gasps, wondering if he’s actually swaying because he feels momentarily* *dizzy from how* good *that half-second of tension releasing all down his shoulder felt.* *“It was good. Really, really good,” he adds Sokka’s hands don’t come back, and* *Sokka makes a pleased little noise and proceeds to go at it with abandon.*

*“What kind of stretching are you doing?” Aang asks, thoughtful. “This kind of stuff?”*

*Zuko manages to drag his eyes open to watch Aang mime a few moves, mustering up a* *belated nod. Fuck, he didn’t realize how tight his neck has been, his shoulders, his* *entire back because of his shoulder but now it's all lighting up, warm with pleasure-pain as knot after knot makes itself known and eases and loosens.*

*“And at the gym?” Aang presses, head cocking, like Zuko isn’t completely beyond* *words right now as Sokka’s hands dig and rub and fuck, it feels* divine. *“What kind of* *stuff are you doing?”*

*“Uhhh—” Shit, no, words. He needs words, even though it feels like it’s taking* *everything he has not to just let out a continuous stream of moans, which…would be a* *bit much. “Rows. And…”*
