*Sokka snorts behind him, close enough for Zuko to feel it move the hairs at the nape of* *his neck, his hands continuing to work as he rattles off Zuko’s usual routine. Which is* *excellent because Zuko feels like his brain might be melting.*

*“There are some things from yoga that might help,” Aang finally says, hopping off the* *couch, the motion jostling Zuko enough to slit his eyes open again. He’s not quite sure* *when he closed them…. “And some extra stretches, too, that I can show you.”*

*A long beat of silence except for the little noise Zuko makes as Sokka begins working* *on a knot that feels like it’s* under *his shoulder blade. Fuck, he’s never making fun of* *Azula for her weekly massage budget ever again.*

*“And do you have a foam roller?” Aang asks, his tone dry and his gaze flicking over* *Zuko’s shoulder. “You can give yourself a little massage with one of those when Mr.* *Masseuse here isn’t available.”*

*“My new career path awaits me,” Sokka murmurs. Zuko lets out a wordless noise of* *agreement.*

*“You’d be great at it,” he slurs, feeling hypnotized by the press of Sokka’s fingers and* *the gentle rocking of his body in counterpoint to Sokka’s touch, warm heat slowly* *spreading from his shoulder up his neck and down his arm, inching down his spine.*

*“Maybe I should do this later,” Aang says, amused, and Zuko makes a noise because* *a response seems necessary even if he doesn’t exactly remember what it is Aang* *should or shouldn’t be doing.*

*“Show us,” Sokka insists, laughter in his voice that Zuko will figure out…later.* *Definitely later. “I’ll show him again if he passes out on us.”*

“Here it is,” Sokka says, voice muffled as he leans back to reach behind the couch. And the morning has already been too long, too emotionally draining already, too full of Sokka’s hands on his chest and biceps and scrabbling for purchase against his back, for Zuko to stop the way his eyes slide down the length of Sokka’s body stretched out and tensed like that, tracing over the span of skin revealed by his t-shirt riding up, something low in Zuko’s gut that he absolutely shouldn’t be indulging sparking to life as his gaze lingers over the line of Sokka’s waistband before dipping —

“There you go,” Sokka says, thighs and hips and stomach all moving as he crunches back up and tosses the tube of packed foam, forcing Zuko back to awareness to avoid getting hit in the face. “Or I can give you a massage,” Sokka adds when Zuko just stares at the roller for a moment, trying to get his equilibrium back, “Take advantage of these hands while you still can.” Which is *not* helping, and Sokka fucking waggling his eyebrows and wiggling his fingers shouldn’t be sexy, either, shouldn’t make that spark flare to life. But it *does,* because Zuko is an *idiot*, wanting kindling in his stomach even as he rolls his eyes and shakes his head, warmth trying to spread through his core as he tosses the roller behind him and makes more of a production than is strictly necessary out of settling his upper back against it. But fuck, he needs the moment to settle his breath, to avert his eyes and hide his face and hope that any flush on his face will be explained away by the change of position.

“Boo,” Sokka pouts, and Zuko can picture the exaggerated frown on his face. “And here I thought *I* was your number one personal massager.”

Holy shit. Zuko swallows back some noise that feels too wild-edged and thin-pressed to be a laugh. “Keep your saucy fingers to yourself,” he tries to tease, hoping his breathlessness will be credited to the change of position, too, and not to his spiraling imagination, his mind is suddenly full of images of his t-shirt and skin and pants streaked with sauce, trails left by Sokka’s fingers and fuck, why is that *hot*.

“Oh, so as long as it’s not saucy, hot stuff?” Sokka grins, and Zuko knows that tone and knows he shouldn’t peek, not when he’s already three stray thoughts away from starting to get hard like he’s in high school again and fuck, this is just ridiculous. But he looks anyway, because he always does, because his self-control when it comes to Sokka is used up on so many other things. And he looks just in time to see Sokka licking his fingers clean, making a show out of it. No, making a *performance* of it, there *is* a difference. And fuck, if Zuko thought the *spoon* yesterday was obscene? Then this, when Sokka is doing it on purpose, his expression joking but his eyes nearly half-lidded, his gaze through his lashes and so *direct,* his dangling earring swaying forward to kiss his jaw as his lips part and his tongue curls around—

“Don’t worry,” Sokka says in a totally normal voice, suddenly popping to his feet and gathering up his trash as if Zuko isn’t having a horrifying epiphany about his future relationship with sesame chicken. “I won’t sauce you unless requested.”

Except Zuko can feel the words to do just that trying to climb up his throat, something needy and desperate and pleading clinging to his tongue when Sokka pauses to grab up Zuko’s trash too and Zuko suddenly finds himself looking up Sokka’s body from calf to knee to thigh to the jut of his hip bone. The sudden dryness of his mouth might be the only thing preventing him from blurting out the words as he drags his eyes all the way up to meet Sokka’s easy smile, his pulse pounding so hard he can barely breathe and the words right *there* and*—*

And then Sokka is disappearing into the kitchen, the sound of water running coming a few seconds later as he washes his hands, and Zuko is floundering, suddenly alone in his wake.

A joke. Just a joke, and Zuko lets his head thunk back against the rug, dragging in a ragged breath, then another. Whatever it was for Zuko, for Sokka it was nothing but a joke.

Zuko stares at a patch of sun on the ceiling, feeling his chest heaving as he tries to settle his body, as he tries not to feel every burning drop of his own blood, as he finally, slowly begins to roll out the tightness of his back, the sweet relief of loosening muscles easing through as he thinks that maybe it isn’t so much that Zuko needs to leave as that he doesn’t see how he can possibly stay.

&#45;-

***Two days and fifteen hours until the end***

Sokka isn’t quite sure when they stopped packing—that’s a lie, he does, it was when they found the photos, and sharing one and then another led to wanting to look through the pile, and now they’re leaning back against the couch, side by side, each with a stack of photos on their bent knees, half-filled boxes forgotten. And for once Sokka isn’t trying to keep them on schedule, is perfectly content to let the time slip away from them, is aware that this was the most reluctant schedule he's ever put together in his life, anyway, so what does it matter if they blow through it once again?

“Shit, is this from *high school?*” Zuko half-laughs, tilting a photo for Sokka to see the big grins and stacks of pizza boxes and a set of faces that are startling rounder and smaller than Sokka is used to these days. “What was this, Aang’s birthday?”

“I think so,” Sokka says, remembering the total chaos that had ensued when Aang opened his gifts to reveal a badminton kit, of all things. “I ate so much I could barely move.” It turned out badminton works up quite the appetite.

“I’m surprised you remember the one particular time doing that,” Zuko murmurs, and Sokka huffs and gives him a shove. He’s not really offended but it feels good to feel the heat of Zuko against his palm even through the fabric of his shirt—*his* shirt, not Zuko’s, technically it's Sokka's still even though part of him doesn’t know how he’d ever ask for it back—to have the warmth of Zuko along his side, to touch him. To just soak in all the tangible markers of Zuko’s presence, the kind of things Sokka never used to think about until their pending loss suddenly became the only thing in his head.

“Oh, remember this?” Sokka says, shoving that aside for now as he flips over a picture of the two of them with Katara and Aang and Toph and Jin, all holding up paddleboards in front of a summer lake.

“How could I forget King of the Water Hill,” Zuko says, dry, and Sokka grins at the memory.

“Toph *was* unreasonably good at that,” he agrees, the next photo showing her triumphant, her oar above her head and the rest of them in various stages of laughing and rueful in the water around her board.

“I’m surprised your dad let us do that,” Zuko says, lips quirked as he gently touches the edge of the photo.

“I’m surprised he didn’t join in,” Sokka says, rolling his eyes and silently grateful he didn’t have to watch his dad and Bato feeling each other up under the guise of wrestling. “Oho,” he exclaims as he flips the next picture, happily tossing aside the thought of his dad’s PDA, too. “Speaking of people being unreasonably good at niche activities.”

“See, that’s totally different,” Zuko protests as he takes in the photo of them all grinning for the camera for Ty Lee’s birthday, dodgeballs in hand and the trampoline park behind them and smiles big and wide and ignorant of the carnage ahead. “I expected Ty Lee and Azula to destroy us all.”

“Don’t forget Mai,” Sokka says, pulling a face at the memory.

“Oh?” Zuko’s voice is suddenly far too prim to land anywhere close to innocent. “I didn’t notice Mai.”

“Yeah, cause she was pegging *me* in the head the whole time.”

“Was she?” Zuko is visibly fighting back a smile, the traitor. He *would* side with his ex. “And with headshots against the rule?”

“There wasn’t a ref!”

“How awful for you.”

Sokka blows a raspberry at him and flicks the picture into Zuko’s chest as if he isn’t biting back his own grin, flipping the next photo over with something like excitement for the reveal beginning to flutter in his chest. “This must be the sports pile,” he says, the next photo of him and Zuko in a crowd of triumphant people, basketballs in their hands and gaudy medals around their necks.

“Oh man, remember when Jessica laid that guy out?” Zuko asks, leaning in to see better, pressing warm against Sokka’s shoulder. “She took intramural basketball *seriously*.”

“Of course I remember,” Sokka snorts. “It was hot.”

Zuko pulls back to give him an incredulous, laughing look. “Was *that* why you could barely get out an intelligent sentence after the game?”

“I had a moment, okay?” Sokka shrugs, feeling his cheeks pink and refusing to be embarrassed by it even if it was by no means his finest one.

“A moment? How abou—”

“Oh *man*,” Sokka interrupts as he flips the next photo, his brief and intensely debilitating crush instantly forgotten. “I forgot about this asshole!”

Zuko leans in again, the huff of his breath when he sees the photo making Sokka’s skin prickle in its wake all down the side of his neck. “She sucked,” Zuko agrees without rancor. “I can’t believe she broke up with me after meeting all my friends.”

“*I* can’t believe she hopped in the picture knowing she was going to break up with you the next day!”

“It feels,” Zuko says, “Like you’re more upset about the rudeness than my heartbreak.”

“Oh, like you cared,” Sokka rolls his eyes. “You dated her for like five weeks.”

Zuko gives him a sideways look like he does when he’s deciding how to respond and anticipation is bright in Sokka’s chest for whatever Zuko might say, replies and jokes lined up and ready to go and disappearing from his head entirely when Zuko suddenly just sticks out his tongue, which—

Sokka blinks himself back to attention when Zuko holds up another photo, hoping he wasn’t zoned out too long as he leans in to look at the details. It’s one of Suki’s shows, and, “Oh shit, look at how full the room looks,” Sokka says, fond warmth bursting in his chest as he takes in Suki captured mid-lyric, eyes closed in passion, her whole body thrown behind her guitar. “Suki would love to see this one.”

Zuko passes it over and then lets out a startled, choked laugh when he sees what’s next in the pile. “Why do you even *have* this?” he asks, flipping around a picture of Sokka dressed as Link standing next to a very buff Zelda, big grins on both of their faces. “Didn’t you date him for like six whole minutes?”

“It was the best couples costume ever,” Sokka says, lips twitching as he tries to hold onto his stuffiness. “Look at that detail work. It needs to be memorialized, even if the couple part of it didn’t last.” And maybe too because his primary memory of that night when he looks at the photo isn’t of…a guy whose name he doesn’t actually remember because really, they *did* only date for a week or two. It’s of scream-shouting with Zuko later in the evening over the results of the Best Candy in History bracket, both of them loopy by that point with sugar and games and the fun of dressing up and Sokka will live and die by the fact that Snickers *never* should have won, even if it’s mostly because referencing it always makes Zuko—

“Oh *god*,” Zuko suddenly says, laughing, breaking through his thoughts again. “Remember this?” He brandishes a photo, and it takes Sokka a moment to place the scene. A college apartment, the whole gang together, takeout containers, not exactly winnowing it down, but then—

***Seven years, one month, and twenty-four days until the end***

*“You’re telling me—” Mai somehow manages to both wheeze and shout at the same* *time.*

*“It’s not that funny,” Sokka protests, looking around beseechingly and seeing nothing* *but traitors all around him.*

*“It’s* hilarious*,” Katara says.*

*“It’s pretty great,” Suki agrees, squeezing his hand in silent apology even though* *she’s openly laughing.*

*“—that you both dated the same guy—”*

*“Didn’t you date him too?” Zuko mutters, kicking Katara’s foot.*

*“Flirted,” Katara says immediately. “Not dated.”*

*Azula raises an eyebrow. “Does that distinction really matter?”*

*“—in the same year—”*

*Suki purses her lips. “Jet’s the one with the straws in his mouth all the time?”*

*“…Yes,” Sokka, Zuko, and Katara all chorus, not quite looking at each other.*

*“It matters,” Suki assures her.*

*“—and you* didn’t realize it *until* now*?” Mai dissolves into another peal of cackles,* *and Sokka contemplates that this might be the most continuous display of emotion he’s* *ever seen from her. It* would *be over Jet…*

*“Why would we know!” Zuko says, throwing out his hands.*

*“Um, because you’re* friends*?” Ty Lee says, incredulous.*

*“That doesn’t mean I’m inviting Sokka into the bedroom with me!”*

*“We’re a package deal, anyway,” Suki murmurs just for Sokka to hear, which is a* *sentiment he will appreciate the humor—or maybe the allure—of when he’s not in* *front of all of his closest friends being roasted.*

*“You’ve been living together for all of college!” Mai exclaims. “It’s been* four years*!* *How did you never talk about the people you’ve dated? How did you not* notice*?”*

*“Only since sophomore year,” Zuko protests.*

*“And I wouldn’t exactly call it dating,” Sokka adds before he thinks better of it,* *belatedly registering his own words when Mai dissolves into more wheezing laughter.*

*“The next thing you know you’re going to find out he hooked up with you both in the* *same weekend!” she crows. Cracking herself up all on her own now, apparently. “In* *the same place!”*

*“Well, he took me back to his place,” Sokka says because apparently, he doesn’t know* *when to shut up.*

*“…Me, too,” Zuko says when all eyes swing to him.*

*Mai chokes to gurgling, delighted silence.*

*“…Let’s stop talking about this now,” Sokka finally suggests, Zuko’s fervent, “Yes,* *please,” nearly lost in the others’ shouting in protest and laughter.*

*“Hey,” Aang says when it finally starts to settle down again, leaning forward to catch* *Zuko’s eye. “Is Jet the guy who’s always chatting with Iroh after the lunch lull?”*

*Sokka can’t help joining in the laughter this time at the look of pure horror on Zuko’s* *face.*

Sokka stares at the frozen image of Mai with tears literally running down her face, Zuko looking exasperated but indulgent next to her and Sokka vaguely disgruntled in the background and unaware of Suki mugging for the camera behind his shoulder. He feels like he can practically feel the burn of his embarrassment, can see again the way even Azula’s lips were twitching into a genuine little smile in her corner of the room, and he's suddenly struck with an intense longing to go back there. To that apartment, that night, to that moment even when he couldn’t stop more incriminating words from tumbling off his lips. Classes were stressful and there was the looming job search and graduation barreling down on them, but god, Sokka thinks he would give anything right now to be able to go back to when it felt like he and Zuko were ready to tackle the whole world at each other’s sides.

“I guess we can copy them, or take pictures or something so we can each have a copy,” Zuko is saying, a little smile on his lips as he flips through more photos. His face is relaxed with happiness when Sokka sneaks a glance, easy to see when he’s leaning against Sokka’s shoulder again, or maybe never stopped, and Sokka wonders if he’s imagining that he can feel the ends of Zuko’s hair brushing against his own neck. “Or we can figure out whose are whose and send the copies to each other?” Zuko adds with a questioning look when Sokka doesn’t respond, and for a moment Sokka feels overwhelmed by seeing that look from so close, unable to do anything except take it in. Golden eyes only inches away, lips gently parted in question, skin warm gold except for where Zuko’s lashes cast the barest of shadows, so insubstantial that Sokka might be imagining them, he'd have to get even closer to be sure, all of it so close it takes Sokka a long moment to process Zuko’s words, and then another to fully understand what Zuko *means*.

To realize that he means—that he's talking about—

“Yeah,” Sokka hears himself say as if everything inside him is normal, “Great idea, hotman.” As if his mind isn’t tripping over the idea of there needing to be *copies*. Over needing to scan and print, to take photos of photos. Of not having the polaroids or the photo booth strips. Over the fact that even if he prints it all on photo paper, the new versions will be without the creases and folds and tack holes and finger smudges and worn edges. They’ll be shiny and new and they won’t—they won’t be *this*.

“Good thinking,” he manages to add, the words somehow coming out casual and easy as if all of his insides don’t suddenly feel hollow and thin because god, Sokka already knows how useless it's going to be. He's going to try anyway, will make a fucking collage of them if he thinks a picture of it will make Zuko smile, but fuck does he know more than he ever wanted about the futility of trying to replicate and replace things that are already gone.

&#45;-

***Two days and twelve hours until the end***

“Did Alex ever get back to you about those deadlines?” Zuko’s question floats from the kitchen to the living room. Sokka had suggested takeout, had suggested looking at more photos or watching a show instead of cooking, had suggested everything he could think of to keep Zuko leaning against the couch at his side, but Zuko is determined to eat down as much of the kitchen as possible before they leave. Which Sokka knows, he knows, and he’s not sure why he keeps going out for breakfast and lunch anyway. It’s like it's slipping his mind over and over or something, which doesn’t make sense because he knows it, he's *known* it. Zuko’s said it so many times, he just…he doesn’t know.

Fuck. Sokka just...wishes time could slow down. Wishes it could freeze, that the clock could stop st

ticking forward, that April 1 could never come. That Zuko could be sitting next to him again*—*not just now but again and again and again*—*warm and relaxed and laughing and not so far away all Sokka has of him is his voice.

But if that’s all of Zuko there is to have…

“Yeah,” Sokka calls back, holding up a card game from the bookcase and trying to remember if they’ve ever played it. “It turned out there was an extension and she forgot to tell me.” Zuko makes a noise of sympathy. “Hey, do you remember Happy Salmon?”

“…Is that a restaurant?”

“No, game. Cards. Green fish on the box.”

A grunt. “Didn’t Bato give you that a year or two ago? And this is like, the third time she’s done that to you.”

“No, I think that was Sushi Go. And I know, I just—there’s been a lot going on.” Which feels like one of the larger understatements Sokka’s ever made. Though maybe it’s that there’s not *enough* going on. Or that all the things going on are none of the things Sokka wants to be happening.

“Yeah, well, that’s even more reason to give you notice!” As if Sokka is going to tell them that he suddenly needs more time on his projects because some days he wakes up and remembers Zuko is leaving and feels like he can’t breathe.

Still, Sokka makes a noise of agreement, because he *does* plan to say something to her next month —or maybe the one after, or maybe after the baby comes…—and pulls out the next box. “Is it Risk or the Lord of the Rings Risk where we’re missing pieces?”

“Lord of the Rings,” Zuko says over the sizzle of a skillet. “But I thought you made new ones?”

“I don’t remember doing that,” Sokka frowns. He doesn’t think he’d forget something like that, usually his projects are rather…involved.

“Must have been Aang, then.” The familiar *tap-tap-tap* of Zuko’s wooden spoon against the skillet drifts down the hall. “Did they hassle you about taking the week off?”

“Eh, kind of.” Absolutely. But only because Sokka told them so late, so he can’t really blame them for it. “Monopoly?”

Zuko snorts. “Like I need a worse relationship with my family.” The sound of the fridge opening and Sokka can picture the way Zuko must be standing there, hand on the open door, a little frown creasing his brow and rings gently tapping against the handle as he considers his options. “I’m sorry you had to use your PTO to pack.”

“Yeah, well. It was a failure of planning.” And bravery, and willpower, and foresight, and so many other things. “My own fault, right?” Which feels more true leaving his lips than he wants it to be.

“I mean, it could be good for them to figure things out without you,” Zuko says, voice slightly muffled before he makes a small sound of triumph. He must be using the fish sauce, then. The cap somehow always manages to stick.

“I’m more worried it’s going to be a shit show when I get back,” Sokka admits because there’s more than one failure of planning going on in his life right now. “Carcassonne?”

“Oh, I love that game.”

“I thought Katara was going to cry the first time we played it,” Sokka says, cracking open the box to make sure all the pieces are accounted for.

“It was great.” A pause, then, “Not that part, I mean, just—good game.”

Sokka breathes out a laugh as he adds the game to the stack he’s making for Zuko, any response he might make about Zuko delighting in Katara’s suffering flying out of his head as he catches sight of the next board game buried at the back of the shelf, pristine and freshly opened and never played.

***Two months and twenty days until the end***

*“You’re not—what do you mean you’re not coming home for dinner?” Sokka asks,* *hearing his voice climb two octaves but unable to stop himself or care as the* *excitement and anticipation in his gut curdles over into upset.*

*A beat of quiet over the phone, then, “I’m sorry. I thought you knew I had work.”*

*“Of course. You’re always working, lately,” Sokka bites out, clenching a useless fist* *and letting it drop, impotent, after a beat of useless straining, words rushing out of him* *instead. “You never used to work this much, and now you’re always—I don’t see why* *—aren’t you leaving in three months anyway? What is there even to work on?” What* *could be better than being* here*?*

*“…It’s transition stuff,” Zuko says, his voice raspy and familiar and right in Sokka’s* *ear and such a fucking lie, such a fucking tease when he’s all the way across the city.* *“Leaving during year-end closing is a bad look, I’m trying not to put them in a rough* *spot.”*

*“I know, I just—” Fuck, why is he so upset about this? It’s not like Sokka hasn’t put in* *years of working late. “We said we were going to do game night tonight,” he says, the* *words bursting out plaintive bordering on whiny. “I thought—we haven’t had one in* *so long.” And how many are they going to have left?*

*“I’m sorry,” Zuko repeats, genuinely sounding crushed about it which doesn’t do* *anything except make Sokka feels even worse. God, why couldn’t he just— “You know* *how work stuff is.”*

*And he does, he does, he does. He’s one to talk. But fuck, he’s been* trying *to be present* *these past few months, trying to be there, every hour spent in the office past 5:00pm* *grating on his nerves in a way it never has before. And to come home to an empty* *apartment—to do it* now, *before he has to—*

*“I’ll make it up to you, okay?”*

*“No,” Sokka sighs, shoving a hand through his hair and letting his forehead drop onto* *the kitchen table, everything in him suddenly deflating. “It’s okay,” he lies, “It’s—”*

*“No, I will, I can—”*

*“It’s fine,” Sokka repeats, bordering on sharp, then gentler, “It’s fine, Zuko.” The last* *thing he wants is to make Zuko’s night at the office even worse than it probably* *already is. “it’s not a big deal,” he adds, lifting his head to look at the brand-new* *game he spent hours researching, at the meal he cooked. It’s one of Zuko’s favorites, a* *finicky pain in the ass to make but the idea of surprising him with it had seemed like* *such a great idea at the start of the week… “I just missed your text. It’s no worries.”*

*“…You sure?” Zuko asks, hesitant, and Sokka can picture the way his lips would be* *half-parted in question, the way his whole body would shift to reflect his uncertainty.*

*“Yeah,” Sokka says, the word falling easy and maybe if he repeats it to himself* *enough they’ll even be true. “Don’t work too late, okay? There’s a black ice warning* *tonight, you don’t want to have to deal with that.”*

*“Alright. Yeah, thanks.” A pause, like Zuko is expecting something else, then, “See* *you soon, okay?”*

*“Yeah,” Sokka forces out, the words wanting to be jagged. “See you soon, hotman.”*

*Sokka lets Zuko hang up first, carefully setting his phone back onto the table and* *staring at the already set-up game and the slowly cooling food for who knows how* *long before he finally moves. He feels like his breaths are coming too fast, like his* *whole body has become strange and awkward and clumsy, like everything is a blur* *around him as he cleans the kitchen and packs away the game with shaking hands and* *listlessly picks at his portion of dinner. He doesn’t know what he’s going to do with* *the rest. It's awful as leftovers, but the idea of just pitching all that work into the* *trash…*

*Sokka turns away from the table like that will solve his problem—if only things like* *that ever worked—and because apparently, he feels the need to punish himself for* *lashing out at Zuko earlier, he reaches for his phone again, ignoring the text chain* *with Zuko and pulling up the apartment listing he’s had open for days now. The other* *thing he can’t make go away by pretending it isn’t there.*

*It’s a perfect apartment, really. Well within Zuko’s budget, right in the heart of his* *second most-wanted location. Near two grocery stores and three bus lines and the* *kind of grungy lifting gym Zuko lives for and Sokka can tell just by looking at it that* *Zuko will love it. He can picture Zuko cooking in that kitchen, can see him lining the* *sundrenched windows with plants, knows exactly what the bedroom will look like* *down to which walls Zuko will hang his art on and what side of the bed he’ll put his* *nightstand on.*

*It's old and it has character and probably hot water that takes longer to heat up than* *Zuko takes to shower, and radiators that probably rattle and bang during the night,* *and all the closets are in the hall. But it has a kitchen with a gas stove and a living* *room best described as cozy and a tiny nook just right for a desk and the built-ins that* *Zuko’s always wanted and a window to a fire escape that Zuko will scale to the roof* *probably before he even finishes getting boxes into the apartment.*

*And one bedroom, to cap it all off. And Sokka doesn’t know why it takes everything he* *has to make himself finally send Zuko the link.*

“Sokka?” Zuko voice sounds like he’s said it more than once, Sokka jolting up to see Zuko’s head popped into the room, a questioning frown on his face. “Are you okay?”

“Yeah, just…yeah.” He quickly sets the board game aside in the donate pile, not letting himself think about it any more than he let himself think about why he buried it so carefully at the back of the bottom shelf in the first place. “A long day, you know?”

Zuko hums in agreement. “Dinner’s done. You ready?”

“Yeah,” Sokka says, the word trying to stick but coming out smooth as he takes in Zuko in his old Jasmine Dragon apron and a towel slung over his shoulder, lit by the warm light of the kitchen. The idea that this might be the last time Sokka ever sees him this way… “I’m ready,” he says and hates how familiar it’s become for those words to feel more and more like a lie.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

There's no way I can make this even more angsty and dramatic, I tell myself, as I find even more ways to make it angsty and dramatic

## Chapter 3: Be Still My Foolish Heart

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Some warnings in this chapter for brief references to Azula's breakdown and Zuko's childhood, more horny pining, the two of them being truly oblivious idiots, and me getting very self-indulgent about some of these scenes lol

### Text

***Two days and one hour until the end***

Zuko’s body feels heavy and slow when he finally gives up on useless things like lying awake staring at the ceiling and wishing things could be different and rolls out of bed. His head is thick and fuzzy like it rarely is anymore these days, his thoughts still tangled as he stumbles out of his room. He can at least put on the morning news and see if that will help him nod off, or some mindless rerun that—

He rocks to a stop as a quiet clatter and muffled curse drifts out of the kitchen, blinking as he slowly processes that he isn’t the only one away in the cusp-of-morning darkness. Which means…

Zuko pads down the hallway to stare blankly into the kitchen, where Sokka is at the stovetop and, judging by the mess around him, has been for a while. Which…maybe Zuko is hallucinating. Or maybe he finally fell asleep, because he can’t remember the last time Sokka has voluntarily woken up before midmorning two days in a row in all their time living together.

And it certainly feels like something straight out of a dream as he looks at Sokka, his hair still down and teasing at the collar of a t-shirt that Zuko tossed years ago when it shrunk in the wash, but Sokka apparently liked enough to rescue from the rag bag. The hem barely kisses the edge of Sokka’s sleep pants, the fabric stretched tight across the breadth of his shoulders and clinging to the curve of his ribs and dip of his waist and—

“…What are you doing?” Zuko asks, knuckling at his eyes and giving himself a surreptitious little pinch just in case he *is* actually asleep.

“Shit, man,” Sokka jumps, fumbling his spoon for a frantic moment before managing to grab it before it hits the countertop. “You’re like a cat when you do that,” he huffs, shaking his head and brushing his hair back with his wrist. “And what does it look like I’m doing? I’m eating down the kitchen,” he says with a little wave of his spoon, the gesture and smile he shoots over his shoulder at odds with the tight edges to his words.

And maybe if he were more awake Zuko would be able to poke and tease and figure out that tone, try to understand the origins of that tightness. But right now all his remaining bandwidth is going to silently inventorying what’s left in the kitchen and feeling no small amount of trepidation for another of Sokka’s cooking experiments. “…Oh?”

“I’m making breakfast drinks instead of going out for them again,” Sokka says, as if ‘breakfast drinks’ are a whole distinct category of food. And maybe they are, the way Sokka drinks them. And the way he starts rattling off ingredients like condensed milk and cayenne and cinnamon and chocolate and *caramels*, shit, Zuko didn’t even know there was any Halloween candy left let alone that Sokka would try to *melt it down for breakfast*.

It sounds like a morning of trying not to vomit more than a meal, and Zuko starts searching for more tactful words than ‘please, god, no’ to convey that as Sokka pulls down a pair of mugs, *still* naming spices.

“And for *you*—” Sokka does a little flourish-thing with his hand that Zuko is going to make fun of him for later before reaching behind his little pot to pull forward a smaller saucepan and neatly pour the dark contents into a waiting mug. “—plain bitter chocolate.”

“Oh,” Zuko says, laughing a little at the dire direction of his thoughts—definitely too little sleep— and feeling a burst of pleasure. It’s such a small thing, a familiar thing, for Sokka to think about him like that, to take extra steps to make sure Zuko will like something. But it still makes Zuko feel as warm, makes him feel as seen, as it did the very first time “Thanks.”

“I wouldn’t do that to you, hot stuff,” Sokka says with a wink, tapping a careful measure of cinnamon on top before offering over the mug. “At least not first thing in the morning.”

“Gotta wake up first,” Zuko agrees, a thread of nostalgia twining through the warmth in his chest as he accepts the familiar paisley-patterned mug.

***Seven years, eleven months, and twenty days until the end***

*“This is ridiculous,” Sokka complains, brandishing his mug of cocoa-salted caramel-whipped-something for them all to see. “How* *is it that I consistently study best when I* *drink out of one specific mug?* How?”

*Zuko purses his lips, considering what to say.*

*“…That’s a plain black mug*,” *Aang points out.*

*“Not* this *one,” Sokka huffs like it should be obvious. “Another one they have here. It’s* *ridiculous!”*

*“I agree that mug appearance is critically important to my academic success,” Toph* *says, deadpan, flipping an earbud around her finger and Zuko still doesn’t understand* *how she can listen to her reading and to a conversation at the same time.*

*“Joke all you want, I ran tests,” Sokka says, glowering at his apparently insufficient* *mug. “I’ve tried the same drink in different mugs, I’ve tried sitting in specific seats, I* *tried going cold turkey—it’s that one specific mug!”*

*“Maybe it speaks to you,” Aang says, grave and lips twitching in amusement. “Maybe* *the pattern calls to your soul. Which one is it?”*

*“…The paisley one.”*

*Zuko glances across the café where the rather alarming combination of colors in a* *rather vertigo-inducing paisley pattern can be seen even through bodies and bags and* *across tables and basically from anywhere, really.*

*“…The one that makes Katara dizzy to look at too long?” Aang asks.*

*“That’s the one.”*

*“So that’s what your soul is like,” Zuko murmurs, looking at the mug out of the corner* *of his eye in case he gets dizzy too. “How unfortunate.” He ducks the half-hearted* *backhand Sokka sends his way, sticking out his tongue.*

*“Rude, all of you,” Sokka says, pulling a face at Zuko before leaning forward again,* *expression earnest. “And ignore what it* looks *like—*

*“Done,” Toph says immediately. “I win.”*

*“—how am I going to do on this test if this stuff only makes sense when I’ve got that* *mug in my hand?”*

*“And how are you going to get the mug past Pakku,” Aang muses.*

*“Poorly, from the sound of it,” Zuko teases, catching Sokka’s swipe this time instead* *of ducking it.*

*“Maybe it’s not the mug,” Aang suggests, not even looking as he reaches out to grab* *the hand Zuko has raised to flick Sokka in the face and gently pushes it back down* *onto the table. “Maybe it’s about the energy it brings. The conditions. You could try to* *recreate that during your exam.”*

*“What conditions,” Toph asks, wrinkling her nose. “Zuko’s air of overwhelming* *paranoia?”*

*“Hey,” Zuko protests, even though he* is *aware that it’s a bit much* *to always sit in the* *same corner of the coffee shop, the one that lets Zuko be hidden if he sits along the* *bench—an out of the way position that’s made vulnerable by the psychedelic paisley* *mug. Zuko’s successfully hidden his coffee habit from Uncle for years. He’s not about* *to let that cat out of the bag when Uncle is still regularly lamenting the fact that Zuko* *decided to move out of their little apartment above the Jasmine Dragon when really,* *two buses and a trolley across town, it's not* that *much of a commute, is it?*

*“Mental state matters,” Aang is saying, face arranged into a solemn expression. “You* *need to do whatever gets your mind in the right state to excel!”*

*“Don’t go making blanket statements like that,” Toph says, reaching forward with* *that unerring accuracy of hers for the last of Zuko’s croissant. “What if I excel when* *I’m punching people?”*

*“Then I’m very sorry you’re living life so below your potential,” Aang says seriously,* *clasping her free hand as she pulls a face and makes a show of gagging at the* *sentimentality.*

*“And remind me to never sit next to* you *in class,” Sokka mutters, eyeing her like she’s* *going to break out swinging at any moment.*

*Toph sniffs, extracting her hand. “Like I’d be caught dead in one of* your *classes.”*

*“Thank god for that,” Sokka says, as if they’d ever have any classes together except* *the lingering gen ed requirements he’s kicking into yet another semester. “I don’t* *need you laying out classmates, we need all the collective brainpower we can get in* *Pakku’s seminar.”*

*Zuko raises his eyebrow. “I thought you said Trace or Tripp or whatever his name* *was is a total waste of air.”*

*“Yeah, well. He’d be a* tripping *hazard on the floor.” Sokka lets that hang in the air* *for a moment before breaking into a wide grin. “Get it? Get it?”*

*“That was awful,” Toph complains, Aang giving an apologetic nod of agreement.* *“I’m pretty sure the only tripping hazard around here is you, Snoozles.”*

*Sokka opens his mouth and then pauses, frowning. “What does that even* mean*?”*

*“Don’t think about it,” Aang recommends.*

*“Don’t,” Zuko agrees, biting back a smile. Sometimes Toph’s insults are just an* *invitation to engage.*

*“I see how it is,” Sokka says after a moment, giving them all a narrow-eyed look,* *making a show of being beleaguered even though Zuko can see the laughter shaking* *his shoulders. “Make fun of my plight! I’m going to bomb my final—”*

*“You’ve never bombed a final in your life.”*

*“—because I have a* problem *with a* mug*—"*

*“The rest of us are going to bomb too if you keep being a distraction,” Toph mutters.*

*“—that none of you think is real—”*

*“No, no, this is important,” Zuko cuts in, putting on an earnest tone, “Sokka can only* *focus when he has a psychedelic paisley mug in his hands? This is a problem.”*

*“*Thank *you.”*

*“Think of all the things Sokka already can’t focus on,” Zuko continues, making his* *face intent and serious. “We have to investigate this.”*

*“With science,” Aang suggests.*

*“Get all the experts on it ASAP,” Zuko agrees. “Who has Professor Wu on call?”*

*“I get the impression,” Sokka says, eyes narrowing, “That you’re humoring me.”*

*“I wonder why,” Zuko grins, resting his chin on his fist and batting his eyelashes.*

*“Oh, you know, just a vibe.” Sokka mirrors his position.*

*Zuko hums. “Vibes are important.”*

*“They are, aren’t they,” Sokka agrees.*

*“Almost as good as gut feelings.”*

*“My gut is full of feelings, Sifu Hotman.” Sokka manages to hold his straight face for* *about two whole seconds before he cracks, taking Zuko with him as they both burst* *into laughter.*

*“You guys,” Toph pronounces, “Spend too much time together.”*

*“Whatever,” Zuko says, setting aside his reading and shifting his bag out of the way.* *“You’re just jealous.”*

*“Where are you going?” Sokka asks over Toph’s scoff, straightening as Zuko slides* *out of the booth.*

*“Where do you think?” Zuko asks, pausing to grab the flannel off the back of Sokka’s* *chair. “I’ve got some performance-enhancing mugs to steal.”*

“Cheers, hotman,” Sokka says as he pours his own mug. The words are quiet, soft. His eyes are on Zuko and his expression is one Zuko doesn’t know how to read, so Zuko just offers up his own mug in return and takes a sip, instinctively bracing himself and pleasantly surprised to find that the drink is thick and smooth, not too sweet and with a hint of spiciness in the aftertaste. It’s shockingly good for one of Sokka’s impromptu creations, and it’s not at all what’s going to get Zuko through these last two days, not the borderline harsh kick he needs to get himself focused for what he needs to do. But Zuko is going to savor it anyway, every sip. Every single drop until it’s gone.

&#45;-

***Two days before the end***

“Okay, here’s the game plan,” Sokka says, waiting to make sure he has Zuko’s attention before slapping his notebook onto the counter. He stayed up all night finally making a real plan to get this apartment packed and they’re actually going to get it done, and Sokka is going to be fucking supportive if it kills him. And he’s going to ignore the fact that it feels like it might.

It’s probably just all the sugar first thing in the morning, anyway, like Zuko is always saying. Maybe some expired candies, too…

Sokka flips through his notes, laying out how they’re going to pack the dishes for transport and how they’ll attack the spices. How to divide and conquer the pantry and the no-man’s land under the sink, and the best way to mix heavy and light things in strategic layers so nothing is crushed and the boxes aren’t too hard to lift while still making organizational sense. He focuses on his lists and diagrams, going more and more into detail because Zuko keeps nodding along, kind of smiling, an expression on his face that Sokka doesn’t quite know how to describe except that it makes him feel warm and he likes it. And it formed sometime in the middle of Sokka’s explanation, slowly growing and taking hold as Sokka flipped between pages of his notebook, and Sokka doesn’t want to see it fade again so he just…keeps going.

Eventually, he does run out of words and pages, though, despite his best efforts. Not that he really has grounds to complain about the fact that they need to actually start *enacting* the plan when they’re about half as far along with packing this whole place up as they need to be…

They divide and conquer, Zuko putting on a playlist and Sokka making sure to set the volume just low enough that it’s still easy to talk over if they want to, just quiet enough that he can still hear Zuko’s raspy hum. And Sokka pays extra attention to the distribution of items in each box just like he worked out last night, trying to balance the breakables with a mind toward what might need to be immediately used or unpacked later, thinking of weight distribution and hefting each box after every addition. Zuko’s shoulder was bothering him yesterday, and Sokka couldn’t be unaware even if he wanted to be of the fact that Zuko is going to have to carry all of these into his new place on his own.

“Are you sure you don’t want any dishes?” Zuko asks, expression openly skeptical as he pauses to watch Sokka unload the cabinets. “Not even some mugs? Some bowls?”

“No, no,” Sokka says, firmly setting everything onto Zuko’s side of the counter. “Take it. It’s yours.”

“I don’t need all this stuff for just me, though,” Zuko protests, and Sokka forcefully shoves the phrase ‘*just me’* from his brain.

“Katara and Aang don’t need it either,” he points out. “Not with all the baby clutter they’re getting.” And he suddenly needs to know that Zuko will still be eating ramen out of his favorite soup mug up in Boston, even if Sokka isn’t there to see it, even if that mug started off as Sokka’s.

“If you’re sure,” Zuko says after a long beat, and Sokka lets out a quiet breath of relief as something tense and ready to fight that’s been knotted down his spine is able to release.

“I am,” Sokka says, making his voice sure and summoning a smile. “Besides, you’ll have people over all the time, right? So you’ll need them.”

“…I guess,” Zuko shrugs, turning back to his cabinet. “Sure.”

Sokka nods again, decisive, and begins to pack the dishes away, all that they’ve acquired and upgraded to and held onto in nearly ten years of living together, padding it with dishtowels and leaving plenty of spice for light things on top, and it’s a weird, painful kind of satisfaction to write Zuko’s name on the side of each box and not his own.

“You realize this means you have to take first dibs on the cookbooks,” Zuko says as Sokka finishes packing away everything except the dishes they’ll need for the rest of the day and god, the idea that they won’t need them tomorrow except for breakfast...

“Wait,” Sokka says, shaking himself out of his thoughts and sitting back on his heels as he replays Zuko’s words. “What? But you bought so many of—”

“You bought just as many,” Zuko says, a familiar stubbornness creeping into the line of his jaw.

“You picked them o—”

“We picked them out together. Most of them.”

“Okay, but you use—”

“You use them just as much,” Zuko cuts in, giving him an exasperated look. “So you can pick what you want first.”

“I just don’t see why you wouldn’t—”

“*Sokka*.” Sokka snaps his mouth shut as Zuko catches his eyes. “Because I want you to. Pick your cookbooks.”

He pauses a moment, clearly waiting to see if Sokka is going to protest, but Sokka can only swallow hard, feeling something in his stomach flip over. He doesn’t have any fight in him for something like that, for Zuko wanting this for him, and it takes far more than it should to tear himself away from Zuko’s determined gaze, his eyes landing inexorably on at the stacks of cookbooks on the kitchen cart teetering somewhere between organized and haphazard.

***Five years, eight months, and twelve days until the end***

*“Turn down the flame!”*

*“It has to brown!”*

*“It’s* smoking!”

*“Cause you didn’t put in enough oil!”*

*“I put in* so much *oil, it’s* everywhere, *what do you—”*

*“Fuck, ow!”*

*“See? So much oil!”*

*“Motherfuck, how am I supposed to—”*

*“Deglaze it! Deglaze it!”*

*“But first we’re supposed to—”*

*”*Deglaze it before it chars.”

*Sokka coughs as steam—smoke, more like—rises out of the skillet, thick and acrid and* *vaguely vinegar-scented, Zuko scrabbling to throw open the kitchen window in the* *midst of his own hacking, both of them waving dishtowels until the air finally begins to* *clear.*

*“…Shit.”*

*“Fuck.”*

*“What did the recipe say to—”*

*“I don’t know.”*

*“…What do you mean you don’t know. You read it!”*

*“And* you *said to deglaze it! That wasn’t the next step!”*

*“Well, what was?”*

*“It said to get color on it!”*

*“Yeah, I’d say this got some fucking color!”*

*Another bout of coughing as they lean in to evaluate the contents of the skillet, the* *bottom beginning to bubble and hiss in a way that feels…questionable.*

*“…Add the stock?”*

*“Already? You think?”*

*A pause, then, “Can’t hurt?”*

*A shrug and the stock goes in, both of them watching as the contents of the skillet float* *up, vegetables and chicken thighs and blackened bits of…something.*

*A lot of blackened bits, actually.*

*“Ah.”*

*“Huh.”*

*“…I think we messed up a step.”*

*“Mai said this wasn’t that hard,” Zuko frowns, poking at the skillet.*

*“Mai does this for a living.”*

*“…Should I call her?”*

*“I’ll call Katara for a backup plan,” Sokka says, grabbing up his phone with relief.*

“As if you won't fight me for *One Pan, Two Plates*,” Sokka says, feeling frozen in place. He can hear his voice shakier than he wants it to be, but hopefully not so much to be noticed over the music.

“We might need duplicates of some,” Zuko allows after a pause, his voice even, almost absent, and Sokka feels himself trip over the idea of a fresh copy. As if anything he makes would taste the same from some uncreased, unmarked, unspattered page. As if corn cakes in the late summer will be anywhere near as good without Zuko’s fingerprints permanently smudging over the measurements, meaning they have to guess every time. As if they both don’t have all their favorites —all of *each other’s* favorites, shit, Sokka cooks more for Zuko than for himself and vice versa— memorized by heart, recipes barely needed, and fuck, this is the photos all over again, isn’t it?

“We just might,” Sokka says, taking a deep breath before firmly shoving that all aside for later— two days, he just needs to get through two days, not even that much—because an early-morning breakdown was not part of the plan. “Maybe we do a draft or something instead of just sorting. I think we bought most of these together.”

“I assure you,” Zuko says, tone suddenly dry, “*I* did not buy the official TikTok cookbook.”

“No, but you’re the one it came into the apartment with,” Sokka says, seizing on an opportunity to feel the lightness of teasing. “That means it’s yours.”

“Only because Toph saw me first, it was supposed to be for you,” Zuko protests.

Sokka raises his eyebrows. “Pretty sure she didn’t see anyone,” he says sweetly, and Zuko scoffs and boos and jeers at the joke as if he hasn’t made it himself a thousand times, as if Toph doesn’t gleefully do it at every opportunity.

And if they somehow find their way back to their divide and conquer without ever touching the cookbooks, well, Sokka is quietly, embarrassingly relieved to be able to push that particular task off to later. Part of him knows it’s just shoving off the pain, letting it slowly stew and build and grow, but he never has been one to rip off his own band-aids, has he?

&#45;-

***One day and twenty-one hours until the end***

“I didn’t realize we had so many chickpeas,” Zuko says as he stacks yet another pair of cans onto the counter.

“Yeah, because you keep *buying them*.”

Zuko shrugs. Sure, it’s…a bit of an excessive amount, but, “They don’t go bad.” Sokka makes a deeply exasperated noise behind him and Zuko glances back, amused. “We can just donate them.”

“*I’m* not moving thirty cans of chickpeas,” Sokka says, crossing his arms, which happens to flex his biceps, which makes Zuko smile.

“Aang and Katara eat a lot of beans,” Zuko makes a show of musing, thoughtfully holding up an extra-large can.

“Do *not* plant that idea in their heads,” Sokka warns. “Not unless you’re going to be doing the moving. I’m not asking my pregnant sister to lift thirty pounds of beans.”

“You could ask her not pregnant husband.”

“…Fine,” Sokka huffs, clothes rustling as he unfolds his arms and drags out his phone like Zuko knew he would, “I’ll ask. I’m sure they already have the pantry stocked, though. Aang is going full prepper with the baby coming.”

Zuko glances back, surprised. “It’s still three months away?” Unless he’s totally lost track of time, except time has been the one thing lately Zuko *can’t* seem to lose track of, no matter how much he wants to.

“I have stopped telling him that.”

Zuko cocks his head, considering. “Is that why he suggested grocery shopping the last two times we hung out?”

“Yup.”

“…Huh.”

“Yup,” Sokka repeats, popping the word extra hard this time and Zuko shrugs after another moment, setting aside for later the question of whether he should have Iroh swing by to talk to Aang about impending fatherhood and getting back to the immediate task of dragging things out of the awkward, elongated nook of their pantry. There’s more packed back here than should rightfully fit, noodles and pasta sauce, cereal boxes, *mini* cereal boxes—

“Did you steal these from a hotel?”

“…Shut up.”

—that some part of Zuko can’t help marveling at all the things they have. And in so much variety.

“If it’s a good sale you have to take advantage!” Sokka insists as Zuko pointedly sets down *another* container of peanut butter.

“And you were on *me* about buying too much of one thing,” Zuko grumbles.

“It’s all different kinds! You got your crunchy, your natural, your creamy, your—”

“Okay, okay,” Zuko huffs, smiling despite himself and quickly cutting off a potential dissertation on peanut butter varietals. “But multiple unopened jars of each?”

Sokka shrugs. “It’s a good snack.”

“Have at it, then,” Zuko says, rolling his eyes and setting all seven jars firmly on the ‘Sokka’ side of the counter.

“Not all of us want to choke down a protein bar every day,” Sokka sniffs as he gathers up the peanut butter. Probably a base layer for a new box, with the way he’s overengineering things, not that Zuko’s going to suggest he stop.

“So instead you glue your mouth shut.”

A snort. “At least peanut butter doesn’t taste like ass.”

“You’d know, would you.”

A beat of silence, then, “I’m not going to deign that with an answer.”

“Sure you aren’t,” Zuko grins, knowing Sokka will hear it in his voice.

“Shut up and sort more,” Sokka grumbles, and Zuko laughs but obligingly reaches back into the far corner of the pantry, grabbing the next thing on hand—it better not be more fucking peanut butter —and freezing as he pulls out a familiar jam jar instead.

***Four years, seven months, and five days until the end***

*The five of them stare at each other, silent, the awkwardness stretching out tight* *enough to snap—for Zuko, at least. Toph and Suki just seemed amused by the whole* *thing, but then, aren’t they always? Even the music has quieted as if to truly showcase* *the uncomfortable moment, and Zuko is actively considering whether he can duck* *under the table and out the other side fast enough to make an escape, etiquette be* *damned, when Sokka finally—shit, has it really only been ten seconds?—finds his* *words again.*

*“These things turned out well,” he says, grabbing up a centerpiece full of fairy lights* *and sprigs of greenery inside. It’s a blatant reach for a neutral topic and Zuko doesn’t* *know if he’s ever been more grateful even as he can’t help but snort.*

*“They better have,” he mutters. He didn’t eat jam three times a day for nothing. Zuko* *totally supported Aang and Katara’s DIY-as-much-as-possible approach to their* *wedding in theory, but in practic? He would have happily bought them fifty empty* *mason jars if it meant avoiding eating jam until he felt sick with it no matter how* *absurd it is that they’re more expensive than fifty identical jars of jam.*

*And when it turned out that they’d actually only needed* fifteen *jars, not fifty…Sokka* *had sent Zuko onto the roof for that one, and Zuko didn’t ask how Sokka disappeared* *all the jars they still had left.*

*“What,” Jet drawls, lips set in that little smirk of his Zuko can’t help but remember,* *eyes hooded and amused as they flick between the centerpiece and Zuko and Sokka.* *“You’ve got some design sensibilities these days?”*

*“I ate,” Zuko bites out, “A lot of jam for those centerpieces.” And yes, Zuko is aware* *that he’s being petty, but he still can’t think about peanut butter and jelly without* *feeling vaguely ill. And he certainly doesn’t need to justify it to* Jet *of all people, and* *how Katara and Aang forgot to share that they were inviting him...Not that Zuko has a* *reason to* need *them to share it, it’s just that Jet is so—*

*But the centerpieces did turn out nice. This whole wedding turned out nice, really, not* *that he ever doubted that Katara and Aang would pull it off. But Bato’s backyard has* *been transformed into something like Zuko has never seen before, something that feels* *truly magical between the tent and the dancing and the twinkling lights and the* *laughter, a wondering feeling that Zuko thinks is about more than just the occasion.*

*“Aw, poor baby,” Suki is saying without an ounce of sympathy. “You’ll recover one* *day.”*

*“*You *didn’t eat it all.” Zuko glowers, aware that his testiness is mostly just jealousy* *that* she *gets to show off the long expanse of bare leg exposed by the slit of her dress,* *but* he *can’t take* his *pants off to air out his legs without people talking about it.*

*They might have lucked out with a breezy night to cut the August heat, but that doesn’t* *mean it isn’t still* warm.

*“You’re right,” Suki says, swishing the skirt of her sleek gown as if she knows exactly* *what he’s thinking. “And it was a great decision.”*

*“Really, Sifu,” Toph rolls her eyes, fanning herself with their table number. “You* *should have just pretended to eat it and bought jars on the side. Snoozles would have* *never known.”*

*“Hey!”*

*“Snoozles?” Jet echoes, lips quirking. “Fitting.”*

*“*Hey!”

*Zuko eyes the blush suddenly staining Sokka’s cheeks and makes a mental note to* *follow up on that later because whatever makes Sokka so instantly flush like that has* *to be good. “I should have,” he agrees. He thought about it. He doesn’t know why he* *didn’t.*

*Toph snorts. “Sokka had more jam jars than he has cheesy pickup lines, Sifu, he* *wouldn’t have noticed if he ended up with some extras.”*

*“I am,” Sokka announces, sniffing, “Deeply offended by this conversation.”*

*“Too many to know how to use them, you mean,” Suki grins.*

*Sokka drops his air of snootiness to give her a dry look. “They work, don’t they?”*

*“Do they, though?” Toph says.*

*“You’re pretty alone right now,” Suki teases.*

*“Excuse me,” Sokka huffs. “I’m sitting* right *next to Zuko here! And I’ve gone out with* *two of you!”*

*“Right.” Zuko doesn’t know if it’s possible for a tone to convey any more skepticism* *than Toph is achieving right now. “Because of your pickup lines.”*

*“Because that’s not exactly how I remember it,” Jet offers with a wolfish kind of grin.* *“It was cute, though.”*

*Zuko blinks. Are Sokka and Jet…*flirting*? It’s hard to tell when they both flirt so easily* *with anything that moves, almost as a reflex. But Sokka’s is blushing* again*, cheeks* *dusted dark even as he makes a show of being indignant. And Jet* did *ditch his dress* *shirt entirely in favor of just the tank top underneath after eyeing the way Zuko and* *Sokka had theirs unbuttoned down to mid-chest—the most they thought they could get* *away with without comment. Which Zuko* thought *was just Jet being Jet, but he can’t* *deny that it’s an unfortunately good look on him…*

*“They work,” Sokka is saying, huffy, “Because of my overwhelming charisma.”*

*Which sets off another round of laughter and teasing as Sokka loudly reminds* *everything that, again, he’s gone out with half the people here.*

*“Which means there’s the whole other half who are immune to your dad puns* *disguised as come-ons,” Toph drawls.*

*Sokka snorts. “Please. They’re* refined*.”*

*“Refined like processed cheese?” Jet suggests and Suki lets out another whoop of* *laughter.*

*“Oh, that was good,” she says with her own wolfish smile, giving Jet…what is* *definitely a blatant once-over. “I like you.”*

*Jet gives her his own look, grinning around the little straw in his mouth. “I like you,* *too.”*

*“Want to dance?”*

*“I love this song.”*

*“Perfect,” Suki purrs, setting aside her water glass to grab Jet’s hand and throwing a* *wink to the table as she leads Jet to the dance floor.*

*Jet throws them a wink, too, because of course he does, and Toph immediately swings* *her feet to the ground as if she can somehow sense it, muttering something about* *wheels and cake as she disappears on their heels and, leaving Zuko and Sokka* *blinking after her.*

*Zuko…is just going to figure that one out tomorrow.*

*“And you all say* I *don’t have lines,” Sokka mutters, rolling his eyes, and Zuko can’t* *help but snort in amusement.*

*“I mean, is ‘I like you’ even a line? Might be up there with ‘want to do an activity.’”* *“Et tu?” Sokka huffs, pressing a hand to his heart in mock outrage. “The roommate of* *my own heart, betraying me?”*

*“I didn’t say anything,” Zuko says, fighting back a smile.*

*“Yeah, I noticed! You were mighty silent back there!”*

*Zuko raises his eyebrow. “What was I supposed to say then?”*

*“Uh, maybe that I do fine? I lived with you in college!”*

*“You dated Suki for three years.”*

*“Well, she said yes!” Sokka protests, eyes bright with laughter even as he makes a* *show of indignation. “And not all of that was in college!”*

*“Are you sure you did the asking?” Zuko teases. “Because it sounded like—”*

*“Oh, shut up,” Sokka huffs, giving his shoulder a shove. “You’re going to take Jet’s* *word for it? Over mine?”*

*“Jet can be pretty persuasive,” Zuko muses.*

*“Don’t I know it,” Sokka mutters, but it's amused, commiserating over falling for that* *smirk. For still thinking about falling for it sometimes, maybe. “But that doesn’t mean* *I can’t pick someone up if I wanted to.”*

*“If you wanted to.”*

*“Yeah.”*

*“Because wanting to is the only thing holding you back.”*

*“…You have no idea what you’re talking about.”*

*“No?” Zuko laughs. “I thought I was the one living with you to know. Roommate of* *your heart and everything.”*

*“Fine,” Sokka sniffs, somehow managing to both toss his hair and give Zuko a* *sideways, assessing kind of look at the same time. “I’ll prove it, then.”*

*“By what,” Zuko scoffs. “Trying to pick up another waiter in front of your dad?”*

*“Rude,” Sokka says, closing his eyes. “You make a mistake* one *time—”*

*“Twice. That I saw.”*

*“—three times, and everyone turns into a critic—”*

*“*Three times?” *Zuko laughs, but Sokka’s only reply is to flick his fingers in Zuko’s* *direction and take a deep breath, eyes still closed like…like he’s* visualizing *or* *something, and Zuko is mid-giggle when Sokka opens his eyes, their deep blue* *suddenly dark and somehow fucking* smoldering *and what—*

*Zuko feels inexplicably caught, frozen, as Sokka leans in, holding Zuko’s gaze. He* *nearly jumps when Sokka’s fingers brush down the bare skin of his bicep and trace* *down his forearm, feeling gooseflesh prickling in their wake like Sokka doesn’t touch* *him every single day. Except this his skin tingling like he’s never felt Sokka’s hands* *before, his breath catching like Sokka's never whispered in his ear as Sokka leans in* *another few inches to murmur, low and smooth, “I’d like to take you to the movies.”*

*Some distant part of Zuko’s brain is aware that even if he had any idea what to say* *he’d be speechless with the way his mouth has gone suddenly dry. More immediate* *though the fact that he suddenly can’t stop looking at the scar bisecting Sokka’s* *eyebrow, the one he’s seen before but only now feels like he’s* noticing*. Just like he’s* *suddenly noticing Sokka’s parted lips, the line of his jaw, the glint of silver on his ears* *and the sweat beading the hollow of his throat. The breadth of his shoulders and all* *the skin on display, none of it something Zuko hasn’t seen before. But something about* *it paired with rolled-up sleeves and an unbuttoned shirt and Sokka's wrists looking* *suddenly startlingly bare without bracelets, Zuko's own arm still tingling from the* *memory of Sokka’s hand—*

*“But,” Sokka whispers, swaying forward and Zuko’s throat is locked, his breath* *stilled, his heart pounding in his throat, “They don’t let you bring in your own* *snacks.”*

*Zuko stares for a long moment, trying to process what suddenly feels like too many* *things at once. Which feels like its own thing he needs to process except—except this—* *this is how he feels when—this is how his body feels—*

*Sokka suddenly lets out a laugh, flopping back on his chair, all that heat and smolder* *and intent disappearing as if it were never there in the first place except for the way* *his eyes linger on Zuko’s face and the warm curve of his grin and the way Zuko can* *still feel his skin prickling with the memory of Sokka’s touch.*

*“See!” he crows as if Zuko’s entire understanding of the world isn’t trying to realign* *itself. “You didn’t even know what to say! It works!”*

*“Yeah,” Zuko manages to get out, mustering an eye-roll and fumbling to grab Suki’s* *water glass from across the table, desperately scrambling for some scrap of* *composure in the brief seconds it gives him. “From the sheer idiocy that you would* *think that would work on someone.” Fuck, fuck, fuck, if that makes Sokka an idiot,* *what does it make him?*

*“Charisma, baby,” Sokka smirks, stretching his arms across the back of his chair, a* *familiar gesture that makes his shirt gape open even more and shit, oh shit, fuck—*

*“We sneak snacks in all the time, anyway,” Zuko blurts out, wincing as he hears* *himself. Fuck, is he becoming* tongue-tied*?*

*“Speaking of,” Sokka says, perking up as he sees something over Zuko’s shoulder.* *“That cake looks good.” Another waiter. Of course. “Want a slice?” He pops to his* *feet in a motion Zuko’s seen a thousand times before and suddenly can’t stop looking* *at because he’s an* idiot*,* fuck.

*What he wants is to roll back this conversation to when he could be vaguely fascinated* *by the idea that Sokka and Jet might be flirting, but what he says is, “Sure,” feeling* *almost dizzy as he watches Sokka leave—unable to* not *watch him leave, eyes dragging* *down his body, every line and dip and curve of it nearly as familiar as his own except* *just like that Zuko feels like he’s never seen it before and fuck, fuck, fuck.*

*This…is not good.*

*…He can’t believe that line fucking worked on him. Dammit.*

“Oh, man,” Sokka laughs as if everything is normal—but then, it’s been nearly five years of this, nearly half the time they’ve lived together, so can Zuko really say it isn’t? “You don’t even need to say it, I know that’s mine,” Sokka grins, leaning in to pluck the jar out of Zuko’s hand, and Zuko braces himself against the casual press of Sokka’s body and the heat of him through their clothes, against the absent hand on his hip like it’s nothing to touch him like this. Always like it’s nothing.

“It’s been years,” Sokka is teasing as he tucks the jar away. “I don’t know how you’re not over it by now.”

“Yeah,” Zuko says after a beat, feeling the question like a dull ache in his chest, a familiar pain he barely notices anymore except when he pokes and prods at it. “Me, too.”

“Did you ever try?” Sokka asks, curious.

“Yeah,” Zuko says again, heartfelt and briefly closing his eyes. He did, he tried so hard. He *is* trying. But fuck, it feels like part of him doesn’t know how to move past or move back or just *move* wherever he needs to go to get over it. Like he doesn't know how to exist anymore without this always at the creeping edge of his awareness and the beating center of his heart. “It didn’t work.”

“I must have missed it,” Sokka shrugs, giving a box a critical look.

“Yeah,” Zuko says, clearing his throat and reaching blindly into the cabinet again. “Must have.” And he supposes that that part of him is going to have to figure it out one way or another, now. If the distance doesn’t do what everything else he’s tried hasn’t, Sokka still won’t be there, won’t be around to reawaken and reignite the burning sparks in Zuko’s blood with every glance and grin and laugh.

“Ohh, what’s that?” Sokka asks and Zuko jolts back to himself, realizing that he’s been staring blankly at…a box of fruit gummies. “Are those fruit snacks?”

“Yeah,” Zuko says, hearing the extra hoarseness of his voice and hoping Sokka just…doesn’t notice. “I didn’t know we still had any.”

“We’re making a new pile,” Sokka says, excited. “Eat it immediately.” He plucks the box from Zuko’s hand—again, again, Zuko’s hip burning with the imprint of Sokka’s fingers through the cotton of his sweatpants—and pops it open, making a happy noise as he pulls out a little bag of gummies as if Zuko isn’t staring at him with his heart in his throat, isn’t hearing that little noise deep in his gut, as if somehow everything Zuko feels isn’t seeping past the borders of his skin, more and more each day he tries to hold it back.

“Finders get first dibs,” Sokka grins, oblivious as he holds out the little bag, and Zuko supposes he can thank five years of practice when he’s able to reach out without making a fool of himself, fingers brushing Sokka’s as he grabs a few gummies from the tiny bag and pops t into his mohemuth, the taste exploding bright and familiar over his tongue as Sokka watches him, eager.

***Six years, three months, and twenty-seven days until the end***

*Zuko is so lost in his thoughts—in his deliberate lack of thought—that he doesn’t* *notice Sokka opening the window or scrambling out onto the roof or jiggling the* *creaky pane closed behind him until he suddenly speaks.*

*“You must have a furnace in you, man,” Sokka says, Zuko startling and abruptly* *coming back into himself. “You never seem to get cold.”*

*Zuko shrugs, glancing down at his hoodie and then over at the sherpa collar of* *Sokka’s denim jacket. “Uncle always used to say I had a lot of fire in me.”*

*“I’m not sure that’s quite what he meant,” Sokka says, lips curved into a small smile* *as he settles in beside Zuko, silently offering over a little packet of fruit gummies. Zuko* *hesitates before belatedly grabbing the bag, still trying to drag himself back to a place* *where he needs to react and process. His hands are cold, he realizes as he fumbles to* *open the packet—and immediately huffs out a surprised laugh when he glances over to* *see Sokka silently holding out a pair of mittens.*

*“Thanks,” Zuko says, making himself take a deep breath of crisp, bracing air as he* *tugs them on, imagining he can inhale it all the way to his toes and trying to breathe* *out the knot in his chest on the exhale, the tension, the tight, tangled up feeling that* *he’d hoped the wide expanse of the night sky would help him release.*

*“Any time, Sifu,” Sokka says, gently knocking his shoulder against Zuko’s as he opens* *his own snack before he asks, diffident, “How’re you doing?”*

*Zuko sighs, watching the way his breath puffs out into the cold air. “I should be* *asking how* you’re *doing.”*

*“I asked first,” Sokka shrugs, and the words are casual, easy, familiar, but out of* *nowhere Zuko suddenly feels a rush of hot, sharp-edged annoyance spilling into his* *chest, the kind of thing that reminds him of middle school and those early years of* *high school and fuck, he* would *be feeling all his hard work slipping through his* *fingers bit by bit right now, as if all Father needs is fucking proximity to his* thoughts*,* *not even—*

*“You don’t have to talk about it if you don’t want to,” Sokka says, offering out another* *pack of gummies, and Zuko jolts out of his spiraling thoughts, staring at the little blue* *bag. The one that Zuko needs, because…because he furiously shoved gummies into his* *face instead of lashing out. Because he gave himself something mindless to do with his* *hands and mouth instead of digging his sharp edges into someone else, and the* *realization is enough for him to suck in a breath, and then the deliberate pause for air* *enough for Zuko to fumble for Uncle’s soothing mantra that Zuko can always control* *his actions even if he can’t control his emotions.*

*It's enough for him to realize how hungry he is, too, and how long he must have been* *up here. Normally someone drags him down by now, at least enough to shove some* *food into him, but Aang must be talking people away from that after earlier…*

*“No, it’s fine,” Zuko finally says, the words billowing like smoke against the clear* *winter sky. He’s aware that his pause suggests otherwise, but he can feel the truth of* *the words as he says them, that for everyone else it wouldn’t be, but for Sokka… “It’s* *just, what is there to say? Father is finally going on trial, which isn’t—it isn’t* bad, *even if—but the press is going crazy, and they’re all* speculating *on whether it’s true* *and what else he might have—and—and* Azula*—"*

*“You know you’re going to do just as good as her,” Sokka interrupts, the quiet words* *somehow managing to cut right through the spiral of Zuko’s thoughts.*

*“I* *wasn’t in his inner circle like she was,” he says, making a face at the old bitterness* *on his tongue even though most days he’s nothing but relieved about that fact. He can* *imagine what it would have cost—is aware of what it* did *cost—and after having a* *front-row seat to his sister slowly become more and more brittle until she broke…*

*“Maybe that’s better,” Sokka says after a pause. “Have someone with an* *unimpeachable character up there testifying.” Zuko can’t help but snort. “Not* *implicated in any of the charges, no history of mental illness—” Zuko snorts harder.* *“You watch enough legal shows,” Sokka shrugs, “You know how it is.”*

*“All those shows are just made up for the drama,” Zuko huffs, rolling his eyes.*

*“You know what I mean,” Sokka says, nudging him, a gentle bump that somehow* *seems to knock loose the last of Zuko’s control against all the things that have been* *boiling and bubbling in his stomach, the things he hasn’t been able to shape into* *words, to say to anyone but himself, not even Uncle, not even Aang.*

*“I should have* known*,” Zuko bursts out, tight, feeling the words shoving up too fast to* *be entirely sure of his coherency but suddenly just needing them* out*. “That it would be* *too much for her, I should have—”*

*Sokka exhales a slow, steady cloud into the cold air, but he doesn’t say anything, lets* *Zuko be the one to fill the space.*

*“She’s been so strong, and doing so well and I should have—if the trial stuff was* *weighing on* me. *And then—fuck, we were talking about it and I fucking* said *I didn’t* *want to testify, I* said *it, that I didn’t—I didn’t know if I could do it. Sit in the room* *with him and not—and she said that it was* okay*. That she could do it well enough for* *both of us. And I was* relieved, *I didn’t—I didn’t even* think*, I should have—”*

*“There’s no way you could have known,” Sokka cuts in again, the words firm, soft,* *implacable.*

*“But it was so* obvious*,” Zuko says, feeling his chest heaving too fast as guilt and* *regret twist around his spine. “How did I not see it?”*

*“Because Azula had years of being really good at hiding that kind of thing?” Sokka* *says gently, and Zuko can’t exactly refute that. “Because you aren’t omniscient? Or a* *mind reader?” Another shoulder bump, just like the others but somehow feeling like a* *reassurance that Zuko doesn’t quite know how to let himself accept. “It’s not your job* *to make Azula’s choices for her.”*

*“I just…” Zuko blows out a hard breath, tilting his head back toward the sky and* *letting out the plaintive, tight words, “She was doing so* well*.”*

*“And she will again,” Sokka says, voice suddenly intent. Maybe realizing that this is* *the thing Zuko’s truly been tangled up over, beyond the trial and the reality of Ozai—* *his failure to* see*. “She’s strong and she has help. Family and friends, and she already* *has a therapist.” Sokka taps the back of his hand, light, grabbing his attention. “She* *checked herself into the hospital, Zuko. She didn’t need to get dragged in this time. It’s* *already not the same. It’s not going to* be *the same.”*

*There’s nothing but confidence in his voice and Zuko* wants *to believe it, he does, he* *just…He sucks in a shaky breath, feeling the hot prick of tears.*

*“It’s not going to be easy,” Sokka allows, “But it never is, right? And it’s not going to* *be like last time. I promise.”*

*“How can you know, though,” Zuko whispers, hating how small his voice sounds, how* *needy he suddenly feels for someone to make him believe that it will be okay.*

*“Well,” Sokka says after a long beat, “This time she made the news because everyone* *is wondering where she went and not because of the wreckage. So.” Zuko lets out a* *surprised bark of laughter. “That’s already better.”*

*Zuko breaths out another noise that might be a laugh if it weren’t quite so suddenly* *choked, sighing at the end of the sound and then just…sitting with Sokka’s words, and* *his confidence, and his presence, and letting them all bolster him up. And hearing in* *them the echo of Uncle’s words that Zuko found so hard to let himself believe, before,* *but now hearing someone else say it…*

*Sokka and Uncle can’t both be wrong. Right?*

*Zuko isn’t sure how long it is before he finally stirs, letting thoughts of breaking apart* *and coming back together, of trials and testimony and Fathers and parents wisp away* *from him, just like he’d hoped when he first came onto the roof. He blows out another* *long breath, staring at it fading into the night sky before pulling up his knees to rest* *his cheek against them, eyes tracing over Sokka’s familiar profile, lingering on the* *little pricks of starshine reflected in the dark of his eyes. “And you?”*

*“Hm?” Sokka stirs, giving him a questioning look, blinking light he was lost in his* *own thoughts right along with Zuko.*

*“Your turn now,” Zuko says. He doesn’t know that he can say much more about this* *right now, not when it feels like he’s maybe found a moment of fragile equilibrium, but* *he knows if he just sits and thinks he won’t be able to stop himself from coming back to* *it. “How are you?”*

*“I’m…fine,” Sokka says, making a face like he already knows that won’t stand on its* *own. “It hurts, you know? Seeing her and she’s kind of the same. Like it doesn’t—* *didn’t—matter if we’re dating or not.”*

*“But,” Zuko prompts after a moment, seeing all the pent-up words in the fidgeting of* *Sokka’s fingers.*

*“…But I’m thinking,” Sokka frowns, picking his words slowly and fiddling with the* *zipper of his jacket, “That maybe I’m the same, too.”*

*Zuko opens his mouth and then closes it again, swallowing back his immediate denial* *as he thinks over Sokka’s behavior of the last three weeks. The stunned, upset frown* *that’d disappeared into every distraction Zuko’s thrown at him. The moody silence* *that breaks into laughter at the smallest of opportunities. The total absence of the* *ceiling-staring blankness Zuko remembers from when Mai broke up with him, and no* *sign of the frustration of the smaller breakups since, the annoyances of time wasted, or* *silly miscommunications, or stupid things blown up into big ordeals.*

*An overall demeanor that isn’t* great*, but that’s also…fine.*

*Sokka sighs into the silence, flopping back against the slope of the roof. “Maybe she* *was right that we’re basically just friends who happened to have sex,” he says, face* *twisted with discontent at the idea.*

*“You guys were more than just fuck buddies,” Zuko protests. “You cared about her.”* *It was obvious in every gesture and comment and enthusiastic greeting, in the way* *Sokka lit up at even the thought of her, in the way just saying her name makes—made* *—him grin.*

*“Yeah,” Sokka agrees after a beat, shrugging. “But I still care, you know?”*

*“It takes a while for feelings like that to fade,” Zuko points out. Emotions don’t just* *turn off at the drop of a dime, regardless of how convenient it would be if they did.*

*“Except,” Sokka says slowly, eyes fixed on the sky and restless fingers now fidgeting* *with the edges of his pockets, “It feels like I care about her the same way I care about* *you.
