Like the only thing that’s changed is that we won’t hold hands and always sit next* *to each other anymore. And the sex,” he adds, belated, and Zuko raises his eyebrow.*

*“That was a little delayed there.”*

*Sokka purses his lips, silent.*

*“…Sokka.”*

*Sokka clears his throat.*

*“*Sokka*,” Zuko says, twisting around to fully face him. “No! Come on, really?”*

*“We aren’t!” Sokka protests, covering his face with his hands. “We—just—the one* *time, okay? And maybe almost—but it was a mistake! We both knew it, we just…”*

*“Needed to get it out of your system?” Zuko fills in when Sokka suddenly trails off,* *visibly sagging.*

*“Or needed confirmation, maybe,” Sokka says into his hands, glum.*

*Zuko swallows back the immediate litany of you-know-better-come-on-mans and takes* *a moment to think through the idea of confirmation, of what exactly Sokka and Suki* *might have felt the need to confirm on the heels of a three-year relationship. “So,”* *Zuko finally says, slow, “You’re not out here because you needed to get away from* *hanging out with her?”*

*Sokka huffs, dropping his hands but still staring up at the sky. “Maybe? Or hey,” he* *says, giving Zuko a sideways little smile, almost hopeful, and nudging him with his* *knee, “Maybe I just wanted to spend some time with you, how about. It’s been crazy* *lately.”*

*Zuko makes a sound that might be a laugh under better conditions, because fuck, has it* *ever. “You see me every day.”*

*“Not like this,” Sokka says softly, eyes drifting back to the stars, and Zuko hums* *because he isn’t wrong. It’s different than the day-to-day, being like this. Having the* *space and the time to let things out that Zuko can let out to so few people. And Zuko’s* *missed it, too, being able to just* be *with Sokka. And there’s no reason not to take* *advantage of the moment now that he has it.*

*Zuko lays back too, settling himself alongside Sokka, giving him a nudge that he hopes* *conveys support and togetherness and affection and thankfulness and everything else* *that’s easing the tension of his shoulders. And Sokka shoots him a quick little smile, so* *Zuko thinks he must have done a decent job of it.*

*He’s happy to let his thoughts drift, to mediate to the sight of their slowly syncing* *breaths mingling together above them, to listen when Sokka points out a rising star,* *and then another guiding one. He’s content to follow along as Sokka traces the shapes* *of constellations and eventually murmurs the stories behind them, as he leans over so* *Zuko can follow the line of his finger—and maybe so he can steal some of Zuko’s* *warmth, judging by how often he does it.*

*“They just look like stars to me,” Zuko admits, sheepish, as Sokka finishes explaining* *Orion and his belt. “I can never see the shapes.”*

*Sokka groans, elbowing him gently in the side. “Sifu Hotman, you’ve got poetry in that* *big, burning soul of yours. You can do it.”*

*“I don’t know, man,” Zuko says, glancing over at Sokka’s profile and lips quirking as* *he takes in the flush of cold over his cheek and the soft, easy expression on his face.* *“Sounds like you might have enough for both of us.”*

The taste of them is as familiar and everyday as something like fruit gummies could be, and Zuko sucks in a slow, deliberately steady breath as it hits him. His hands don’t shake even a bit as he reaches for another, his return smile coming right on cue and his replies falling automatic off his lips in response to whatever comment Sokka makes that Zuko’s ears are buzzing too loud to hear, that his brain is too busy to retain. He feels suddenly overwhelmed by a rush of simultaneous affection and grief for this man in front of him because Zuko knows it could never be nothing. That even when it wasn’t something, it was never nothing.

Or maybe it’s that it’s always been something, there even before Zuko realized it. And it’s Zuko who that something suddenly wasn’t enough for anymore. Zuko who started to want it could be something else, something it wasn’t. And he can’t blame Sokka for that, could never truly be upset with him no matter how much he might wish things could be different. Because Zuko’s the one who suddenly found that Sokka’s friendship wasn’t enough when it’s been one of the most amazing, consistent things in his life. And it's Zuko who couldn’t find a way, no matter how hard he tried, to just keep being happy with what he—what they—had.

***One day and seventeen hours until the end***

“That’s not lunch!” Sokka shouts, sliding in his socks as he scrambles around the table.

“It’s *food*.” Zuko skids to a stop on the other side, holding his body tense and ready, panting a little as he fixes his gaze on Sokka’s torso and looks for the little twitches that will signal which way Sokka is going to break.

“But it’s not *lunch*,” Sokka says, darting right, Zuko immediately mirroring the motion. Zuko grins, baring his teeth, as Sokka grabs onto the table to halt his momentum again, his eyes wide and laughing as he scrambles back toward the center of the table.

“You just drank condensed milk with chocolate for breakfast,” Zuko scoffs, slowly shifting his weight back to center and waiting for the moment when Sokka is going to realize that he can’t go left because of how tight the turn is.

“That’s different!” Sokka protests, his eyes darting in little flicks as he assesses the situation, and Zuko knows Sokka, he can see the wheels turning and he knows that Sokka’s going to brush the hair falling into his face back at the same time he glances left which is going to briefly obscure his vision so Zuko can—*there.*

Sokka lets out a noise that can only be classified as a shriek as Zuko comes *over* the table. Sokka tries to dash to the right but he’s laughing too much already, socked feet sliding and giving him no traction, and it’s easy for Zuko to hook him around the waist and ignore the heat of skin under his palm and the reality of that too-small shirt as he yanks Sokka back into his body.

“It’s the exact same thing,” Zuko pants, breathless as he tries to corral Sokka’s squirming enough to get one of the gummies in question into his mouth.

“It’s different,” Sokka mumbles through tight-pressed lips, wriggling and twisting and throwing around his weight and continuously making Zuko need to grapple for a better grip.

“*How?”* Zuko grunts as Sokka suddenly becomes deadweight, Zuko just managing to shift for better leverage around Sokka’s waist to drag him back up again.

“It just *is*,” Sokka giggles, contorting to hide his face against Zuko’s shoulder, the words warm against Zuko’s skin in a way that nearly makes him lose his hold entirely.

“That’s not an answer,” Zuko growls, bracing himself in anticipation just before Sokka suddenly throws his weight back, Sokka letting out a shout of laughter when Zuko doesn’t even sway. He throws his weight forward when Zuko hauls him back in, crashing into Zuko’s body and somehow managing to end up with his mouth pressed against Zuko’s ribs, apparently laughing too hard to keep it closed against the candy, the sound of it sparkling through Zuko’s vein’s and the feel of his breath soaking through Zuko’s shirt doing something else entirely.

“It *looks* more like lunch food than that looked like breakfast,” Zuko gets out, somehow managing to hold on to his train of thought as he maneuvers Sokka into a headlock braced against the side of his torso, squishing Sokka’s face against his stomach as he tries to get the gummy into his mouth again.

“No!” Sokka squawks, laughing and turning his face into Zuko’s hip to defend his mouth, the words muffled against Zuko’s sweatpants and seeping through the fabric to Zuko’s skin. “Assault! This is assault!”

“What’s assault is that you made us buy it!” And Zuko has wrestled with Sokka enough to know the feel of him playfully squirming or genuinely trying to escape, has seen Sokka fend off enough of Katara’s headlocks to know he wouldn’t have let himself get into one in the first place unless he felt like it, and Zuko’s whole body feels like its thrumming with that knowledge. That awareness and the thrill of the impromptu game and the adrenaline and *fuck*, having Sokka pressed up against him like this because Sokka wants to be there.

“You let me buy it!” Sokka accuses, his fingers suddenly digging into the ticklish spot along Zuko’s ribs and Zuko can’t help but jerk away with a startled laugh, Sokka immediately worming free and dashing out from behind the table into the open space of the kitchen.

“*You* spent twenty-five dollars on a full-size gummy cheeseburger,” Zuko says, eyes narrowing as Sokka gives him a big, flushed grin, his wolf tail a disaster and half the hoops in his ear flipped upside down and his blue eyes intent and laughing as he reaches down to tug off his socks one by one in a gesture Zuko can’t help but linger on. Fucking *socks*. “Now you have to eat it.”

“You didn’t tell me no,” Sokka says, slowly backing away as Zuko starts staking out from behind the table, keeping the distance between them and not even noticing or caring that his t-shirt has ridden up almost to his ribs. “*You* eat it.”

“I didn’t—” Zuko scowls, brandishing a gummy pickle and barely able to contain incredulous laughter enough to sell his outrage, “What are you, a child?”

“I need to be contained!” Sokka takes a quick glance down to navigate a set of boxes, Zuko shifting to the right to encourage Sokka to keep backing up left. “You know this!”

“You *said* you *wanted it.*”

“I said it looked interesting! You didn’t disagree!”

“That doesn’t mean *buy it*.” Zuko takes advantage of another of those downward glances to shorten the distance between them, grinning as startled delight flashes over Sokka’s face when he looks back up to see it.

“Then,” Sokka says taking a few quick steps back like Zuko knew he would and letting out a little noise of surprise when his back hits the counter and *fuck*, the way he grabs onto the edge and *leans* *back*, like he’s fucking inviting Zuko to— “You should have said that!”

***Three years, five months, and seventeen days until the end***

*The clamor of the grocery store spills out of the automatic doors and Zuko* *immediately breaks right for a cart, Sokka making a beeline to the dairy aisle for eggs* *and milk because they both know city weekend shopping and they’ve both been* *without one too many times to make that mistake again.*

*They meet back up in the produce section like always, Zuko already working on the* *fruit and Sokka glancing briefly into the cart before taking vegetables as if Zuko might* *have decided that today was the day to switch it up for the first time ever.*

*“Are we doing a green curry?” Sokka asks as he contemplates the produce display.* *“Or the yellow?”*

*Zuko pictures the contents of their cabinets. “Yellow.*

*Sokka hums an acknowledgment and reaches for the yellow squash, just like Zuko* *knew he would. As Sokka is always stating, thematic consistency is important—and* *Zuko can admit that he has become something more of a creature of habit than he ever* *thought he could be, himself. But why change up something that works? He and Sokka* *both know what goes into a yellow curry or a green just like they both know the* *shopping list, the store layout, the aisles that get picked over first or clogged with* *carts. It’s nice to be able to divide and conquer without words—which means that the* *chatter is purely because they want it.*

*“The chicken thighs look kind of measly,” Zuko frowns, Sokka setting down some* *premade breakfast scramble thing he’s been contemplating with far too much interest* *to come over.*

*“Do quarters,” he says, reaching for the package. “These will be better.”*

*“We need to break them down, though,” Zuko says. Or adjust the recipes to deal with* *bone-in, skin-on chicken, which is perfectly doable. It’s just the cook time and the* *crispy skin consideration in a curry…*

*Sokka bumps Zuko’s shoulder, scoffing. “I’ll do it for you in like thirty seconds.”*

*Zuko gives him a sideways look, raising his eyebrow. “Speed demon, are we?”*

*A flashing grin as Sokka goes to look at the breakfast stuff again. “You can time me,* *hot stuff.”*

*It’s a familiar, easy kind of rhythm, everything about it normal, barely even worth* *thought let alone comment. Except for the fact that Sokka is remarkably upbeat and* *cheerful—bopping along to the music, exclaiming over snacks and darting to look at* *end displays, disappearing down the pasta aisle and coming back with some fruity* *cereal and memories of eating it as a kid and didn’t Zuko eat it too?—for someone* *who just had a breakup.*

*“It was because of work,” Sokka explains, leaning against the front of the cart in* *automatic balance to Zuko propped against the handlebar as they wait in line—which* *is also familiar. Sokka refuses to grocery shop on a Saturday and can never wake up* *for a Sunday morning, which means Sunday afternoons along with everyone else in the* *city, a fact that Zuko has resigned himself to at this point unless he wants to shop* *alone.*

*“Still, breakups aren’t easy,” Zuko points out. And it’s not exactly the first time* *Sokka’s been dumped because of his over-commitment to the office.*

*“Says the man who got over what’s-his-face, Jason, in a hot second,” Sokka says,* *amused, his attention catching on a bright display that the line winds through* *designed to hook people exactly like Sokka.*

*…Fair. “It’s easier when you’re doing the breaking up,” Zuko admits, studying* *Sokka’s profile, looking for any thinness to the edges of his smile, for any cracks in his* *cheer or agitation in the beat to the jingly pop song he’s tapping out against the cart.* *He* seems *fine—but he seemed fine after Suki, too, and Zuko is still skeptical about that* *one. He’s more than familiar with the way that Sokka seeming okay doesn’t* *necessarily mean that he actually is.*

*“Ain’t that the truth,” Sokka says, wry, dropping something shiny into the cart, and* *Zuko for once doesn’t immediately snatch it back out, caught on the realization that…* *Sokka must have been the one to end it, then. And with it a rush of awareness that this* *is the first time he and Sokka have both been single since the wedding, both been* *single and not nursing a bruised heart and it’s such a stupid, useless thing to dwell on* *but so hard to* quiet *and Zuko can* feel *the sudden temptation to read into the warmth* *in Sokka’s eyes, to make something out of the way his hand had brushed across Zuko’s* *lower back on the way out the door today, to—*

*Zuko blinks as another something lands in the cart and sends a cereal box toppling* *over. Looks like they’re buying chocolate nut clusters, now, too.*

*“Besides,” Sokka adds, brandishing some probably unfortunately flavored lip balm off* *the shelf, “How could I be sad about it when it means I’ve got time to do things like* *this again?”*

*Meaning the grocery store. Meaning shopping with Zuko. Meaning going at the exact* *wrong time of day and spending the majority of the trip shooting the shit in line, the* *words delivered with a sideways smile that’s flirtatious and teasing and full of so much* *genuine affection that can a moment Zuko can’t breathe around the stab of it in his* *chest, sweet and painful.*

*“Okay, so,” Sokka is saying, like Zuko isn’t quietly reeling from the force of his smile,* *“I was thinking we could make the curry right when we get back.”*

*“That’s early,” he manages to point out without even a pause or a wobble because* *Zuko has more experience than he’d ever admit to in pretending to be unaffected by* *those kinds of looks from Sokka.*

*“Yeah, but we can binge that period comedy you like—what’s it called, with the girl?* *And the traveling show?” Zuko can’t believe he knows exactly which one Sokka* *means. “The new season is coming out soon, we gotta refresh so we’re ready.”*

*“We can just watch the first few episodes and cook in the middle.”*

*“Except have you ever wanted to stop in the middle?” Sokka asks, arch, sorting* *through a bin of hair ornaments the line is inching past.*

*“…Okay, sure,” Zuko says, because that isn’t* wrong*. “Can we get a quick working in* *beforehand, though? You didn’t come to the gym this morning.” Sokka snorts, which* *Zuko knows is for the idea of waking up at seven in the morning in general and on a* *Sunday in particular. “We can go for a run, something quick. Work up an appetite.”*

*“I always love working up an appetite with you, hotman,” Sokka says, the words* *teasing and on the edge of suggestive and Zuko rolls his eyes because he knows it* *doesn’t mean anything. “And oh, what kind of appetite we’d need for* this,*” Sokka* *adds as the line turns the corner, looking delighted as he admires the end display* *before grabbing…some kind of candy monstrosity?*

*“What,” Zuko asks as Sokka thrusts out the—the* thing. *“Is* that*.”*

*“A giant life-sized hamburger made of gummies,” Sokka reads off the package, as if* *that’s something that should exist at all let alone something to look so awed in the face* *of.*

*“…Right.”*

*“With that soccer player on it, the one in all the commercials. See?” Sokka flips the* *packaging around so Zuko can, indeed, see. “It’s his new specialty sandwich.”*

*That is…the most disturbing marketing campaign Zuko has ever heard of. “Someone* *thought this was a good idea,” he says, poking tentatively at the packaging. It’s all* *rubbery and it fucking presses* back*.*

*“It looks interesting,” Sokka decides, hefting what has to be a truly unconscionable* *amount of food dye for all the colors to be—*that*.*

*“It looks like something,” Zuko mutters, eyeing it. Indigestion in solid form, a curse* *upon the unwary. Things like that.*

*Sokka gives the bun a thoughtful squish. “It’s twenty-five dollars.”*

*Zuko can’t tear his eyes away as the gummy immediately bounces back to its previous* *shape.*

*“It’s got that dude’s face on it.”*

*Zuko shifts his incredulous stare to Sokka. “You don’t even know his* name*?”*

*“Don’t need to know his name to like his commercials,” Sokka grins, tossing the giant* *gummy into the cart like that decides it.*

*Zuko eyes it askance. “Are you really going to eat that?”*

*“Zuko,* that *isn’t the question to be asking!”*

*Zuko knows he’s being set up, he knows, but, “Oh yeah? Then what is?”*

*“The real question is—” Sokka sweeps his hand through the air. “—how can we pass* *up this* opportunity*?”*

*“…Opportunity.”*

*“Hot stuff, how often do you see real-size gummy food replicas in your life?”*

*One time too many. But Sokka is giving Zuko his most winningest smile, leaning in* *over his folded arms and looking into Zuko’s eyes, and, well. He did just have a* *breakup. Even if he doesn’t seem to broken up over it…*

*How could Zuko say no?*

“Oh,” Zuko growls, slowing his approach now that Sokka has nowhere to go and shit, is he imagining the way Sokka leans back just a bit more? Was Sokka always up on his toes? To shove his weight back, or maybe to lengthen the line of his legs, and that *shirt*— “Am I supposed to specifically tell you all the things you aren’t supposed to buy?” Another step closer and something like anticipation flashing over Sokka’s face and Zuko can feel his heart racing. “Don’t buy every latte on the menu, Sokka,” Zuko says with another step. “Don’t buy every car you see, Sokka.” Another, and his voice is more like a purr than a growl but he can’t seem to stop it. “Don’t buy,” he says, setting his hand on the counter next to Sokka’s, close enough that it would only take the barest of movement for their fingers to brush, “Novelty gummy lunch food *from the grocery store* *checkout lane.”*

“Yes!” Sokka says, swaying back a bit so he can grin up into Zuko’s eyes, that *shirt* pulled tight across his chest and fuck, does he have any idea what he looks like right now? “Clearly the answer is yes! I bought a gummy hamburger from the checkout lane, clearly I need to be told!”

“Well, this is me telling you, then,” Zuko says, lifting up the alarmingly green gummy pickle again and trying not to let his mind spin down thoughts of murmuring things like *open up*, of pressing Sokka’s mouth open with his thumb, of pushing the gummy through Sokka’s parted lips, because none of that is what’s happening right now no matter what it feels like Sokka is playing at, “Now you have to eat it.”

“Absolutely not,” Sokka huffs, suddenly dropping back down to the floor to duck under Zuko’s arm and spin out into the open kitchen, nearly tripping over a box in the process—which is a small miracle, considering the way Zuko needs to temporarily throw all his effort into stopping himself from sinking into the skin-memory of Sokka briefly brushing against him chest to chest and hip to hip and thigh to—

“This is taking eating down the kitchen too far,” Sokka proclaims as he finds his balance again, lips twitching into a smile even as he puts on a haughty tone.

“You little shit, I *knew* you weren’t going to—” Zuko lunges for Sokka and just skims the fabric of his pants as Sokka darts back, laughing, and ignores the way his fingers tingle. “If you think I’m going to cook another thing in this kitchen while this gummy hamburger still exists—”

“I can cook my own food,” Sokka huffs, rolling his eyes. “I’m not *that* bad.”

Zuko raises his eyebrow and gives a pointed look to the haphazard pile of ingredients that hasn’t been packed away yet before making another sudden grab for Sokka. He gets a handful of hip and nearly loses his balance in the process as Sokka darts *toward* him instead of away, crashing into Zuko and grabbing onto his shoulders and biceps and waist to try to swing around him, their limbs tangling as Sokka’s socked feet nearly take them both down before Sokka manages to get his footing—via grabbing onto Zuko’s thigh, *how*—and duck free again.

“Fine,” Sokka huffs as he back peddles away, dodging their pile of still-unsorted cookbooks. “But you have to share it!”

“What?” Zuko nearly pulls up short. “*I* didn’t buy it.”

“But you’re at least fifty percent responsible for it being in the house.”

Zuko makes a noise of outrage, nearly stumbling over the corner of a box. “How!”

“You didn’t stop me,” Sokka says, prim and looking far too pleased with himself as he ticks the point off his finger. “And you haven’t thrown it away yet.”

“Pfft. That’s all?” Ridiculous. “Twenty-five percent at best.”

“So you eat a quarter of this thing, then,” Sokka says far too readily, which means Zuko came in way too high. Shit, he should have gone with ten percent to start. “And then we get normal food.”

Zuko eyes the somehow both electric and muddy green gummy in his hand, then the upsettingly large package on the counter. “It’s probably expired.”

“It never expires.”

“Like that’s a selling point.”

“I didn’t hold onto it for three years just to throw it out at the last minute,” Sokka says, folding his arms, jaw setting with a rare stubbornness. “Besides, we’re eating down the kitchen, right?” he adds, face flashing with triumph. “Because we don’t want food waste.”

Zuko glares and folds his arms back. “I hate you.”

“You love me.”

“I *really* hate you.”

“Whatever you need to tell yourself, hot cheek,” Sokka grins, darting for the counter as Zuko blinks, momentarily off balance—*hot cheeks*? Where did *that* come from?

“See,” he says, taking the gummy burger out of the package and apparently utterly cheerful about it now that he has someone to share in the misery with. “I knew we’d eat it!”

“I am going to throw you out that window,” Zuko grumbles as he reluctantly accepts his plate— his, *his*, how did he fucking agree to this.

“Sure you are,” Sokka grins, hopping up to sit on the counter and raising his horrifyingly large pile of gummies in a toast. “Bottoms up.”

Zuko glares, but begrudgingly offers his slice of burger in return before taking a cautious bite. They might as well be in it together…

The kitchen turns to silence as they nibble, Zuko doing his best not to think about what he’s eating and a contemplative frown slowly settling over Sokka’s face as he tries all the layers together, then each separate gummy, testing patty and bun and cheese and pickle and tomato as if there’s any different to them besides shape and color.

“Amazing,” Sokka finally says, looking at the gummy burger in wonder.

“Yeah.” Zuko gives his portion a suspicious look.

“It takes like absolutely nothing.”

“Not even sugar.” It’s unsettling.

“It’s like,” Sokka says around another considering bite, “Chewing on squishy air.”

“How do you do that by accident,” Zuko says, feeling a reluctant kind of wonder. “A gummy that doesn’t taste like *anything?*”

“Incredible,” Sokka says, shaking his head. “I think this might be the best purchase I ever made.”

He lets out a squawk and another laugh, the rest of his gummy burger flying out of his hand, as Zuko unceremoniously shoves him off the counter.

***One day and sixteen hours until the end***

“This has to be the weirdest set of *stuff* to ever put under a sink,” Sokka mutters, the words muffled with his head and for some reason a third of his torso inside the cabinet. Muscle memory from all his time tackling Katara’s sink, maybe.

“I didn’t realize there was a standard,” Zuko says, leaning back against the counter and unable to entirely stop himself from admiring the view. Normally he might be able to fix *friends* in his mind, to remind himself that leering is generally a frowned-upon activity. But fuck, it’s just—it’s only *looking.* At Sokka’s sweatpants shaping his ass and hips and thighs, sure. At the dip of his lower back revealed by that shirt riding up, yeah. But it’s not *doing* anything. And the memory of Sokka purposefully lingering in his arms a few hours ago—because that’s exactly what Sokka had been doing, even if to him it was just a game—is still so fresh. And Sokka’s been so *touchy* today, and Zuko still can’t get *hot cheeks* out of his head because what *even*, and it’s all just…

It’s a lot.

“Okay, dishwasher stuff, yes, but cleaning supplies?” Sokka is saying, his incredulity coming through loud and clear even as his voice echoes around the cabinet. “A duster?”

“That’s under there,” Zuko points out. Which should be obvious. Sokka is probably shoving his face into it with the way he’s squirmed under there.

“Yeah, and rubber bands?” Sokka’s whole back flexes in a truly fascinating way as he cranes his arm to hold the container up behind him. “And is this a *stapler*?” It is. “A hole punch?”

“I mean.” Zuko tugs the tie out of his hair, pulling it back into a new loose bun. “That’s what happens when you say there’s no room for a desk and I work for two years at the kitchen table?”

“That was like three apartments ago! Whatever happened to move out clean outs?”

“Office supplies are useful,” Zuko says, mostly because he knows it will rile Sokka up.

And sure enough, Sokka wiggles out from underneath the sink to pop up onto his knees, slightly mussed and all indignant and fuck if it isn’t a good look on him and fuck if Zuko doesn’t know better than to think things like that, just like he knows better than to let himself admire Sokka’s ass. But where’s the harm at this point, when the time they have left is only—

“Zuko. This is construction paper.”

Zuko shrugs, shunting those thoughts to the side with the ease of long practice. “The nonprofit was weird sometimes. Not all of us can be a fancy—” he waves a hand at Sokka’s general dishevelment. “—whatever you do.”

Sokka snorts, because he knows full well that Zuko knows exactly what he does when Zuko hears about it every day. “But you’ll be a big fancy lawyer soon, writing all your briefs on construction paper with what, glitter pens? And—"

He cuts off as Zuko crouches down next to him, Zuko raising his eyebrow and holding eye contact —dangerous, dangerous, there’s ‘where’s the harm’ and then there’s ‘fan the flames’—as he reaches past Sokka to pull out a pack of shiny gel pens. The one with Sokka’s name printed neatly across the front.

Zuko dangles them between them, pointed, and ignores the fact that it takes Sokka a moment to tug his eyes aways away from Zuko’s to focus on what’s in front of his face.

“So you *have* been using my gel pens!”

“Just the fun colors,” Zuko assures him, making his voice as earnest as he can.

Sokka gives him a mock-dire look, grumbling as he ducks his head back under the counter, “If I knew *this* kind of sabotage was what was going to come of our ‘no desk in our shoebox living room’ policy…”

“However will your poor heart recover,” Zuko murmurs, settling on the floor by Sokka’s legs now that he’s down here.

“I don’t know, but you better make it up to me real good.” Zuko refuses to let himself run with that. “For example, *some* organization—”

“It’s organized perfectly fine.” Zuko is endlessly amused by the areas of Sokka’s life where organization suddenly becomes important. Everywhere except his bedroom floor, t feels like. “There’s two sections, cleaning and office.”

“And *bathroom*? These are bath bombs, Zuko,” Sokka laughs when Zuko hesitates, wiggling back out to display the soft pink globes. “Not what, gigantic dishwasher pods?”

***One year, ten months, and eight days until the end***

*“Oh, thank fuck,” Zuko mutters as he winds his way back to his table to see that a* *waiter has filled up the water glasses while everyone was out on the dance floor,* *gratefully draining his and then Sokka’s, too, before dropping into a chair to flex out* *his feet—new dress shoes, a mistake and he knew it, but he needed ones that would go* *with the tux—and watch the dance floor.*

*It feels like the entire wedding is out there, those who can’t dance dragging chairs* *over to the side of the floor to join in the energy, the whole room full of an overflowing* *kind of joy that is exactly what Zuko wanted for Mai and Ty Lee’s wedding. The two of* *them are resplendent in white and red at the heart of the crowd, dynamic and laughing* *as they whirl around each other, dancing by themselves or with friends but with eyes* *always for each other.*

*Mai’s face is radiant, bright with happiness and affection as she looks at Ty Lee, and* *Zuko can’t help but smile to see it showing so openly, the only thing that can pull his* *eye away the spectacle that Suki and Sokka are making as they dance, twirling and* *jumping and dipping—Sokka being the one dipped most often, of course—like only two* *very athletic people who know each other’s bodies very well can.*

*“Why are you hiding in the corner, Sifu,” Toph asks from behind his shoulder, ruffling* *his hair as she drops a plate of dessert in front of him before flopping into her own* *chair.*

*“I’m not hiding,” Zuko says, kicking out another chair for her to prop her feet onto* *and scraping his hair back up into a high bun. “I’m just…”*

*“Being by yourself?”*

*Zuko shrugs. He knows she’ll hear it.*

*“Saving up your stamina for later?”*

*He rolls his eyes at that. As if he has anything to save his stamina for right now. As if* *he’d need to hold energy in reserve even if he did, with how much there is in the room* *right now to lift them all up.*

*“Hm.” She takes a bite of cake, pursing her lips as she taps the fork against the table.* *“Pining from afar, then.”*

*It’s not a question, and Zuko feels himself go utterly still, hand frozen halfway to* *another water glass, breath stuttering, his heart suddenly pounding so loud in his ears* *it momentarily overpowers the music. Did she—he must have misheard—how—*

*“Are you ever going to tell him, Zuko?” Toph sighs, and Zuko doesn’t know what to* *do with the rare use of his name, with her tone, except to force himself to suck in a* *breath and be grateful that she can’t see the way his gaze is inexorably drawn back to* *the dance floor. He suddenly feels so achingly overexposed, so unexpectedly stripped* *naked, all the careful walls he thought he’d built up around himself smashed with just* *a few words.*

*“You’re not going to say anything?” she finally says into the silence, fork tapping* *once again before she presses it still. “You’re not going to deny it?”*

*Zuko can’t help letting out a short, mirthless laugh. “Like you’d believe me?”*

*“At least you’re not a total idiot,” she mutters, but the words are somehow gentle,* *apologetic, and it's Zuko’s turn to sigh.*

*“You won’t say anything, will you?” he asks, low, feeling itchy and restless and trying* *not to twitch as Toph’s silence draws out longer and longer, finally snatching up the* *pink ball thing that seems to the table favor so he can toss it from hand to hand. His* *chest is churning, the kind of seething that used to make him look for a fight back in* *high school, and if he doesn’t move he thinks he’s going to bolt and fuck, the last thing* *he needs to add to this is to being seen fleeing Mai’s wedding.*

*“I haven’t yet, have I?” she says after a long beat, matching his tone. Which somehow* *doesn’t actually make Zuko feel any better.*

*“Thanks,” he manages, wincing at how raspy the word comes out. He wonders just* *how long Toph has known for. Just how long he’s been as transparent to her as he’s* *feared he must be to everyone around him, some days.*

*“You don’t want to tell him?” Toph asks, cocking her head in his direction, skeptical.* *“Wouldn’t it be good for him to know for sure?” As if it’s as simple as ‘knowing’ or* *‘not knowing.’ As if it could stop there. As if this is the kind of knowledge a person* *can just have, the kind of knowledge that doesn’t require* action.

*“So what, so he can feel sorry for me?” Zuko asks, turning the ball in his hands, eyes* *drifting back out over the dance floor. “So I can make things weird? We’re so—” He* *forces himself to take a deep, even breath. “We’re so close, Toph. I don’t—I can’t ruin* *that.”*

*A long silence, then, like she’s picking her words slowly, “That’s what you think will* *happen?”*

*“It could happen.” Some nights the threat of it feels like all Zuko can think about,* *lying awake staring blindly into the darkness.*

*“So what, you’re just going to pretend that everything is the same?”*

*Zuko swallows back the inappropriate urge to laugh until he cries. “Everything* is *the* *same,” he rasps, and the fact that it’s true, that this is just how things are between* *them now for Zuko— “We treat each other the same as we always did. We act the* *same as we always did. It’s fine.”*

*“Fine,” she echoes, wrinkling her nose.*

*“Fine,” Zuko repeats, firm. “What’s the point of bringing it up? It’s not going to* *last?” Why upend everything for just some—some fleeting thing that Zuko’s sure will* *fade back into the normal beats of their friendship. Any day now, it’ll fade, just like* *every crush that’s come before it. Any day.*

*“Sifu. It’s lasted until now,” Toph points out slowly, like Zuko needs help following* *along, and Zuko squeezes his eyes shut, pinching the bridge of his nose.*

*“I’ll get over it,” he says, tight. A statement of fact that will become true because Zuko* *needs it to be. A statement that’s always been true before.*

*“That’s real confident, when you dated Seung and what’s his name, Adam, and you’re* *still not over it.”*

*Zuko inhales deep and slow at the confirmation that Toph has known for…longer than* *Zuko would have hoped, feeling the air expanding his chest, visualizing it filling his* *stomach, sweeping down to his toes. She’s right. He knows she’s right.* She *knows* *she’s right. But did he really give either of them a fair shot? Or any of the people* *who’ve flirted with him or asked him out over the past three years? Shit, did he give* himself *one? A fair shot, a real attempt, at genuinely reaching for something he could* *actually have?*

*How can he say it didn’t work when he can admit, if only to himself and only when* *pressed, that he isn’t sure he actually ever* tried*?*

*“I think you’re making the wrong choice,” Toph says when it becomes clear he isn’t* *going to reply. “I think you should tell him.”*

*“Noted,” Zuko says, carefully setting the ball—soap, his hands smell floral, he thinks* *it's soap, back on the table.*

*“But you won’t, will you,” Toph says as she finally takes another bite of cake, not so* *much a question as a weary statement.*

*“I just…what we have now is so good, Toph,” he whispers, speaking more to his* *hands than to her. It’s so good and the idea of disrupting it, wrecking it with even the* *best of intentions, coming in with his messy heart on full display and forcing Sokka to* *apologize, to demure, to hold himself back, to restrain himself so Zuko doesn’t get* ideas—*no. Zuko can’t do that. He doesn’t know how he could handle boxing Sokka* *into some thin facsimile of what they have now, how he could force them into some* *pantomime of one of the few solid, consistent things Zuko’s ever felt sure enough to* *lean on.*

*Zuko might be able to imagine living without that, but he doesn’t* want *to. And if* *silence is the price to keep it, well, it’s a small enough one to pay. One Zuko’s paid* *plenty of times before.*

*“For how much longer, though,” Toph asks, a frown furrowing her face. “How much* *longer is that going to be enough?”*

*For as long as Zuko can ride it out. For as long as Zuko can hold on, his whole body* *feeling suddenly shaky to hear it dragged into the open, the possibility that he can’t* *deny—that one day, if he can’t get over it, his longing might mean even the best thing* *he’s ever had isn’t enough anymore.*

*“It’ll pass,” Zuko forces out, low, watching Sokka and Suki fall into each other,* *laughing as they mistime a spin. The words are as much a statement as a wish, a need,* *a pang of preemptive mourning for something he knows he can never have the same* *way he used to mourn the idea of a normal family and parents that loved him, up until* *he realized that he had found something better. That something better had found him* *back.*

Zuko shrugs, flicking the pink ball—the bath bomb, apparently—in Sokka’s hand but making no move to take it. “I don’t know, soap is soap. It goes in the cleaning section.”

“There’s a *clear* difference between bathtub soap and under-the-sink soap,” Sokka huffs, rolling his eyes, and Zuko shoves his hands under his legs against the urge to grab his phone and film that expression so he never forgets what it looks like.

“Bathrooms have sinks, too,” Zuko points out, aiming for pure pedantry and earning a glower that would be a lot more effective if he didn’t think it were so fucking cute.

“We can’t put any of this stuff in the kitchen boxes,” Sokka mutters, transferring his expression down to the pile around him, which Zuko really shouldn’t think is a shame, and Zuko tosses out an eye roll of his own even though he secretly—or maybe not—loves Sokka’s indignation over something as arbitrary as what you put beneath a kitchen sink. “Don’t give me that look, none of this is kitchen box stuff,” Sokka insists, absently backhanding Zuko in the chest.

“Uh,” Zuko laughs, incredulous and resting the urge to grab onto Sokka’s hand, to keep it pressed against him. “The sponges? The dish detergent?”

“That bottle is barely full!”

“There’s months’ worth of dishes left in that! If you do dishes like a *normal* person,” Zuko adds when Sokka scoffs, “Instead of using half the bottle in one squeeze like a *monster*.”

“So I have superior grip strength,” Sokka sniffs, putting his nose in the air. “Sue me.”

“Oh, grip strength, is that what—”

“And anyway,” Sokka interrupts, a smile in his voice through his scowl, “Are you really going to come at me for the way I clean up *your* pots and *your* pans?”

“That I use when I make *your* dinner,” Zuko says, the words sour but his chest warm from the easy bickering, from the proximity, from the barely-there bump of Sokka’s leg before he dives back under the sink. A strong, enduring flame that very much did not pass, did not burn out through sustained proximity, and Zuko wonders who exactly he thought he was kidding in ever thinking it would.

***One day and fifteen hours until the end***

Urgency is beginning to dig its claws around the base of Sokka’s spine as the sun starts to set, awareness building too much to ignore anymore that all their goofing off means that they’re behind schedule—again, again, what a joke, these schedules—when there really wasn’t wiggle room to work with in the first place. And along with it is the creeping, sharper realization that if they actually manage to stick to this schedule that they’ll be done in one more day. Which means that tomorrow is the last full day left, which means they’re into second-to-lasts now, and the next time around the clock it will be lasts—the last breakfast, the last ten in the morning with Zuko, the last noon. And tomorrow when Sokka wakes up it'll be less than twenty-four hours until the U-Haul will be here, and it feels like it’s all rushing down at him at once, sudden and hard and impossible to push down and crashing together in his chest, stealing the space from his lungs and making him just…

Making him want to move fast.

“You sure you don’t want those?” Zuko asks from where Sokka has him bagging the food to be donated by type so it can be dropped off nice and neat. “Aang and Katara aren’t on the total opposite side of the city.”

“They have their own takeout menus,” Sokka says as he sorts through their junk drawer, trying to figure out a way to start tackling it and unable to focus when all he can think of is all that still needs to be done and all the *time* it’s going to take when there’s so little time left.

“Yeah, their own vegan menus,” Zuko snorts, dry. “You think Katara doesn’t want to know the best hotpot around when she gets the chance to eat some meat?”

***Four months and eleven days until the end***

*“Okay, tell me all about it again,” Sokka says, aware that he’s nearly bouncing in his* *chair but unable to stop. He’s just so* happy, *so full of excitement and warmth and* *pride that he has to move, kicking Zuko lightly beneath the table as he puts the last few* *cuts of meat into the hotpot and grinning when Zuko gives him an exasperated,* *amused look.*

*“You heard about it like four times already,” Zuko shakes his head, adding some* *aromatics in after the meat, his fingers deft and sure with his chopsticks.*

*“So tell me again!” Sokka says. “It’s so exciting! It’s law school! It’s really* *happening!”*

*“It still doesn’t feel real,” Zuko admits, almost bashful.*

*“It is! Just wait, before you know it you’ll be Zuko Sozin, Esquire,” Sokka says,* *sweeping a hand through the air and imagining how shiny and bright it will look* *stamped on a metal placard or a business card or whatever lawyers use these days. “It* *has a nice ring to it. Very official.”*

*Zuko ducks his head, his cheek pink like he always gets when he’s overwhelmed by* *compliments, the sideways look he gives Sokka full of a fond kind of indulgence.* *“What do you want to hear about?”*

*“Uh, how about how awesome you are? And smart, and how you fucking nailed that* *interview and they loved you so much they gave you a full ride which of course they* *would, how could they not. You’re obviously going to blow everyone out of the water.”*

*“Okay, you can stop,” Zuko laughs, flushing again but his lips twitching into a* *telltale, pleased little smile.*

*“No!” Sokka says cheerfully, grinning at that smile. “I can’t, you’re amazing! How* *can I stop, I’m so impressed by you. You’re awesome! And like, fuck yeah, they saw* *how awesome you are, too!”*

*Zuko clears his throat. “Thank you,” he says, the words sticking a little still, but Sokka* *knows how far Zuko has come to be able to accept compliments at all.*

*“You are very welcome, my good hotman,” Sokka says, somewhere between teasing* *and totally serious as he sits back to let the waitress refill his water glass. He feels like* *he’s about to burst at the seams with pride, his chest full of a sparkling kind of warmth* *that’s spiraling through his whole body until he has to let it out.*

*“You two are so cute,” the waitress says, smiling as she tops off Zuko’s water. “And* *no need to rush, we’re* not *trying to hurry you out. But we noticed you and your* *husband were celebrating.” She gives them a half-sheepish look, pulling a little plate* *from behind her back, “And we always like to throw in something a little special.”*

*“I didn’t know you guys did desserts,” Sokka says, feeling a fresh wave of excitement* *as he straightens to admire the cupcake and bright little star cookie on top. This day is* *just getting better and better!*

*“We don’t,” she admits. “But there’s a bakery right down the block, and you two* *seemed like you wouldn’t mind sharing,” she adds with a cheeky grin.*

*“Aw,” Sokka says, touched by her thoughtfulness and unable to resist the chance to* *tease, slinging an arm around Zuko’s shoulders and smiling at the side of his face,* *feeling the soft fabric of his sweater beneath his hand—hm,* very *soft, Sokka is going* *to have to see if this one is slouchy enough for him to steal—and the press of Zuko’s* *body against his own as he leans in. “We do know how to share, don’t we?”*

*Zuko huffs a laugh and bats him away, shoving Sokka back down the booth with an* *exasperated look. “You didn’t have to do that,” he demurs to the waitress.*

*“Oh, it’s no bother at all,” she assures him, “We love when we can be part of people’s* *celebrations!”*

*“This genius here just got a full ride to his top choice law school,” Sokka can’t resist* *bragging, nudging Zuko beneath the table. It’s just like when Katara graduated and* *Suki got her first big—for the Kyoshi Warriors—venue booked and he didn’t know* *how else to express the feeling filling his entire body except to run around telling* *everyone all about it.*

*“Congratulations! That’s so exciting for you both!”*

*“It is,” Zuko says, dry, the tone he always gets when they’re mistaken for a couple.*

*“This guy’s a real special one,” Sokka says, smiling into Zuko’s eyes. “I’m lucky to* *get to be along for the ride,” he adds, partly playing it up for the waitress like they* *always do but meaning it with all the sincerity in his heart.*

*Zuko clears his throat, head ducking and cheek going pink again, and Sokka busies* *himself cutting the cupcake neatly in two to give Zuko some space as the waitress hops* *back to her shift. Zuko might be better at accepting compliments these days, but Sokka* *knows that was still a lot of close-range sentiment for the guy.*

*“Yeah,” Zuko finally rasps, foot briefly pressing against Sokka’s beneath the table.* *“Me, too.”*

*“I’m serious,” Sokka says, trying to figure out how to split the cookie. Maybe they can* *just take bites and share directly... “I’m lucky to have someone like you in my life.* *You’re awesome.”*

*“…Yeah,” Zuko says after a pause. “I—thanks. You—you too.”*

*“Any time, hot stuff,” Sokka says, shooting him a grin. “Any time.”*

“Good point,” Sokka says as he stares down at the menu of their favorite hot pot place, suddenly feeling shakier than he wants to be. The effect of being so aware of the rushing time, he supposes. And maybe of eating definitely expired gummy hamburger for lunch. “Let me just—I guess I’ll save a few.”

“A few,” Zuko snorts. “You’re saving all of them. You have reviews of all the dishes you’ve tried in there, you rated their delivery times, you wrote commentary on how suitable things are for transportation and what needs to be eaten in the restaurant. You aren’t saving just a *few*.” The words are fond, though, and his face is…gentle, maybe, rather than teasing when Sokka glances over.

“Hey, I never heard you complaining,” Sokka says, feeling weirdly frozen under that look, strangely helpless as he stares back and looks at Zuko looking at him like that and realizes that this is yet another almost-last, one that he didn’t even know to look for and treasure.

“I wouldn’t dream of it,” Zuko says, lips quirked and maybe a little *sad* and god, Sokka feels like a hole is opening in his chest with each passing second.

“You’re going to need to find the best hot pot spot in Boston,” he blurts, suddenly desperate to see that warmth again, to make the sadness disappear, to bring back Zuko’s smile. “And ramen, and an Indian place that delivers.” And Thai, and Mediterranean, Italian worth going out for, fuck, the idea of starting fresh all over again…

“I’m sure there’s lots of options,” Zuko says after a beat. “I haven’t really looked at the maps yet.”

“It could be fun to explore,” Sokka says, folding and unfolding the menu in his hands and not entirely sure he believes it but fuck, suddenly needing it to be true. Needing it to be true for Zuko no matter how much he hates the idea of it, and maybe it was the right thing to say because Zuko is giving him a suddenly opaque look that…well, it isn’t quite warm but it isn’t actively sad anymore, either, so Sokka will take it. Fuck, he’ll take absolutely anything Zuko will give him

“Yeah,” Zuko finally says. “It could be. I’ll have to keep you informed.”

“Yeah, I’d—yeah,” Sokka says as the idea of being *informed* makes the hole in his chest yawn a little wider. “That—keep me in the loop.”

&#45;-

***One day and eleven hours until the end***

“You realize we could just—”

“Nope,” Sokka cuts in, the word cheerful, “We should have started cooking an hour ago and we didn’t, so now we have to get moving.”

Zuko raises his eyebrow. “By making it harder.”

Sokka raises his eyebrows back as he lays out the two pots and single tiny skillet that are still unpacked. “Did we cook anything when it wasn’t a challenge?” he asks as he goes to start adding ingredients to the table.

Zuko purses his lips, because, “…No.”

“And are you capable of ignoring direct or implied challenges?”

…Sokka knows him too fucking well sometimes. “No.”

“And do you trust me with *this* set of ingredients?” They spill onto the table, two carrots and a butternut squash, a container of slivered almonds and miscellaneous spices and the odds and ends of half-used vegetables and…a bag of frozen blueberries? “To make something edible on my own?”

“…Definitely not.”

“Exactly,” Sokka says, triumphant about his own inability to cook without a recipe. “So—your challenge, Sifu Hotman, should you choose to accept it, is to figure out what you can make with all of this.”

“Butternut squash and carrot soup,” Zuko says immediately, smirking when Sokka blinks. “Cook it down with some butter, some onions if we have it, simmer it in some chicken stock, add in that apple juice, puree it—” the immersion blender might be packed, but Sokka might not know that. “—top it with almonds.” They can just eat some frozen blueberries or something. Put them in water instead of ice cubes. Be fancy.

Sokka stares a moment, then goes back to the food pile and adds eight almost-empty bags of different kinds of rice to the table.

Zuko purses his lips. “I mean, stir fry.”

“Too easy,” Sokka immediately pronounces. “Next.”

“Fine,” Zuko huffs, rolling his eyes but not trying to hide his smile because he can feel his energy sparking again, his mind finding its focus now that it has a challenge to wrap around. “Some pressed rice cakes with crispy bottoms, and the vegetables like a ratatouille over top.” Though it would be a weird one without any tomatoes to work with…

“Okay,” Sokka says, breaking into a grin, clearly delighted, “What about—” he darts to fridge, adding ingredients to the table one by one, and Zuko can’t deny that it’s fun to try to come up with something to make of them. He can feel himself getting more and more into the competition of it— holding back his good all-purpose recipes for when he gets stumped, trying to anticipate what Sokka will pick next since they mutually and wordlessly decided no repeats, feeding off Sokka’s energy.

Sokka seems to be having just as much fun trying to stump him, tongue caught between his teeth in focus and gratifyingly impressed every time Zuko comes up with something, slowly reaching into packed but not sealed boxes and the donate pile for increasingly outlandish things, and it's going far past the point of a reasonable meal for two, well into multicourse dishes and elaborate sides, but Sokka wasn’t wrong—it *is* a challenge now, and Zuko is hungry to win.

“Alright.” Sokka is hiding his latest addition behind his back. “How are you going to make *this* work,” he says, smacking a familiar bottle full of thick amber liquid on the table and grinning like he’s won as Zuko suddenly feels his stomach lurch.

***One year, four months, and eleven days until the end***

*“You sure you know about this?” Zuko asks, popping the top of the garum bottle and* *making a face at the unexpectedly pungent aroma. “Why not just use fish sauce?”*

*“I’m a chef, Zuko,” Mai says, her movements brisk as she sharpens her knives, barely* *even looking at what she’s doing as she hits him with a flat look. “Live a little.”*

*“I live plenty,” Zuko grumbles, dabbing a bit of the amber liquid onto a spoon and* *pointedly giving it a try. He blinks. The taste is…unexpectedly subtle compared to the* *smell.*

*“Do you now, Mr. Compliance Man.” Mai gives him a dry once-over, her lips tilted in* *the corner with amusement at his apparent daring, and there’s no reason for Zuko to* *feel the need to push back, to prove something, and he doesn’t know that he even* *wanted to say something tonight anyway, and maybe he shouldn’t, he isn’t even* sure *after all, he’s just—*

*“I’m thinking of applying to law school,” he blurts, belatedly grabbing up a knife and* *a cutting board, a jittery kind of anxiety suddenly jangling through him in the wake of* *the words when Mai just pauses before carefully aside her honing rod. “Finally, I* *guess,” he adds, wincing because he knows he’s just awkwardly fumbling into her* *silence now and unable to stop himself. “I know I haven’t talked about it in a while,* *but—I’ve been thinking about it. Still. Or again, or—yeah.”*

*If Zuko didn’t know her so well, he would think Mai was just choosing not to reply as* *she grabs an onion and efficiently peels it in a few quick motions. But does know her* *so he waits, trying not to be visibly anxious for her reaction even if they both know he* *is.*

*“So,” she eventually says, knife nearly a blur as she starts dicing, “You’re finally* *deciding to end it, huh.”*

*“…If I don’t do it now, I’m not sure I ever will,” he says, low, the admission painful* *and trying to stick in his throat.*

*Mai exhales long and slow, pursing her lips, her expression unreadable. But Zuko can* *see the signs that she’s thinking, can read into the fact that she’s not protesting, not* *arguing, not telling him he’s dumb and imagining things and needs to get over himself.* *“You’re just thinking about it,” she eventually says, slashing him a look. “Not* *decided?”*

*Zuko doesn’t know how to articulate the tangled ball that always rises in his chest at* *the thought of something as final as a decision, a writhing knot of upset and fear and* *denial and anger and too many things to name.*

*“I don’t know if I know how to be without him,” he makes himself say, wincing at how* *it sounds out loud. “Not as a crush, but just…” As a friend, a confidante. As someone* *Zuko has built his life around without ever meaning to, a cornerstone of the foundation* *of who he is today and the house can stand without it but fuck, it won’t be the same.* *“It’s going to be different,” Mai says, concise as always.*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says, setting his knife down because he feels like he might be on the* *verge of choking over the fact that she’s speaking of it as a ‘will,’ as an ‘is.’ As a thing* *that’s going to happen and not a thing that might.*

*“Not everything, though,” she adds with a sideways look before passing him cloves of* *garlic to dehusk. A knife-free task. “The rest of us will still be here.”*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says after a pause, shaky, his eyes stinging for a different reason, now.* *That’s—that’s true, it is, and it’s something to hold onto and ground himself against. It* *is. It’s just…*

*“It’s okay if you’re scared,” she says, almost diffident, not looking at him because she* *knows him, too.*

*“I don’t...I don’t know how I’m going to do it,” Zuko whispers, feeling like saying it* *out loud, like hearing those words—*I’m going to do it*—in his own voice is striking* *some indelible bell that can never be unrung.*

*“It’s going to hurt,” Mai says and Zuko can’t hold back a flinch. “You can’t kid* *yourself that it won’t. You two have woven together too much for it to be easy to* *untangle. But you’re going to need to. Pry yourself out of all the bits of his and him out* *of all the bits of yours.”*

*“But—that—we’ll still be friends—”*

*“Zuko, you’re* friends *now,” Mai cuts in, sharp. “Isn’t that what you need to change?* *What’s the point if it just stays the same?” And all Zuko can do is slowly close his* *mouth, throat dry, because how could he possibly try to deny it? “It’s going to hurt,”* *she repeats after a pause, her voice gentling. “But not forever. Nothing hurts forever.* *You’ll think about it, a bit less each day until some days you don’t think about it at all.* *You’ll find other things to do. Other friends. School. Hobbies if you even have the time* *with classes.”*

*Zuko makes a low sound, involuntary, something in him wrenching at the idea of new* *friends, of new hobbies. Of a new school and new classes and new exams and new* *homework, of new places to study and new nights spent staying up too late. Of doing it* *all again and Sokka nowhere to be found in any of it.*

*“And you give yourself space,” Mai continues, firm, like she knows exactly how he’s* *thinking. And after Azula, maybe she does. “You don’t have to cut him out. But you* *don’t give him open access to every part of you anymore.”*

*“I don’t—I don’t want to lose him as a friend, though,” Zuko protests, pressing his* *hands flat against the counter. The idea of that—it feels worse than any kind of* *painful, useless longing could ever be.*

*“You don’t have to. But Zuko—you can’t just keep pining after him forever.” She gives* *him a long, level look, then taps the garlic cloves sitting untouched on the cutting* *board in front of him. “So what are your options?”*

*“…You make it sound like a breakup,” he finally says, low, picking up a clove. Space* *and distance and new habits and new friends, all the things Zuko remembers needing* *after her.*

*“You think it won’t be?”*

*He supposes that’s true enough, but fuck, “I can’t believe we’re breaking up without* *even dating first,” he mutters, trying to find some kind of stab of bitter humor and* *falling far short, but Mai snorts, smacking her knife down as she reaches for the oil* *and the skillet.*

*“I’ll say it again,” she says briskly, eyeballing the amount of oil she’s pouring into the* *pan. “What are your options, Zuko?”*

Zuko stares a moment longer at that bottle, remembering the taste of that meal on his tongue, delicious and unique and somehow impossible to get through, then says, firm, “I can make it happen.” Except *can* and *should* aren’t exactly the same thing and he needs to stop right now because that kind of wondering is useless. There’s no going back, and he knows—he does, he really does—that what he did was right for all that it hurts more than he ever imagined it would. What he and Sokka had was wonderful and amazing and after ten years, Zuko didn’t know how to keep doing it anymore. He didn’t know how to keep acting like nothing has changed when so much —everything, it feels like, some days—has.

“Just saying that isn’t a recipe,” Sokka huffs, still playing the game and missing all that’s going on in Zuko’s head when normally he sees it so clearly. But then, he’s never seen this, has he? And Zuko’s always been happy for it.

“I guess you’ll just have to taste it and see,” Zuko says, shoving up his sleeves and slapping their lone cutting board and remaining knife onto the counter, his playfulness suddenly feeling hollow and empty and isn’t that just the rub? That everything can be good and wonderful and some part of Zuko can’t stop grieving that he can’t be happy with it, that it isn’t enough. How greedy, to want more. To blow up Sokka’s life along with his own and he so didn’t want to ruin this amazing, wonderful thing and that’s exactly what he ended up doing anyway but fuck, he couldn’t just pretend to be unchanging forever. He couldn’t just be *pining*.

But just like Mai said to him then—it will hurt, but a little bit less until eventually, it will be something he doesn’t think about every hour, every day, every week. Until he’s so busy with law school and a new city and new friends that he won’t have the headspace for it even if he wants to give it his attention. And it might be awful now, a breakup in slow motion, a breakup like a car crash he can see coming from a mile away but he’s on black ice and he already knows he can’t stop it, he got in this path too long ago to be able to just get off now without any wreckage. A breakup worse than Mai and Seung and Adam and Jin**.**

But isn’t that the evidence all on its own for why it needs to happen?

&#45;-

***One day and ten hours until the end***

“I can’t believe you pulled this off,” Sokka says, taking another bite and making one of his happy little food noises that are so familiar.

“Told you,” Zuko says, leaning against the counter on the other side of the stovetop, something about the boxes and piles and the looming pressure of all that’s left to be done making sitting at the table to eat feel wholly inappropriate.

“I shouldn’t have doubted you,” Sokka says around a mouthful, circling the table, seemingly equally unable to settle.

“That’s right, you shouldn’t have.”

“I suppose you couldn’t have used all of this for just us,” Sokka says, shifting his bowl to one hand as he pokes through the ingredients left on the table.

“*That* wasn’t the challenge,” Zuko says, giving Sokka an arch look.

“My mistake,” Sokka murmurs, grinning, and Zuko scrapes him with a look Azula would be proud of.

“You’re right, it was,” Zuko sniffs, finally breaking into a smile as Sokka laughs.

“I can’t believe we got so much of this all packed today,” Sokka says, resuming his wandering, opening and closing cabinets and Zuko can practically see him mentally checking things off his carefully-printed-in-legible-handwriting to-do list.

“There’s still a lot left, though,” Zuko sighs, his lean against the counter turning into more of a slump. More than Zuko wants to think about, but that’s a problem for—

“Tomorrow, though?” Sokka suggests, voice hopeful, and Zuko knows they should power through, shouldn’t risk leaving so much for the very end, but he’s suddenly feeling nothing but relief at the idea of being able to stop. It's hours before when Zuko normally tries to go to bed and he has a feeling that this is going to be another sleepless, restless night usually best spent on some mindless tasks, but he doesn’t know how much more of this he can take tonight.

“Works for me,” he says, and the way Sokka’s shoulders visibly relax lets Zuko know that regardless of the schedule, regardless of how much tomorrow is going to suck for more reasons than Zuko wants to think about, it’s the right decision.

“Awesome,” Sokka says. “And that gives us some time to figure out what to do with stuff like this,” he adds, going up on his toes to pluck an unfortunately familiar pan off the top of the fridge. “What do you think?” he asks, flashing the burned, scratched bottom. “Save it? Toss it?”

***Four months and seven days until the end***

*Zuko jolts upright as he hears the jangle of keys on the other side of the door, twisting* *hopefully around and then realizing what he looks like and throwing himself front* *again, trying frantically to figure out something more casual and less I’ve-been-sitting-here-waiting-for-you-all-evening in the bare seconds before Sokka comes in.*

*“Hey, man,” Zuko says, hitting something relaxed but inviting, silently congratulating* *himself for managing to not let the anxious twisting in his gut seep into his voice.*

*“Hey,” Sokka says, words heavy and tired as he drops his keys in the bowl and sets* *down his satchel. “What’s up?”*

*“Not much,” Zuko says, tracking Sokka’s movements and the tension on his face and* *the set of his shoulders and trying not to openly worry over the lack of Sokka’s usual* *energy. “How was your day?”*

*“Oh, you know, the usual,” he says, and Zuko wonders if it’s just him who feels like* *the silence after those words is deafening in the absence of Sokka’s usually-immediate* *stories about projects and coworkers and what he ate for lunch and things he saw on* *his commute and thoughts he had about the evening.*

*“Yeah?” Zuko finally prompts, making his face attentive and interested and hoping to* *draw out a story. “Alex being Alex?”*

*“Yeah,” Sokka shrugs. “Work is work, you know? Bosses are bosses,” he offers with* *a wry smile that Zuko’s been able to see through for years. Not that Zuko’s going to* *press just yet for what Sokka is pushing down, not when Zuko is so full himself with* *things he couldn’t bear for Sokka to see and flinch away from.*

*Zuko chews his lip, trying to think of something that might cheer Sokka up. “I got the* *official acceptance letter in the mail today. Physical paper, not just email. A fancy seal* *and everything.” Which Zuko thinks is a waste, but Sokka has kept every wedding* *invitation and save the date and holiday card he’s ever received in his life, including* *the envelopes. He loves that kind of thing.*

*Except Sokka’s “Oh, wow. That’s awesome,” feels lackluster, his lack of wanting to* *see it loud in its absence, and Sokka failing to light up with happiness at any mention* *of the news like he was a week ago, and Zuko would think the sudden moodiness* *meant something the way he thought Sokka’s enthusiasm for Zuko to apply in the first* *place was its own sign, its own confirmation, if Sokka wasn’t down about just about* *everything this past week.*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says inanely, painfully aware that he’s been silent too long, feeling the* *stumbling hitches in the conversation like he rarely does and searching for something* *else to catch Sokka’s interest. “I was thinking about kebabs for dinner?” Meat on a* *stick with sauce, Sokka’s favorite genre of food.*

*“Sounds great, dude.” Dude. Not hotman, not hot stuff, not hot thing and fuck, when* *did Zuko start* wanting *to be called hot cakes again? “I’m going to change, alright?* *Maybe shower, watch a show or two.”*

*“Okay, well do you—”*

*“See you in a bit,” Sokka says, already disappearing down the hallways and leaving* *Zuko frowning after him. No work stories, no interest in dinner, wanting to watch a* *show* alone*…*

*Zuko doesn’t think suggesting a game or a movie is going to land right now, not when* *it feels like Sokka just wants to not have to think. Which Zuko* *can understand, and* *Sokka so very rarely wants space and to be alone but when Zuko does, he always* *appreciates quiet gestures, things he doesn’t have to respond to but that still show* *support. Things like his attempt to make brownies a few days ago, not that he can do* *that again considering the burned baking tray he rather thoroughly destroyed and hid* *behind the cereal boxes in a frustrated, annoyed, embarrassed fit over the fact that* *most days he can cook anything with any kind of heat source, but something about the* *finicky rigidness of baking never works for him. But Sokka has been weird for a week* *now, distant and getting lost in thought, frowning into space and falling too quickly* *out of jokes, and Zuko might not understand what’s going on but he can at the very* *least bite the bullet of his embarrassment if it means surprising Sokka with something* *that will put a smile on his face.*

*Decided, Zuko pulls out his phone, dialing as he pops in an earbud and scrolls for a* *good playlist—not a podcast, he’s going to need to concentrate for this.*

*“Sifu!” Aang exclaims as he picks up the phone. “Great to hear from you!”*

*Zuko smiles at Aang’s audible happiness as if they didn’t talk just this morning while* *Zuko was taking the bus to work. “Hey, Aang,” he says, walking to the kitchen and* *feeling something determined and hopeful settling over the anxious churn of his gut at* *the sound of Aang’s voice. “You might be the Sifu today, actually.”*

*“Oh?” A rustle down the line and Zuko can picture Aang unfolding himself from some* *yoga pose. “What’s up?”*

*“That blondie layer thing. With the panna cotta and the jam.”*

*All the rustling stops.*

*Zuko takes a deep breath. “I want to make it.”*

*“Zuko,” Aang says slowly, “You almost lit your kitchen on fire making a box cake.”*

*He is well aware. “That’s why I need you to walk me through it.”*

*“Okay, well I can come over this weekend, and we can—”*

*“No,” Zuko says, too fast, too sharp, too* upset, *but it's already out there now, tone and* *all. “Tonight? Can it be tonight?”*

*“…Everything okay?”*

*“Yeah,” Zuko says, closing his eyes and feeling a bit desperate at the idea of not* *being able to do anything for Sokka, of being out of things to try. “Just—please?”*

*“…Do you want the normal recipe or the way Sokka likes it?”*

*“…Sokka’s.”*

*“It takes like three hours. For me.”*

*“I have time,” Zuko says, feeling his jaw set with stubbornness*

*“You have work tomorrow,” Aang points out, which…says something about how long* *he thinks this three-hour recipe is going to take Zuko to pull off. But it’s not like he’s* *going to sleep anyway, worrying like this.*

*“I need to thank Sokka for helping me with the LSATs,” Zuko says, firm enough to* *shut down any questions about what other reasons there might be. Hopefully.*

*“Alright,” Aang says after a moment, his voice going muffled as he says something to* *Katara before popping back. “Okay, let’s start with laying out all your ingredients. Is* *your butter cold or on the counter? And do you have gelatin?”*

*“I think Sokka bought Jello cups at the store last week, there might be some left,” Zuko* *frowns, heading for the fridge.*

*“…Okay,” Aang says, voice going bright and determined. “First up—let's learn what* *all the ingredients are.”*

“It seems like it might be a pain to get clean,” Sokka says dubiously, poking at the bottom of what was once a brownie pan. “The nonstick is never going to survive getting the burned bits off.”

“Yeah,” Zuko says. He doesn’t need to get close to confirm. He remembers what it looks like. And he knows some things can’t go back to the way they were before, no matter how much you might try your best to make it happen. “Toss it.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Every single person around them thinks they're complete idiots
