## Chapter 4: The End

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

An early update so Jo can read this on her flight!

Warnings in this one for grief over the death of a loved one (in a flashback), an onscreen panic attack, and explicit sexual content. Mind the rating bump up, and let me know if you want to skip it and I can give you the line(s) to jump to.

Are we ready for the end?

### Text

***Twenty-three hours until the end***

Sokka stumbles out of his room at an ungodly early hour feeling hungover, achy, and awful. This is the third night in a row he hasn’t slept well, if at all, and he can’t even fucking blame it on his mattress being on the floor because his mattress is perfectly comfortable—he’d know, he’s been lying awake in it enough to write a fucking review. Besides, he hasn’t slept well in what feels like months, way before he turned his bedframe to preemptive planks of wood, and god, just the thought makes his whole body feel heavy with exhaustion.

He's making a beeline for the kitchen and the nearest caffeine source he can find when he pulls up short so fast he nearly skids out in his socked feet, slapping a hand against the wall to regain his balance as he stares into the living room, mouth agape as he takes in every suddenly empty, packed-away inch of it.

Every last game has been packed away, all the surfaces cleared of papers and knickknacks and odds and ends, every plant packed for transport, each box neatly stacked and sorted, labeled in Sokka’s own messy scrawl and then again in Zuko’s precise, neat hand. The rug is rolled, the one crooked blind straightened, and Sokka bets that if he checked he’d find that even the ceiling fan has been dusted. Sokka might not have seen one in a while but he remembers college, remembers the months around Ozai’s trial. He hasn’t forgotten the signs of one of Zuko’s sleepless, restless nights, because Zuko there’s no other way Zuko could have done all this except to work until he crashed only a few hours ago.

Sokka stares, swallowing hard as he takes in the echoing emptiness of the room, leaning into the wall more than he cares to admit as he tries to make sense of how clean it feels, how *sterile*. As he tries to find a single memory left in this space of laughter or streaming marathons or game nights or spilled popcorn, feeling more and more desperate as he scans for anything at all to suggest that he and Zuko lived here until his eyes finally catch on a neat row of plush stuffed animals leaning against the wall so close to his feet that he isn’t quite sure how he missed them.

***Nine years, one month, and twelve days until the end***

*Sokka bumps and jostles his way down the sidewalk, letting himself be buffeted by the* *crowd instead of his usual bobbing and weaving because right now it feels like it’s all* *he can do to keep his eyes on Zuko’s back and follow along from bus to subway to bus* *again to wherever it is they’re going.*

*Sokka would normally be more involved, especially with the serious, focused way* *Zuko keeps checking the map on his phone. But Zuko had firmly and gently refused* *any help and today…today Sokka doesn’t have the will to push the issue, or the energy* *to think about engaging his brain and problem solving and pretending everything is* *fine one second earlier than he has to. Getting through a morning of Katara’s* *concerned ‘just dropping by’ and Aang’s careful texts was more than enough, and* *with wherever Zuko is taking him on the horizon…*

*The only small favor of getting hauled along for this little adventure when all he wants* *is to hide in his room is that it’s going to be just Zuko he has to manage for and not a* *whole mess of people constantly looking at him, all upset and worried and wanting* *him to be okay. As if that’s even an okay thing to want in the first place, for him to just* *not be* bothered*, to* get over *it, to—*

*“Hey,” Zuko says, grabbing Sokka’s arm to keep him from walking right by him.* *“We’re here.”*

*Sokka stares for a blank moment before dragging his gaze up and past Zuko’s* *shoulder to see… “What?”*

*Zuko shifts his weight, eyes anxious on Sokka’s face. “You, um. You said you and Yue* *used to spend a lot of time at the planetarium.”*

*“She worked there,” Sokka says, hearing the words more than feeling himself say* *them, his body feeling numb as he stares up at the sweeping façade and bright* *banners framing the entrance. “At the ticket booth.”*

*“And so I thought—” Zuko rubs the back of his neck. “I know it’s not a* happy *occasion, but I thought, that maybe…it would help? Being somewhere that reminded* *you of her?”*

*Sokka…doesn’t know what to say, his throat suddenly choked to the point that he* *doesn’t trust what would come out of it. Katara is always so concerned and wants to* talk*, his dad gruff and stilted, and Sokka never knows what to say or how to act, how* *to reassure them because he hates being a fucking* problem*. But he hates acting like he* *isn’t bothered even more, hates having to do the calculus on what’s the correct level of* *bothered to be by the anniversary of his high school girlfriend’s death—words Sokka* *barely even knows how to say now, college making them feel so small, so insignificant* *some days. Yue was so much more than that, than just one of the high school* *girlfriends everyone acts like it’s their job to leave behind and move beyond and* *replace with the college variety, and it’s not like Sokka doesn’t date and hook up but* *he can’t—he—*

*Zuko’s voice is low and raspy as it breaks through his tumbling thoughts. “We don’t* *have to if you don’t want to,” he says, fiddling with the zipper of his jacket. “We can* *just go back or—or get ice cream, or find a coffee shop, or…yeah. Whatever.”*

*“No,” Sokka manages, “No I—I want to. I do.”*

*“Okay,” Zuko says, expression easing in relief even as he still searches Sokka’s face.* *“I…I have a program for the day. Some ideas, but if you—”*

*“No, that…Whatever you planned. That sounds good,” Sokka says, feeling his breath* *going unsteady and his eyes going hot because it’s just…it’s all hitting him all at once* *and it’s not just good, it's* perfect*. A day that’s all about Yue without being all about* *her absence. A day of remembering her without making her feel like a ghost, a specter,* *the elephant in the room that everyone is staring at but no one knows how to talk* *about. A day that makes her feel like…just part of the fun. The way it always was with* *her. “That sounds great.”*

*“Okay, well—Let me know if you want to do something else,” Zuko says, shoulders* *straightening a bit with something that looks like determination. “I also, um—I also* *brought these,” he adds, looking suddenly uncertain again as he reaches into his* *jacket pocket to pull out…a pack of tissues. “I, um. I have a lot.”*

*“I—thank you,” Sokka says, making a noise he’ll choose to think of as a laugh and* *grabbing a tissue to blow his nose as his eyes sting fresh all over again. “Thank you.* *You didn’t have to do this.”*

*“You’d do the same for me,” Zuko shrugs, like it’s that simple, that easy, before* *clearing his throat. “Want to get started?”*

*“Yeah, I—yeah.” Sokka takes a deep breath, feeling steadier than he has since he* *woke up. “Let’s get started.”*

*“Okay,” Zuko says, pulling out a* schedule*, and Sokka has never seen Zuko use one in* *his life, not even to figure out his classes, and the fact that he made one just for this* *just for Sokka—no, just for* Yue*—*

*Zuko takes them through ticketing, refusing to let Sokka pay with more of that quiet,* *unmovable stubbornness of his, and then they’re stepping into the arching atrium, the* *solar system gently rotating above them and Sokka takes a moment to look up at it, to* *take in this dance that Yue loved so much. He blinks hard and sucks in a quick,* *gasping breath, for once uncaring about the people around him as he blindly reaches* *out and fumbles for Zuko’s hand, squeezing probably harder than he should because* *he just—he needs to not be solely alone in this right now.*

*And he isn’t, because Zuko squeezes back, shifting closer after a moment to fit their* *hands together more comfortably and waiting patiently until Sokka can finally breathe* *again.*

*“Okay,” Sokka finally says, still staring up at Neptune’s streaks of blue and white.* *“Okay.”*

*He registers Zuko’s wordless nod, and then there’s a small tug on his hand, Zuko* *gently guiding him through the light crowd to the—*

*“The gift store?” Sokka asks, surprise momentarily cutting through the painfully,* *sweetly close memories.*

*“Mhm.”*

*“Usually that’s for last.”*

*“We can do that too,” Zuko agrees, glancing around as they step through the glass* *doors into the bright chaos of posters and games and books and t-shirts and mugs.* *“But don’t you want a fuzzy moon to walk around with?” he asks, grabbing one off the* *shelf and giving it an inviting shimmy, his lips curled into a tentative smile.*

*And because he’s teasing, because he’s not trying to make it go away, because he’s* *just trying to be there with Sokka through it, Sokka manages to muster a sniff and an* *arch, “Maybe I do,” in return, plucking the plush toy out of Zuko’s hands and* *snugging it against his chest as primly and prissily as he’s able to. Its cheerful smiling* *face looks up at him, spindly arms and legs flopping as he leans to grab a stuffed* *comet. “And maybe you need one too, then.”*

*“Right, of course, my mistake,” Zuko says, solemnly tucking the comet under his arm* *so its grinning face is right under his own and it looks so ridiculous that Sokka can’t* *help but crack a smile, what feels like his first all day.*

*“Get ready, guys,” Sokka says to the stuffed animals, smiling a little as Zuko rolls his* *eyes like it’s just any other day, any other joke. “I’m about to blow your minds. You’re* *going to learn all about where you guys came from.”*

*“China?” Zuko says under his breath, checking the tag.*

*“Okay, Zuko can handle that part. But get ready, Papa Sokka is going to tell you* *about* space.*” The same way Yue told him.*

*"Ugh,” Zuko mutters, pulling a face. “Never refer to yourself that way again.”*

*“For you, hot stuff? Anything.” And as they walk into the first exhibit, plushies under* *arm and Zuko’s hand just close enough to be casually at the ready but not pushy,* *Sokka feels like he’s never meant it more.*

They made it a tradition after that. Or Zuko did, rather. Finding a planetarium, a natural history museum, an aquarium, a science center—something to keep Sokka busy on the anniversary of Yue’s death, yes, but something that always made pains to remind him of her, too. To make her memory feel vivid and close in a way that felt gentle and omnipresent rather than sharp and painful. To make an entire day into an homage to a girl who dreamed of being an astronaut one day and exploring the ocean’s floor the next, even once the ache of it faded to something manageable, something Sokka’s known longer than he ever got to know her.

And always, always, starting with the gift shop to pick up a friend for the experience. And always a pair, one for each of them.

Sokka presses a fist to his mouth, breathing out hard against it before slowly approaching all those grinning faces and bright plastic eyes, finally seeing as he steps into the room the note slipped into the hands of the turtle in the front.

*'I couldn’t remember whose was whose. But I figured you would know if you want to take a stab at* *separating them. –Z'*

Sokka sucks in another shaking breath, touching that Z—as if anyone else would be writing him a note during the night—before looking at all these pairs that have always lived together, that have never spent even a day apart. The idea of separating them—of the shark being without the stingray, the stuffed avocado without the smiling bowl of guac…Sokka knows exactly where each one came from, can remember exactly how they picked them out and who went to the register holding each even if Zuko always insisted on paying. He knows all their names, the ones they came with and the ones they were given. He *could* separate them, he knows *how*. But oh, god, he can’t, he can’t, he *can’t.*

Sokka squeezes his eyes shut, sucking in air until he’s almost dizzy with it, making himself breathe until he finally feels like he can open them again without crumpling over. Then he carefully tucks the note back into Shelldon’s little hands like he never saw it at all and turns around to go back to his mattress on the floor and press his face into the pillow until his eyes stop stinging, until he stops feeling like he’s on the verge of a scream, on the verge of letting loose some raw, terrible thing that was never meant to see the light of day.

And maybe when Sokka comes out the other side and tries to roll out of bed again it will be better, somehow.

Maybe it will all be a dream.

&#45;-

***Twenty-one hours until the end***

Eventually, Sokka hears Zuko getting up and beginning to putter around. The faint sounds of the bathroom, of the kitchen, and then the unmistakable awful noise of somehow more cardboard boxes and packing paper and bubble wrap and Sokka could get up, he should, he needs to. He’s aware that the schedule started an hour ago and Zuko is already working and there’s still so much to do and less than a day left to do it, but it’s like those stuffed animals all over again. Sokka knows that he *could* do it, that he needs to do it, but he *can’t*.

It's like his entire body is existing outside of his control, like part of his mind is saying *get up* and *go* and *just do it* but the rest of him can only curl tighter and tighter in response. Like maybe if he stays huddled under his blankets with his eyes pressed closed and his hands over his ears he won’t have to admit that this is the last time he’ll ever wake to the sound of Zuko quietly moving around the apartment, the last time he’ll walk into the kitchen and get a quick smile and a quiet hey and a cup of something warm waiting for him, the last time he’ll go to brush his teeth and find his toothpaste neatly lined up against the edge of the sink like Zuko doesn’t even seem to realize he does every morning when he brushes his teeth, organizing and straightening and aligning things around the vanity in idle, absent motions that always make Sokka smile and fight the urge to poke something just out of center to see if Zuko will notice.

He needs to get up, he needs to get *up*, to *help*. But his body feels far away, his limbs tingling and numb and god, he doesn’t know how to handle the fact that it’s the last time he’s going to do that, too, the last time he’ll pitch in at Zuko’s side and get something done together. The last time for that just like everything else this whole time has been a last and Sokka hasn’t known how to handle any of that, either. Except pretend it's fine like if he does it well enough, that'll make it true. Except to push through until suddenly he can’t anymore, until suddenly all he has left is pulling himself into a tighter and tighter ball like maybe that will stop time from catching up to him.

For months it’s felt this way. Four months and thirteen days, to be exact. Four months and thirteen days of feeling like time is running out, like every minute slips faster through his fingers the harder he tries to hold on. Like every moment is a potential last, an end coming over and over and each as painful as the first. Like suddenly he’s crying over video games and upset over Zuko’s work schedule and apathetic about his own job, his emotions all over the place, unsettled and out of sorts and swinging from happiness to grief and back again at the tilt of Zuko’s lips and god, he just wants it to *stop*. But at the same time, he knows what stopping would mean and he can’t, he can’t, he *can’t.*

Sokka gasps, air clammy and dragging and hot, sticking in his throat. He doesn’t know how to go out there and face the fact that any time today could be the last time he hears Zuko’s startled huff of laughter, the last time Zuko teases him, the last time Zuko’s eyes linger on him warm and fond. The last time Zuko listens to him rambling with that indulgent little smile on his face, the last time Sokka has someone to ramble *to* who actually listens, the last time they’re in a space together both content to just *be* and he doesn’t know what to do because he can’t stay here but now that it’s finally happening Sokka doesn’t know how to make himself face it. He feels shaky and weak and wavering and fuck, but he doesn’t know if he’s strong enough for this, when being strong suddenly feels like all he has left to offer that Zuko can take.

Now of all times, Zuko needs him to step up and handle it, needs Sokka to not make this any harder than it already is. Except Sokka’s *been* handling it and now his whole head feels full of static, his lungs heavy and thick, his body impossible to move and he doesn’t—he doesn’t think it’s working anymore.

Eventually, Sokka realizes he’s in the midst of a panic attack. It’s a slow, creeping kind of realization, one that’s simultaneously a relief and its own kind of horror. But he knows what to do now that it’s been named, how to handle this at least, calling on breathing patterns from Iroh and mantras from Aang and the kind of cues he learned in his brief stints in therapy before he got too busy with work again and it fell off his agenda and he never went back and isn’t that looking like a big fat mistake from his nest of blankets on his mattress on the floor right about now.

But he does manage to calm himself, finally. Enough to realize that the air is so hot and oppressive because he’s covered himself in blankets, and then that his hands are tingling because he’s lying on them weird, that his joints ache because he’s gripping into the blankets tight enough for his knuckles to pop white against his skin. And from there it’s a matter of slowly uncurling, straightening inch by inch, emerging still trembling and stumbling from the blankets to trip to his feet.

He moves on autopilot from there as he fumbles for clothes, jeans and a sweater for the first time in days instead of workout shorts and PJs and fuck, and things that remind him of Zuko. He texts Katara as he brushes his teeth—'*Still craving ham and cheese croissants?'*—and is scraping his hair into some semblance of order when her text buzzes back—'*Always'*—nearly shooting his hair tie off his wrist instead of tugging it free from his stack of bracelets in his haste to rush to the door.

“Hey,” Zuko says, poking his head out of the kitchen as Sokka struggles with his coat. “Are you going for—Sokka?” His face immediately goes worried, concerned, seeing the signs of a panic attack like Sokka knew he would, and Sokka’s skin suddenly feels tight under the scrutiny, every inch of him stretched too thin at the idea of Zuko soothing him, trying to talk it out, trying to make it better. At the idea of it being the last time for that, too. Unless Sokka has a full-blown breakdown tomorrow, of course, which feels increasingly—

“I have to go,” Sokka blurts, fumbling his keys and dropping them again when he tries to scoop them up, aware he’s betraying himself and just—needing to leave. “I have to—I can’t—”

“Oh. Yeah. Sure,” Zuko says, slowly stepping back as if to give Sokka space and god, the fact that Sokka *needs* it, that Zuko *knows*. “Whatever you have to do,” he adds, searching Sokka’s face. “Let me know if I can help.”

Sokka can only jerk his head in some semblance of a nod, nearly falling as he tugs his shoes on, biting his tongue and swallowing back all the words suddenly crowding up his throat, feeling like the one thing he suddenly wants from Zuko is the only thing he could never ask him for.

&#45;-

***Nineteen hours until the end***

“I can’t believe they still have you on night shifts,” Sokka says, waving Katara back to the table as he finds plates and mugs in the cabinets.

“I’m not *that* pregnant,” she huffs, the statement somewhat undercut by the way she grunts lowering herself back into her chair.

Sokka snorts, but he knows better than to respond to *that* comment. “I can’t believe you’re awake if you’re working nights.”

“Well,” she says, giving him a look, “My brother texted me out of nowhere asking if I wanted a ham and cheese croissant, which he knows I’ve been craving my whole pregnancy and can almost never eat because of the ham and the cheese. Which means you were getting the fancy kind, not the deli kind, and how was I supposed to go back to sleep after that?”

“Sorry,” Sokka says sheepishly as he puts a fancy croissant in question on her plate and passes it over. “I didn’t think you’d have your sound on.”

Katara shrugs, too busy digging in to bother with a reply, apparently, and as Sokka takes a nibble of his own he can’t deny that they’re worth eating with relish.

“How are you, though?” Sokka asks once she comes up for air. “And the baby? Is Aang still stocking up on canned goods?”

“That man,” Katara says, mock-annoyed but with laughter in her eyes, and they fall into familiar chit-chat as she finishes her croissant and takes another. About how she’s feeling, about work and Aang and the new intern who can’t seem to work the fax machine. About the baby and the changes her body is going through, Katara casually oversharing the gruesome details with a wicked light in her eye and Sokka wincing his way through hearing them but managing to find some response, if not necessarily the right one.

Contemplating his sister and cervical plugs in the same thought wasn’t on his agenda for the week, or ever, really, but Sokka isn’t exactly going to be picky about his topics right now. Not when he can feel something that’s been knotted up along his spine and across his shoulders and threaded all through his lungs and into his stomach finally loosening as they cover this familiar territory. Checking in, staying close, seeing for himself that his little sister is okay and happy and thriving, it’s an instinct that’s always hummed in the back of his mind, beating with new urgency since her pregnancy. He researched things to hell and back when he initially heard the news and promptly scared the shit out of himself until Katara and Zuko talked him down, but it still soothes something in him to hear from her own mouth that she’s doing well. Still gives him something to ground against and hold onto when he can finally admit that it feels like his other touchstones are suddenly crumbling around him.

“It’s going to be good to have you around, though,” she admits, absently stretching like her back is sore and Sokka makes a mental note to go down the pregnancy-stretches rabbit hole again. “Aang and I tried to hang some shelves this weekend…”

Sokka raises his eyebrows when she just trails off, earning himself a huff and an eye roll.

“We could use some expertise getting the nursery over the finish line,” she says with a wrinkle of her nose as if to punctuate the fact that she’s asking, and Sokka pictures the last state he saw the room in—piles of flat-packed furniture needing to be assembled, stacks of blinds and curtains to hang, paint still in its cans instead of the walls. And now, apparently, shelves.

“It might help,” he says, happy to be amused at her expense. “But I’m happy to pitch in. I’ll be your one-man nursery crew,” he adds, straightening a little as he gets into the idea. He can build furniture, hang things on the walls, paint, maybe build a radiator cover—and oh, try to rehang the door that always swings open if it’s not latched all the way. Give the hinges a good oiling so they don’t squeak like every other door in this place, maybe figure out a better closet system…. “Just let me know what you need. A mural? You guys mentioned that, right? I can paint a mural.”

“…Yeah,” Katara says, taking another big bite of croissant. “Let’s see how it looks first once everything else is up and then we can decide. We don’t want it to be too busy.”

Sokka nods, pleased with the idea as he mentally adds creating the perfect nursery to his Holy Shit There’s Going To Be a Baby list alongside researching the perfect set of monitors and learning how to handle late-night diaper changes and bottle making. He already knows what he needs for bulk cooking and laundry and cleaning, the kinds of things that will make Katara and Aang’s lives easier when his niece or nephew arrives, but maybe it’s a good thing he’s moving in now, actually. Maybe it’s a plus that he’ll have time to see how they like to do those things first so he can make sure to get them just right, mastering recipes and perfecting his laundry folding technique. Getting it all just perfect so he can slip seamlessly into any space where this little trio that’s about to exist will need him.

“We have the guest room all ready for you at least,” Katara says like she’s reading his mind. “We cleared out the closet and dresser so you have space for all your clothes,” she teases, and Sokka sticks his tongue out at her. “And I know how you like your blankets, so we have extras on the bed.”

“I do have my own,” Sokka says, wanting to be teasing but feeling more touched, his words coming out softer than he intended, without the sharp edges of sarcasm.

“That will be in storage,” she points out—with a bit of question in her voice because Sokka has admittedly been less than communicative about the topic of what he is or isn’t bringing into their apartment beyond ‘no big furniture.’ “How’s moving going?”

Sokka feels himself open his mouth. He can feel his chest expanding as he breathes in, can feel his tongue ready to shape words, his face automatically settling into something self-deprecating, amused. But nothing comes out, that tingly-numbness suddenly threatening the edges of his awareness again as his reply catches on the back of his throat, hooking into his tongue instead of falling easily off his lips, light words that cling and scrape, ragged and painful and *wrong.*

***One month and six days before the end***

*“…So I got a lot of boxes,” Zuko is saying, “And I went for the sturdy stuff since you* *can’t just have all your stuff in garbage bags in storage,” he adds with a small,* *flashing smile.*

*“Sounds like a challenge to me,” Sokka says, the words automatic, his hands shaking* *a little as he straightens out the leaning edge of the pile of flattened cardboard.*

*“Packing tape,” Zuko says, ignoring him except for the twitch of his lips.*

*“A necessity.” Sokka’s mouth feels dry, his throat parched.*

*“Bubble wrap.”*

*“Often overlooked but always key.” He hears the words as if from a distance, his body* *feeling numb, like it belongs to someone else.*

*“New markers,” Zuko rattles off, glancing up when Sokka is silent. “Because our* *other set was running dry last time,” he explains. “Remember? We could barely read* *the boxes.”*

*“Yeah. Good call,” Sokka manages after a beat, suddenly struggling to get the words* *out because that’s the kind of thing Sokka wrote down to keep track of but never acted* *on after their last move. Except apparently, he didn’t need to act on it. Zuko has his* *own meticulous list that he’s currently frowning down at as he makes sure everything* *he needs actually made it into the apartment, an almost-literal page out of Sokka’s* *book that he should theoretically be proud of and instead makes him want to scream,* *to storm out of the room, to—*

*“And packing paper since we don’t get a newspaper anymore,” Zuko says, oblivious* *to the storm brewing in Sokka’s gut.*

*“RIP to Mr. Timothy Sullivan’s never-ending subscription,” Sokka gets out as if* *everything is normal, as if he isn’t on the verge of tearing the paper from Zuko’s hands* *and demanding to know why Zuko needs it, why he made it, why he didn’t ask Sokka to* *help.*

*“It was a gift,” Zuko agrees.*

*“I’ve never been more well-informed,” Sokka says, but he can’t join in when Zuko* *snorts a laugh even though it’s his own joke because he can’t seem to stop staring at* *the packing paper, suddenly feeling the overwhelming urge to light it on fire along* *with everything else Zuko bought.*

*Which is a page out of Zuko’s book, now that he thinks about it. Go figure.*

“Sokka?” Katara straightens, croissant forgotten and face flashing with alarm as all Sokka can seem to do is stare at her, adrenaline and something too close to panic trying to shudder through him again. “What is it, what’s going on?”

“Nothing,” he manages to shove out, “It—nothing.” The words are automatic, familiar and bitter and he suddenly can’t get the rest of it past his lips, things he’s been saying since his mom died and his dad got deployed and Yue got sick, words he’s said so many times, over and over until he’s been able to make them true, again and again except this time—this time—

“You’ve been weird for months,” Katara says, suddenly leaning forward, voice going intent. “*Months*. What’s going on? And don’t try to tell me again it’s just work, you’re *never* like this about work.”

“I just—” Sokka feels like he’s floundering, like he’s drowning, like he’s being doing everything he can to keep his head above water for months and it’s suddenly not enough anymore. “It’s been a lot, you know? Packing and—and everything.”

Her eyes narrow, flicking over his face before she says, openly suspicious, “Moving is always stressful.”

Which is an out, of sorts, and Sokka could take the out like he’s been taking them for months now. But he thinks this time it’s maybe more a test than an out, that this time she won’t let him dodge away. That this time he might not be able to pull it off.

“It’s—it’s different,” he hears himself saying, hands clenched so tight together his fingers ache, and when did that happen? “More.”

Her words are so, so careful. “Because Zuko is moving away?”

Sokka manages a nod, his throat locked tight.

"...And because you’re not going with him?” she adds, so, so gentle, and Sokka gasps at the blow of it, flinching and feeling everything that’s been teetering on the thin edge of his desperate, white-knuckled control suddenly boils over.

“It’s like everything is *wrong*,” he bursts out, raw. “We had this—this *amazing* thing going, it was *fine*, and then suddenly out of nowhere, he’s going to law school? And leaving? If he wanted a change why didn’t he *say* something? I would—there were *options,*” Sokka insists, words he’s barely let himself think spilling out messy and plaintive. “We could have moved, we could have worked something out. If he just—if he needed more time, more space, *whatever*, we could have made it work. But he *didn't* and now—and why does he have to *go*?”

“Sokka,” Katara says quietly, her expression soft with something far too close to pity for Sokka to want to look at it, “You know Zuko’s been talking about law school for years.”

“I *know*,” Sokka says, voice cracking. He’s the one Zuko’s been talking *to.* “But—aren’t there law schools here?”

Katara opens her mouth and then hesitates, hitching like she doesn’t know what to say. But that’s okay, because after months of biting his tongue, Sokka has more than enough to get out for the both of them.

“I saw his list, you know. I helped him *make* it,” Sokka says, letting out a laugh that tastes bitter and ugly. “He wasn’t looking at *anything* within a five-hour drive of here. When we have one of the top ten law schools in the country a fucking bus line away? And two more you can get to just hopping on a train?” He scrubs roughly over his face, pressing the heels of his hands to his eyes and speaking into the darkness, “I just—I don’t know what I *did*. And I feel so stupid*,* I didn’t even know something was wrong until—and I could have *done* something, and now all I can do is fucking help him *pack.*” He spits the word like a curse, like it's the worst word he knows. “And even that—I fucked cried over stuffed animals this morning, Katara. *Stuffed animals*. And the day before it was video games, and I fucking—I unbuilt my bed when he wasn’t even *going* for days, how *stupid* could I—what am I even *doing*.”

“You aren’t st—”

“Like why am I even fucking *here*,” he says over top of her, jerking a hand out at the kitchen. “I should be taking advantage of every last bit I can get with him and instead I’m *here* because I can’t even—I can’t *deal* with it. I can’t—I should be *helping*, but it’s like I have this—this hole in my chest and nothing makes it better. It just keeps getting bigger and bigger and it used to be *okay,"* he says, voice breaking with something that feels needy and desperate, “Like yeah, sure, it was *there*. But we could joke and Zuko was happy, and he—he did what he wanted to do, he was chasing his dreams, so it was fine. It was *fine*.”

“Sok—”

“But now it’s like I can’t stop thinking that this is the last time I’m going to see him smile like that, and the last time he’ll ask me if I’m okay, and the last time he’ll just see on my face that something is wrong—which only makes it *worse*, by the way, like fuck, I can’t even keep it together for one day? But it’s just—it’s like suddenly I can’t even handle the *idea* of hearing him laugh because *what if it’s the last time.*”

“It’s not going to—”

“And I *know* we’re still going to talk all the time,” Sokka says, the flood of words feeling suddenly endless, the hole in his chest feeling suddenly hot and angry and keening instead of aching and so, so cold. “Okay? I *know.* I know I’ll FaceTime him and I know I’ll visit and I know he will too. I know I’ll still see him. But it won’t be the *same*. It’s not going to be like this, like it *was,* and just —*fuck.* It feels like Yue all over again," he chokes out, scrubbing at his stinging eyes, voice going thing. "But Zuko’s still here, he's right in front of me, he’s not—he’s just *going,* he’s not—but it still *feels* the same and I don’t know *why*, why does it—why is it this *hard?*” he gasps out, pleading, “It’s not supposed to be, it’s not, but I can't, Katara, I *can’t*,” he cries, his voice going ragged and begging and god, he's not sure what he's even begging *for* as he finally manages to cut himself off, suddenly painfully aware of all the messy, aching parts of himself he just put on display, but god, he just—he just wants it to *stop*.

“Sokka,” Katara finally says into the silence, her voice soft and pitched to soothe in a way that makes him want to burst out of his chair, “These are normal things to be feeling.”

Sokka lets out a noise that’s maybe trying to be a laugh. “Over my roommate moving out? Yeah. Sure.”

A delicate pause, then, visibly picking her words with care, “You know it’s not like that with you two.”

“Then what’s it like, Katara?” he snaps. “Tell me what it’s *like*, because that’s what it *is*. My best friend is *leaving me,* and he’s not coming back!”

“You don’t know that,” she immediately bites back, hot. “You don’t know he’s not coming back.” Her eyes narrow. “And you *know* it’s more than just that between you.”

“Yeah?” Sokka says, dropping his face against his clenched fists because he can feel the way he wants to poke and snarl, to shove and push and prod until she’s as upset and uncontrolled as he is, until her words are an angry flood to meet his own and fuck, fuck, he hasn’t felt like this in—a long time. “What's it like, then?” he asks again, trying to exhale the wild thing inside himself. “Because I’m pretty sure this isn’t how most people feel when they finally get their own place.” Which he isn’t even doing, he’s moving in with her and Aang. Fuck.

“But,” she says after a beat, cutting right to the hurting heart of him, “I’m pretty sure it’s how most people feel going through a breakup.”

“A bre—a fucking *breakup*? That would at least be *easier*,” Sokka explodes, hearing the words bitter and harsh and too full of things he’s spent too long trying to shove away. “Trust me, a breakup would be a hell of a lot better than *this*, Katara, at least then we would have gotten to *try* instead of this—” Sokka bites his tongue, trying to swallow back the sudden anger trying to spill out of him. Katara’s eyes are wide, her face stricken, and Sokka can hear his own ragged breaths suddenly over loud in the silence.

He's distantly aware that his hands are gripping the edge of the table, that his chest is heaving. That he's glaring at her like she's about to take a swing at him, that every bit of him is coiled and ready like he's cornered, his adrenaline spiking like he’s on the verge of a fight or bolting out of here or —or *something* as his words hang loud and undeniable between them. Echoing off the cabinets, reverberating off the walls, ringing through him like a bell. Vibrating into muscle and sinew and bone like a revelation, like a realization, like something that’s always been there the way his lungs and his eyes and his heart pounding in his chest have always been there. Such a part of him that he never thinks about them at all, until suddenly he does.

*Try, try, try. At least then we would have gotten to try.* Sokka can’t move as it ricochets through him, the idea of trying. The idea that there's something *to* try. *Try, try, try,* beating along to his overly-fast pulse, clinging to the inside of his lungs, screaming for attention in a way that feels just like the painful, aching dread of the past few months. *At least then we would have gotten to try,* looping over and over through his head until it's the only thing he can think or hear until finally, Katara takes a slow breath, sitting back like suddenly the very air has become fragile.

“*Do* you want to try?” she asks, carefully looking down at her clasped hands, and it's just a question, a simple question, yes or no, but Sokka can only stare at her, dumbstruck, with absolutely no idea what to say, *try* all that he can hear. *Try, try, try. At least then we would have gotten to try.* *Do you want to try?*

“Look,” she eventually says into his frozen uncertainty, “I’m not trying to say anything.” She shoots him a half-guilty look before squaring her shoulders, chin coming up. “But I’ve seen the guys you date, and the girls. And he’s not *not* your type.”

“I—we’re friends,” Sokka says, his tongue finally unsticking but feeling clumsy and thick as he fumbles for words, trying to make sense of what she’s saying. “We’ve always been friends. I mean, have I thought about it in passing every once in a while?” he adds when she gives him an openly skeptical look. “Sure, but who hasn’t? Zuko’s gorgeous and smart and funny and just—you know how he is. Who wouldn’t? But that doesn’t *mean* anything.” …Right?

“I mean. I haven’t.”

Sokka blinks.

“Aang hasn’t.”

He slowly closes his mouth.

“Toph doesn’t.”

“Wait. Did you *ask—”*

“And Aang is going to kill me for saying it,” Katara continues, already wincing but plowing forward anyway, jaw set with determination, “But I can tell you everyone except him is basically positive that you two hooked up at least once already, and I’m honestly shocked right now that it sounds like you haven't slept with him and wow, I can’t believe I’m saying that to my brother.”

Sokka’s mind is utterly blank. What—*everyone*? They all—they talked about it? They *talk* about it? “…What?”

“Sokka, come on,” Katara huffs, giving him an exasperated look. “You guys are *always* finding excuses to touch each other. You wear each other’s clothes more than your own. You *cuddle* when you watch movies—”

“My toes get cold! He’s always warm!”

“—you always sit next to each other, you don’t even need to look up most of the time to know he's walked into the room—”

“That’s just being familiar—"

“—you have entire conversations just by looking at each other—”

“So I know how he thinks, that’s not—”

“It’s not just knowing how he thinks! You guys cook like you're dancing!"

"That doesn't—wait, we what?"

"Sokka. You and Zuko have *chemistry*.”

“Because we’re friends!” Sokka protests, feeling like he’s suddenly scrambling for balance over ground he thought was solid, immutable. “Really good ones, yeah. And we’ve known each other a long time, and we like each other, because we’re *friends*, and so what if we don’t hide it? There’s nothing wrong with platonic displays of affection between men!”

“There isn’t,” Katara agrees after a beat, searching his face. “But do you really, honestly think it’s just friends between you two? That all of that is just platonic?”

“I—he’s my friend,” Sokka says helplessly, unsure of what else to say, of how to put into words all that 'friend' means when it comes to Zuko. “My best friend.”

“Who,” Katara says slowly, “You just said you wanted to *try* with.”

That's…true. He did just say that. And he wasn’t exactly picking his words when he did, but that doesn’t mean that he didn’t…that it wasn’t…

“Look, it’s your life,” Katara eventually sighs, slumping a little as she shoves back her hair. “You’re right that I don’t know what goes on in it, and I haven’t wanted to push this—” Sokka blinks. Wait, has *not* pushing it been an ongoing, active effort for her? “—but Sokka, if you’re suddenly thinking about it? If you suddenly want it?” She leans in, her face going fierce and her fingers gentle against his own where he’s still gripping onto the table. “If you finally want to try, why *not*? What’s the harm in giving it a shot?”

“I wouldn’t—I don’t…” He doesn’t what, doesn’t know what to think, when he feels like Katara might as well be telling him the sky is green and the earth is flat? Doesn’t know what to say, when he’s still half stunned by the revelation that not everyone idly thinks here and there about undressing Zuko and what it would be like to stroke down his ribs on the way to grabbing onto his hips—except Sokka knows that one, doesn’t he, his hand suddenly tingling from the skin-memory of doing just that the other day.

No, what he doesn't know what to do with is the idea that not every pair of best friends loves to make each other smile and surprise each other and see each other happy the way he and Zuko do, because isn’t that just how best friends *are*? It’s not like Sokka wants *any* of his friends to be unhappy. It's not like he doesn’t like to plan special days for all of them and help them when they need it and so what if he does all that for Zuko a bit more than everyone else? He lives with Zuko. He has the opportunity to do it a bit more, just like when he dated Suki, he had the opportunity with her…

“I don’t...What if he doesn’t—I don’t want to mess anything up,” Sokka finally says, shaky, something almost scared pressing against his lungs. “Zuko isn’t just—he’s *important*, he’s not—” Not just some guy. Not just some person Sokka can become infatuated with and get over, someone he can just fall in love with and then out again like it wouldn’t change every part of him, every part of *them*. He’s *Zuko*. Sokka’s best friend, his confidant. Who he turns to when he needs help, the first person he wants to tell about his day, the only person he wants to see him when all he needs is space. He’s not just anyone, he's...everything.

“But Sokka, what’s the harm?” Katara asks again when it becomes clear he doesn’t know what else to say, how else to say it. “What’s the worst that could happen?” A hesitation, then, “He’s leaving tomorrow anyway.”

“Yeah, he’s *leaving*,” Sokka says, flinching from the reminder. “What, I just dump all of this on him when I’m not even sure—” the words stick on his tongue like they might not be entirely true. “—and send him off to his shiny new life and what, tell him to let me know? To think on it and get back to me? He’s *going. H*ow could I ask him to—if he wants to go, I can’t try to make him stay,” he says, chest clenching from how much he wants to do just that. *Has* wanted to, fuck, the temptation for months to make it as hard as possible for Zuko to walk away... “He wants to go. He’d be better off not knowing, I’d just— I’d just be making it harder. I can’t ask him to give up his dream like that. Not when it’s this close.”

“What if it was the other way around,” she asks after a long pause, offering her hand and squeezing tight when Sokka grabs on like a lifeline. “If Zuko felt that way about you, would you really prefer to never know? For him to leave and never tell you?”

Would he—If Zuko…Sokka can only stare, holding onto her hand for balance as he reels under the idea that Zuko could possibly feel—be feeling—the same as this.

“Besides,” she adds, her hand tightening around his, her jaw tensed with something like… determination? “It doesn’t have to be about asking him to stay.” Which…doesn’t make sense.

“What, long distance for three years?” he frowns, seizing onto logistics because that’s what always makes sense when nothing else does. “Longer?”

“…It’s not the craziest thing,” she says carefully.

Sokka makes a skeptical noise. “In a brand new relationship?” When there isn’t even a foundation yet? A sense of who they are together*,* of how they fit? Of the thing that makes it *them* and not just two individuals who happen to be in each other’s orbit?

Except…they already have something together, don’t they? Something amazing. That’s what Sokka said at the start of this whole thing. That’s what he’s been so upset over Zuko changing without ever saying anything needed to change. And hasn’t *them* been the problem this whole time? That after so many years, Sokka’s life is so molded around Zuko’s that he doesn’t know what shape to make of it without him?

“There’s the other way, too,” she breaks into his thoughts, voice painfully neutral, “I know you’ve been there since you were an intern, but jobs *are* mobile,” she says, not quite meeting his eyes as she delicately tosses a bomb at another corner of his world.

“…They’re talking about promoting me again in a few months,” he hears himself say, blank. “To manager. I’d have my own team.” He interned with them for three years in college, has been with them ever since graduation. He’s worked there in some form or another for more than a third of his life. The idea of no longer walking through those doors, of not knowing all the faces around him and the best coffee machines in the building and what favors to offer to get something done on a tight timeline...It makes him feel lost, makes him feel cast adrift, uncertainty fluttering uncomfortably in his throat.

“That’s really good,” Katara says, squeezing his hand. “I’m really happy for you. But they’re not the only company where you can do your work. They’re not the only place that will realize how great you are.”

“I…I never thought about leaving.” Except that’s not true, is it, not true at all. All those nights he said he was looking at apartments whenever Zuko asked what he was working on and he found his way over to employers and job boards and industries in Boston instead. All those times he told himself it was part of researching the area, of making sure Zuko lived in the best possible place, when his search results had nothing to do with *Zuko’s* wants—and—and—

“You have enough saved to figure it out,” Katara says, each word precise and deliberate. “If you wanted to.”

If he wanted to. That it could be just about *want*, that wanting could so easily translate to *happening*—

“But—you have the baby coming,” Sokka says, feeling himself scrabbling for handholds like he’s in some kind of slow-motion freefall, the things he thought so sure and unchangeable suddenly thin and insubstantial as mist all around him. “I’m moving in to help. You guys need me.”

She stares at their clasped hands and takes a deep, slow breath. “We have dad close now, now that he’s retired from the navy, and Bato,” she says, the words sounding deliberately level. “And Gran Gran isn’t that far, and Suki isn’t that far out of the city, either. Toph can get here in like twenty minutes when she wants to, and Aang’s been talking up the neighborhood to Teo every chance he gets.” Another breath before she looks up, catching his eyes. “It’s not all on you, Sokka.”

“But—we—you just asked me to hang the shelves, and paint, and…” He trails off, helpless, gripping tight to her hand because god, he can hear how needy he sounds, how scared. How much it sounds like it's about being needed, not them needing him.

“It’s *not* that we don’t want you here, Sokka,” Katara says fiercely at whatever she’s seeing on his face. “Okay? We’re more than happy to have you for as long as you want to stay, and that will always be true.” She waits for him to jerk his head in a tight nod, all he can manage with his throat feeling tight and choked. “But if there’s somewhere else you’d rather be? We don’t *need* you. *I* don’t need you.” Not like she used to need him when they were kids, Sokka hears, suddenly struggling to breathe. Not like he used to need her back. “And I *never* want to be the reason you hold yourself back from something you want to do. From something that could make you *happy*.”

“You make me happy,” Sokka manages to whisper, the table blurring in front of him as his eyes go stinging and hot. “You always—you all—”

“I know,” she says gently, “But Sokka—” She gives his hand a little shake and offers a small, slightly watery smile when he manages to glance up. “You don’t have a car? Trains don’t exist? Isn’t it like a two-hour flight? It’s not like it’s permanent. It’s not like we’d never see you again.”

Sokka swallows through the thickness in his throat, struggling for words as he finds himself so abruptly untethered from all the things that used to anchor him down, scrabbling for something to hold onto when it feels like everything he thought was immovable is suddenly sliding out from beneath him. “…What are you saying?” he finally manages, something tight and hopeful and afraid curling in his chest. “What…what are we talking about.”

“We’re talking,” she says carefully, “About how you’ve spent ten years putting down roots with Zuko. And maybe you don’t want that to end.”

“It’s more than that,” Sokka presses, on the verge of desperation. He needs to know what he’s trying to hold onto, needs to hear it out loud but god, he’s suddenly afraid to say it himself beause what if he’s wrong? What if this wild, scary wanting leads him astray? “You’re saying—it’s more than that.”

Katara gives him a long, searching look, her face slowly settling into something like understanding. “And maybe you did it on purpose,” she finally says, squeezing his hand. “Maybe you wanted your lives to be together even if you didn’t realize it yet. And maybe now you want more than to just go back to the way it used to be.”

“That’s—I don’t…” Sokka doesn’t know what to say. Hearing it out loud like that…he feels like he can barely process it, can barely think through the feeling of teetering on the edge of something huge and impossible, something he’s never let himself imagine before. But now that he has, something that every part of him wants to cling to with an urgency that steals his breath.

“And maybe it’s not that much of a change, even,” Katara suddenly adds, giving him a wry smile when he jerks his head up in confusion. “You guys are practically married anyways, most days.”

They’re…? “…What?”

She snorts. “The pet names? Hotman?”

“Everyone calls hi—”

“Hot stuff? Hot thing? *Hot cakes?”*

Sokka…can admit that not everyone calls him *those*, and he can point out that he picked up the nickname from Aang, that it started as a joke, but he isn’t sure he could say if—when—it stopped being one...

She gives him an amused look. “And the regular grocery trips together? Date nights each week? Board game *and* movie nights? Taking each other out? Sokka, you were planning your ten-year anniversary.”

“Of living together,” Sokka says weakly. Those—those are friend things. Things that two friends do who love to hang out together and want to spend their free time with each other. Two friends who…turn down dates and adjust plans and weigh invitations and share a calendar to make sure they get enough time together…

“Which is a thing that couples do,” Katara is saying, shaking her, fond and exasperated. “Just because you guys *somehow* weren’t having sex doesn’t mean you aren’t in a relationship. It doesn’t mean you don’t have something to build on. Something you’ve already built.”

That…isn’t wrong, but, “Usually, you need to know you’re in a relationship to have one,” Sokka finally says.

Katara gives him a flat look. “Sometimes the people involved are both idiots,” she says, tart, and Sokka can only huff a humorless laugh.

“Yeah, maybe,” he says, slumping back in his chair and tugging out his hair tie to shove a hand through his hair. It’s not like he can exactly deny it, sitting here after all that.

After a moment he nudges another croissant Katara’s way, automatically glancing down at his phone as he sets the bag back on the edge of the table and checking on reflex when he sees the flashing light of a notification—there’s a reason he’s done so well at work, and it isn’t because of his skill at disengaging—and blinking to see a text from Zuko instead of an SOS from his boss.

He hesitates, hand hovering over the notification as he feels an unexpected stab of trepidation— though is it really so unexpected, by this point? He bailed on Zuko basically without a word this morning, with so much left to do…But he makes himself flick the message open anyway, bracing himself for a rightfully frustrated message, or understandably probing questions. Except it’s not Zuko upset at being walked out on during the last day they have to finish packing, it’s not Zuko calling him out for leaving him all on his own, not Zuko sending a reminder of the list of tasks that Sokka said he’d do today.

It’s just a simple, unassuming statement, ‘*The lunch rush at the JD usually clears by 1. It’s pretty* *dead until 4ish.’ E*xactly the kind of quiet, restful, in-public-but-alone environment Sokka would want if he were still panicking. Exactly the kind of thing that makes Sokka feel warm and cared for and seen that Zuko reached out to offer. Exactly the kind of thing that Zuko is *always* offering, and oh, oh, oh, how could Sokka have missed that this was here—has *been* here—the whole time?

“What if,” Sokka starts, shaky, something that feels like fear fluttering in his chest, “What if he doesn’t feel the same? What if—” he clears his throat, not quite able to meet Katara’s eyes, staring down at his phone screen instead. “What if this makes it weird? What if things change?”

“Sokka, what if it *doesn’t* change?” Katara says, urgent, and Sokka forces himself to actually sit with that instead of just reacting. With the idea of it being the same. Not as it was a year or even an hour ago, but the same as it is now, now when Sokka *knows*. “And what if he feels the same?” she adds before he can get too stuck in that spiral, those words almost more terrifying to contemplate. “Do you really want to miss out?”

Oh god he doesn’t, he doesn’t. That this could exist in more than just the fleeting confines of his mind…

“And besides, do you *really* think he’d stop being friends with you over it?” Her sudden wry tone grabs his attention before it can get tangled between the idea of Zuko leaving, still leaving, and phone calls and texts and occasional visits and trying to keep their friendship alive from a distance but now with Sokka wanting more, and the idea of what *more* could even mean. “Zuko was in Mai and Ty Lee’s wedding party, you never stopped being best friends with Suki, you *both* still hang out with Jet,” she says, giving him a knowing look and a light kick under the table. “Neither of you throw away friendships because of sex, Sokka. Even the sex you don’t have.”

Sokka blinks and slowly closes his mouth. He…hadn’t thought of it that way. That he could say something and not have to fear Zuko’s withdrawal. That the worst-case is that things just… continue as they are, and isn’t that the thing Sokka’s been wanting so desperately to hold onto this whole time?

It feels monumental, overwhelming. Too huge and impossible for him to know where to begin wrapping his arms around it. But big with possibility, he realizes, not the looming dread he’s somehow become so used to these past months. Big with a potential that feels bright and fragile and sweet like even without being able to wrap his mind around it, he still knows it's something to touch carefully, to gently hold. To sink his fingers into and never let go.

&#45;-

***Fifteen hours until the end***

Sokka is in a daze by the time he finally gets home. He missed his bus leaving Katara’s and decided to walk instead of waiting under the haphazard logic that physical activity would help him get his head on straight, that somehow moving his body would help him pull together the Plan to Woo Zuko—or at least, the Plan to Confess His Feelings Without Making Things Weird and Awkward and Terrible.

Except he forgot that the walk to Aang and Katara’s new place is about twice as far as their old one. And then he wasn’t paying attention and had to backtrack around the highway interchange that the city *still* hasn’t built a walking path through, and now evening is falling and his feet are sore and his mind is full to bursting instead of crystal clear.

It’s like every step he took unleashed another montage of memories—trying to find a birthday card for Azula in that store, eating until they could barely walk at that restaurant’s opening, hunting the city for some tea Zuko wanted to give Iroh and going to more shops on this street than Sokka generally cares to think about. And threaded through it all are memories of Zuko’s smile and exasperated glances and huffs of laughter. Of the way his hand drifts to Sokka’s lower back in that absent way of his when he’s ushering Sokka through a door, which Sokk’s never thought about before except that he likes it but now it’s like his eyes are suddenly open to the fact that he *likes* it. The same way he likes the way they playfully wrestle and shove at each other, and the way Zuko feels under his hands during a shoulder massage. The sight of Zuko in clothes he stole from Sokka’s wardrobe and fuck, the sight of himself in Zuko’s. Zuko cooking food that Sokka likes and greeting him when he comes home from work and being a hypercompetitive weirdo about the strangest things, like tea making and plant care, and Sokka always delighting in egging him on and god, how did Sokka live so long loving Zuko and never realize it?

Sokka jolts out of his daze the second he walks through the door and is hit with the unmistakable smell of his favorite takeout—ramen from the place that doesn’t have a website or accept cards and only recently got a phone and Sokka usually has to call over and over and over before he finally gets through to place an order. And the idea that Zuko, who’s been talking about eating down the kitchen for weeks now, who has so much to do before tomorrow, spent the time to call again and again and walk over, all because he wanted to make Sokka’s day better…

Sokka toes off his shoes in a rush, hurrying so fast to the kitchen that he has to grab onto the wall to skid to a stop as he whips around the corner. His eyes skip over the unmarked paper bag on the counter to latch onto the sight of Zuko kneeling on the floor, cradling a can in his lap—Manwich, the corner of Sokka’s brain still capable of coherent thought notes, Zuko and Sokka used to put that into Bato’s mailbox as a prank back in college when Zuko came home for breaks and they were feeling silly and giddy, giggling and shushing and hanging off each other in the darkness and fuck, no *wonder* everyone thinks they’ve hooked up.

Zuko jerks his head up at Sokka’s entrance, his expression briefly stricken and wide open for one aching second before it shutters, a flash of so much that Sokka feels like his heart might bruise the inside of his chest under the force of it, and then like it might break right through his ribcage entirely at the realization that Sokka has seen Zuko do this before. Just the tail end of it, the barely-there edge of a flicker into deliberate smoothness, and god, how many times...

“Hi,” Sokka says after who-knows how long of staring at the faint remnants of all that emotion on Zuko’s face, clearing his throat as the word comes out hoarse almost beyond recognition.

“Hey,” Zuko says after a beat, small, his fingers tightening almost imperceptibly around the can. Then, a little stronger, “I got you takeout.”

“Thanks,” Sokka says, nearly a whisper, aware that he's staring at Zuko but unable to stop tracing his features like he’s never seen them before. He lingers over the familiar shape of his cheekbones and the landscape of his scar, loses himself in the loose strands of hair framing his face and the tattoo disappearing into his collar, feels his throat tightening as he recognizes one of his own flannels over Zuko’s t-shirt. Zuko is staring back, something raw and uncertain growing on his face, his eyes wide and lips parted and apparently just as speechless as Sokka as Sokka’s thoughts tumble in a messy spiral over every detail he drinks in and the reality of Zuko in front of him, circling again and again around how much he loves Zuko, how much he’s always loved him.

“I—” Sokka finally manages to start at the same time Zuko says, “You—”

They both hitch to a stop, staring another moment before letting out matching laughs, too thin and tight and uncertain.

“You go, you go—”

“No, you—”

They both stumble to a stop again, looking at each other, expectant.

“I was going to—”

“I was just—”

Another long silence, then Sokka opens his mouth to start at the same time as Zuko and immediately biting his tongue just as Zuko snaps his lips shut. But this time Sokka isn’t going to be the one to break first. He’s going to be patient and listen and support Zuko like Zuko tried to support him today, and only find a way to say his piece after. Except shit, Zuko is looking at him with a resolve that says he isn’t going to say anything until Sokka does, and Zuko's always been more patient than Sokka outside of those moments when—

“Hey, what if—”

“I don’t want you to go,” Sokka blurts.

“—you came with me."

Sokka barely manages to bite his tongue as he registers Zuko’s words, nearly choking on a rush of carefully planned explanation, coughing a bit as he swallows it back and trying to make sure that he—did he really—could he have actually—

Sokka is distantly aware that he's gaping at Zuko in a stunned, uncertain kind of bewilderment as he replays the words over and over and over again, unable to entirely believe that he actually heard what he thinks he did. He *wants* to believe it, but how could he...Except Zuko’s unscarred eye is peeled as wide as his own, and Sokka might feel too afraid to even hope right now but there's something fragile and a little bit scared slowly growing over Zuko’s face, something that mirrors the fluttering *what if* churning in Sokka’s gut. Fuck, what if Sokka misheard, what if he misunderstood? What if Zuko doesn’t mean it the way Sokka heard it?

But god, what if he *does?*

Sokka sucks in a breath, nearly dizzy as he squeezes onto the doorframe and asks, unsteady, the only words he can seem to find, “…To be your roommate?”

Zuko just stares, totally still, not even breathing.

“Because that would be a lot, right?” Sokka finally manages to continue, his pulse pounding hard enough to choke his words. “To change your whole life and career and everything. That—that’s a lot. For a roommate.”

Zuko’s hands are white-knuckled around the can. “…Yeah,” he says, voice so rasping Sokka winces in sympathy. “It would be. A lot. To ask a—a roommate.”

Another extended silence, both of them staring again, uncertain, and holy fucking *shit,* Sokka can’t take the idea of getting this out in fits and bursts, in half-voiced questions and stumbling answers. He can't stand the thought of trying to seize onto every twitch of Zuko’s expression for some scrap of a clue all the way through this. “I’vebeenlookingatjobopeningsinBoston."

Zuko hesitates, mouth half open, brow furrowing as he tries to find the pauses that were supposed to be between Sokka's words.

“I’ve been looking," Sokka repeats slower, feeling like his heart is about to burst with every syllable he forces himself to annunciate, "At job openings. In Boston. For months."

“*Fuck*, Sokka.” The can clatters to the floor as Zuko drops his face into his hands. “Fuck, you—for months? *Fuck*.”

“Because I—I don’t want to stop being with you,” Sokka says, the words stumbling off his tongue, all out of order from what he planned but coming out anyway. “I don’t want you to go.
